# C3PO action plan (from BACKLOG.md, 2026-11-03)

Scoped by 9 read-only agents that read the code, one per work package (WP). A, B, C and E (the safety-touching ones) each got an adversarial critic, and a cross-package critic checked shared files and ordering. Every subtask names real files.

**How to read it.** Work runs in **waves**. Inside a wave, the listed branches edit disjoint files and can run in parallel. Each subtask lands only with its tests green. A critic issue marked _apply_ is folded into the subtask when it's implemented.

**Status (2026-11-04):** waves 0, 1 and 2 done. Wave 2 also closed backlog 1.5 (X15) and the cross-check's person-rule item (X16), and gated zero_torque. 🟢 done · 🔵 in progress · ⚪ todo. Robot-only steps are in §4.

## 1. Waves

### Wave 0

- 🟢 F2 .gitignore graphify-out
- 🟢 F1 make CI green (prettier fails on docs/autonomy/audit/evidence/back/{conv,rej,sink}.ts, confirmed; ruff reports 2 errors in apps/perception/bringup/build.py, confirmed; arm_sdk.py passes ruff check and ruff format, so re-check why F1 lists it)
- 🟢 F8 test_mic_listener wall clock
- 🟢 F4 mcp>=1.30,<2 (uv.lock has 1.27.1), uv sync --locked in bridge_sync:41
- 🟢 F5 bun audit fix
- 🟢 F6 robot_internet.py 80/443 only
- 🟢 F3 c3po.sudoers
- 🟢 G5 pin today's go_to_object tool body
- ⚪ 8.3 push feat/autonomy once the user says so

_Why:_ Unblocks everything after it. A green CI and a pinned test baseline make every later change checkable. F4 must land before A2/A4 because it changes the FastMCP base class that GovernedMCP.call_tool overrides. F8 must land before B1, and G5 before E2-E4, because each pins behavior the next one changes. Every item is S, all of WP F fits on one branch (scripts/tests/run.sh is shared inside F), and none touches A, B or C files except test_safety.py, which F4 adapts first.

### Wave 1

- ⚪ Branch bridge-gate: A1 then A2 then A4 then A6 (estop.py, safety.py, GovernedMCP.call_tool, /safety/confirm, state.py battery)
- ⚪ Branch voice-stop: B1 then B2 (listen.py and mcp_server voice route, then loop.ts/conversation.ts/routes/voice.ts)
- ⚪ Branch back-hardening: B3 (with G6's test), B5 (bind 127.0.0.1), B6 (disableSignUp plus social providers plus a bootstrap emailVerified check plus create-user, which also handles 8.5)
- ⚪ Branch web-safety: C1 no-JS PARAR, C2 battery banner (thresholds aligned to A6)
- ⚪ Branch perception-geometry: D4 then D2 then D3 (detector.py and grounding.py serial), then D1 (after F1's build.py)
- ⚪ C3 XR texture upload (camera-layer.ts only)

_Why:_ Safety first: the PARAR latch (11.2), arg-bound grants (11.1), confirmation for high-danger T1 (1.3), the battery refusal (1.10), the spoken stop on the legacy engine (1.1/1.2), the turn leak (11.8), loopback plus closed sign-up (1.4), the pre-hydration PARAR (1.9), and the camera pitch everything perception depends on (3.1/3.2). The six branches touch disjoint files. B1 shares mcp_server.py with the bridge-gate branch but in a different region (:4013-4035), so merge B1 after bridge-gate.

### Wave 2

- ⚪ Branch confirm-ui (after wave-1 bridge-gate): A3 then A5 then B7 (skills.ts, vr-voice.svelte.ts and tool-card shared, so one branch)
- ⚪ Branch runtime: B4 then G3 then G7 (runtime.ts serial, on top of B3)
- ⚪ Branch teleop: C6 then C7 (teleop server, walk_to/turn yield, estop lease on top of A1), then 1.5 Origin allowlist and token
- ⚪ Branch perception-age: D5 (owns the world_model_publisher age field) plus E1 tests only, plus the person rule using capture age in safety.py (after A6)
- ⚪ Branch eval: G1 then G2
- ⚪ G4 Gemini fallback (mcp_server look/find/verify; merge after bridge-gate)
- ⚪ H1 ROBOT-API.md

_Why:_ A3/A5 need A2/A4's bridge contract. B7 shares skills.ts and vr-voice with them, so all three go on one branch instead of three. The runtime.ts chain can only be serial. C7 needs A1's estop.py, and E3 later needs C7's walk_to.py. D5 must define the age field before E2 consumes it. Each branch is disjoint from the others in this wave.

### Wave 3

- 🟢 Branch go-to-object: E2 (pose at capture, reject older than 0.5 s) then E3 (closed loop, monotonic deadline) then E4 (Nav2 goal only when the gate is already armed; depends on A4)
- ⚪ Branch nav-depth: E5 (after D5 in detector.py) and E6 in parallel, then E7, then E8 (after D1 on spec.py and scripts/robot/c3po), plus the gate failing closed on a stale depth cloud
- 🟡 Branch headset-voice: 🟢 C4, C5, C8; ⚪ B8 (needs the robot) (only if the lease decision is b), then B8 (after B2 and B7)
- 🟢 F7 robot_session.sh (after F6)

_Why:_ Capability work that rests on the wave-1/2 gate. E4 must not exist before a person is the only one who can arm Nav2 (A4). E3 builds on C7's yield. The vr-voice chain starts only after confirm-ui has finished editing that file. E5 waits for D5 to finish with detector.py.

### Wave 4

- ⚪ H2 then H3 then (H4, H5 including A7's safety.md and preflight copy, H6, H7)
- ⚪ H8 closes the BACKLOG once (absorbs A7's BACKLOG edit)
- ⚪ F10 then F9 (CI on push, voice job), once 8.3 has pushed the branch

_Why:_ The docs describe what merged, so they come after the code. OPERATIONS.md, README.md, the test-day docs and BACKLOG.md are each touched by several WPs, and writing each once avoids churn. CI changes only matter once the branch is on GitHub.

### Wave 5

- ⚪ I1 / I2 (kinematics plus manipulation, after D5)
- ⚪ I4 sim harness (pyproject/uv.lock rebased on F4), then I17, then I18 (after 5.5 on the robot)
- Superseded: I5 then I6 (toolApproval). one-press Confirmar (76995ad, 2026-11-05) fixed the same problem; see I5.
- ⚪ I7 then I9 or I8
- ⚪ I12 (after E5), I14 (after D2/E5), I15 (after E8)
- ⚪ I11 (drop I10 or fold it into E6, see inconsistencies)
- ⚪ I3 only if I1/I2 fall short; I13/I16 on the robot

_Why:_ Design-grade and M/L items. Each one sits at the end of a file chain already ordered above, and none is needed for safety. I5/I6 were to replace the A3 Confirmar UI; one-press Confirmar (76995ad, 2026-11-05) replaced the A3 flow instead.

## 2. Decisions

These are the scoping agents' recommendations. **They are the defaults unless you say otherwise.** The ones marked ✋ need you.

- **[A] 11.1: bind the grant to the chat id too, or only to the tool arguments?**
  - Options: Arguments only now (tool + sha256 of canonical args); Arguments + chat id: back passes MCP _meta {c3po/chat_id} on every agent callTool (runtime.ts, client.ts), web sends chatId with Confirmar (chat page, tool card, vr-voice), back forwards it, bridge reads request_context.meta in call_tool
  - **Recommendation:** Arguments only. The backlog's stated hole is 'another caller ... with other arguments', and the args hash closes it. The chat id would be asserted by the caller itself (any MCP client can send any _meta), so it only separates honest concurrent chats and adds no protection against a hostile client. It also touches about 7 files across 3 apps, and the headset voice chat is chat-less. Revisit if two operators ever drive at once.
- **[A] 1.3: how does the bridge tell 'the agent' from the console bring-up buttons?**
  - Options: Bridge gates high-danger T1 for every MCP caller; apps/back /invoke (admin only) pre-grants with the secret, so the admin's click is the confirmation; Back-only gate in runtime.ts buildTools for the agent (a second grant store in TS; Claude Code and direct MCP stay ungated); AI SDK needsApproval on those tools (no approval UI exists in apps/web; the approval lives in client-sent message history, which routes/agent.ts accepts as t.Any())
  - **Recommendation:** Option 1 (A4+A5). It keeps the bridge as the single gate. It fails closed for any client without the secret, including Claude Code and both voice engines. It reuses /safety/confirm and the existing Confirmar card. The bring-up keeps working because back already holds SAFETY_CONFIRM_SECRET. It is also the only option where a model cannot grant itself anything.
- **[A] 1.10: which tools does a low battery refuse, and can the threshold be changed?**
  - Options: T2 + locomotion with expected_duration_s >= 15 or danger high (walk_to, go_to_object, navigate_to, navigate_to_landmark, look_around, arm_navigation); fixed 20%, no override; Same set without look_around (it only turns in place); Add an env SAFETY_MIN_BATTERY_PCT override
  - **Recommendation:** Option 1 with a fixed 20%, the vendor threshold state.py already uses. An unknown battery is allowed so sim and stub keep working. No override: lowering it is a way to weaken the gate, and raising it is a one-line change if a session shows 20% is too late.
- **[A] Should free-text, audit-only arguments (arm_navigation.reason) be part of the grant digest?**
  - Options: Strict: every argument is hashed; Exclude declared audit-only fields from the digest
  - **Recommendation:** Strict. The cost is an occasional second Confirmar if the model rewords the reason on its retry. A per-tool exclusion list is extra code in a safety gate, and someone could later abuse it to hide a meaningful argument.
- ✋ **[B] Who may use /agent and /voice once the role check lands?**
  - Options: Admin only (the existing `user.role !== 'admin'` check); 'user' accounts become view-only; A new 'operator' role via better-auth access control; No role check; rely on closed sign-up alone
  - **Recommendation:** Admin only. Once sign-up is closed, an admin creates every account anyway, and PARAR and voice stop stay open to everyone.
- **[B] How is the first operator created once sign-up is disabled?**
  - Options: CLI apps/back/scripts/create-user.ts calling auth.api.createUser (server-side, no session needed); Keep sign-up open only for ADMIN_EMAILS addresses through a databaseHooks.user.create.before guard; A 'Crear usuario' form in /settings (authClient.admin.createUser)
  - **Recommendation:** The CLI: one small file, uses better-auth's own API, gives an admin with no restart. A /settings form can come later.
- **[B] What should back's listen address be?**
  - Options: Hardcode 127.0.0.1; HOST env, default 127.0.0.1, so 0.0.0.0 stays possible for a LAN box; Leave it on all interfaces
  - **Recommendation:** HOST env defaulting to loopback. OPERATIONS.md documents a LAN-box deployment, and nothing today needs LAN access.
- **[B] When does the robot speak the headset reply?**
  - Options: After the turn ends, every voice-mode turn (simplest); Sentence by sentence while streaming, like the legacy VoiceConversation; Only when the operator turns on a 'hablar por el robot' toggle
  - **Recommendation:** After the turn, always in voice mode. Stream sentence by sentence later if latency bothers people at the stand.
- **[B] Piper fork for the robot's Spanish voice**
  - Options: Keep the pinned rhasspy/piper 2023.11.14-2 aarch64 binary (MIT, heard on the robot 2026-08-21); Move to OHF-Voice/piper1-gpl v1.8.0 (GPL-3.0 Python package; adds onnxruntime to the bridge venv)
  - **Recommendation:** Keep the binary. 6.1 needs no TTS change; switch only if the archived binary breaks.
- **[B] Should the bridge act on the stop phrase locally while the legacy loop is connected? The MicListener docstring says 'reported, not acted on'.**
  - Options: Yes, while a legacy subscriber is connected, mirroring realtime; No, keep relying on apps/back's stop event
  - **Recommendation:** Yes. A mis-decode only halts the robot, and it removes apps/back and the model from the stop path.
- **[B] History window for the agent's model context**
  - Options: 40 UI messages, tool parts kept for the last 10 model messages; A token budget; Prune tool parts only, no window
  - **Recommendation:** 40 / 10 for now, marked as a shortcut. Measure before building a token budget.
- **[B] The /signup page once sign-up is closed**
  - Options: Keep it, showing 'El registro está cerrado. Pedile una cuenta a un admin.'; Remove the page and its links from /login and the landing page
  - **Recommendation:** Keep it with the message now. Removing the links is a follow-up.
- **[B] Mark headset conversations in the console Chat list (6.8)?**
  - Options: No: they already appear, titled with the first transcript; Prefix the title (e.g. 'Casco: …') or show a channel badge
  - **Recommendation:** No code. Confirm on the headset that they appear.
- **[C] 1.9: after a no-JS PARAR, should the console land on a /stop result page or go back to the page you were on?**
  - Options: /stop result page showing the classified outcome (Parando… / La orden de parada no salió); 303 redirect back to the referring page (the result is lost)
  - **Recommendation:** The result page. A pre-hydration press is exactly when the operator needs to know whether the order left, and the copy already exists (estopMessage).
- **[C] 1.10: the topbar chip's colour bands are <=40 amber and <=20 red, used on 4 surfaces and tested. The backlog asks for warn at 25 and strong under 15. Re-align the bands, or add a banner with its own thresholds?**
  - Options: Keep the chip bands; add a banner at <25 warn and <15 alert (C2); Change batteryLevel to 25/15 everywhere (dashboard, live-map, health, chip); Banner, plus a battery line in the headset menu alert as well
  - **Recommendation:** Keep the chip bands and add the C2 banner, not dismissable. Do the headset battery line as its own small follow-up once the bridge-side refusal under 20 % exists.
- **[C] 11.3: the teleop-channel stop raises the bridge-wide sentinel, but in the MCP process it only zeroes velocity and blocks new motion. Running gestures and arm skills are not cancelled until back's stop_everything lands. Should the bridge watch the sentinel and run a full stop_everything when another process raises it?**
  - Options: Only C6 now (sentinel + teleop session latch); back remains the full stop; Also add an external-stop watcher to the MCP process (bridge work package, overlaps 11.2); Also open a one-shot teleop socket to send the stop when no stream is connected
  - **Recommendation:** C6 now, and the watcher as a bridge work-package item next to 11.2, so any process's stop becomes a full stop. Skip the one-shot socket: without the stream, the headset's own motion already goes through back.
- **[C] 11.6: when an agent walk (from headset voice) is running and the operator moves, who wins?**
  - Options: a) Any headset input preempts, head yaw included (C7 only; looking around ends the walk); b) Explicit input preempts (stick, walk buttons, arms); head yaw does not steer while the headset's own voice turn runs (C7 + C8); c) The agent wins until its walk ends; only PARAR interrupts
  - **Recommendation:** b. It keeps 'the person in the headset wins' (estop.py), but only for deliberate input. Without it every voice walk dies the moment the operator looks around. c contradicts the existing lease policy.
- **[C] 11.6: does an engaged arm mirror (arm_sdk weight up) count as driving for the lease, even with zero velocity?**
  - Options: Yes: lease while commanding velocity OR arms engaged; No: lease only while commanding velocity
  - **Recommendation:** Yes. An agent walk under arm_sdk weight 1.0 in mode 801 is ignored or turns into a shuffle (BACKLOG §12), and walking with the headset arms engaged has never been tried.
- **[D] Which frame is the vision container's base_link, and so where are range_m and bearing_deg measured from?**
  - Options: pelvis at waist (0,0,0), the URDF root: x=0.0537 y=0.0175 z=0.4739 pitch 47.6; torso_link, the frame the bridge's arm IK uses: x=0.0576 y=0.0175 z=0.4299 pitch 47.6
  - **Recommendation:** pelvis. It is what locomotion moves and what the nav container's base_link stands for. The two differ by 4 mm in x, so range does not change in practice. The waist caveat (the camera rides the torso) is written as a shortcut comment either way.
- **[D] Accept the URDF CAD pitch (47.6 deg, confirmed by pinocchio) as the default before anyone tape-measures it (3.1 is marked robot-needed)?**
  - Options: Yes: CAD default now, the tape check on the robot, a measured value goes in ~/.c3po/perception.env; No: keep pitch 0 until measured
  - **Recommendation:** Yes. Pitch 0 is known wrong by 47.6 deg: the person rule is blind at 0.5-0.8 m and the glass fallback is inverted. CAD is the best number we have, and the override path stays.
- **[D] Which keys may ~/.c3po/perception.env set?**
  - Options: Allowlist: VISION_ENV_PASSTHROUGH + C3PO_LIDAR_SOURCE; anything else is ignored with a warning; Any key, as if exported in the shell
  - **Recommendation:** Allowlist. A persisted C3PO_TAKE_OVER=1, C3PO_VISION_FAKE=1 or C3PO_NAV_SLAM would silently change every boot. Same reasoning as spec.py's 'an explicit list, not a prefix match'.
- **[D] What happens to the existing robot-only ~/.c3po/yoloe.env?**
  - Options: Merge it into ~/.c3po/perception.env once by hand and read only perception.env; Also read yoloe.env as a fallback
  - **Recommendation:** Merge once by hand (one robot step). Two files is a second place for a stale model path to hide.
- **[D] Should the bridge person rule (safety.person_proximity) also treat a person detection older than PERCEPTION_STALE_S (1.0 s, by its new age_s) as not fresh?**
  - Options: Yes, in D5: it can only add 'perception_stale' aborts; No, only manipulation.detected_target uses age_s for now; person rule later
  - **Recommendation:** Yes, but as a separate small commit after D5 is verified on the robot. It strengthens a safety check, and nuisance aborts are possible if the detector runs slower than 1 Hz. Backlog 11.5 (go_to_object rejects detections older than about 0.5 s) also becomes a one-liner once age_s exists.
- **[E] 11.5 says 'reject detections seen while turning'. Should we do that, or correct the error at its root?**
  - Options: Correct it: apply the bearing to the odometry pose at capture time, from a ~2 s pose history in the sampler (E2), and drop the turning rule; Reject it: refuse sightings while |yaw rate| > ~0.2 rad/s, using SportModeState_.yaw_speed (not yet confirmed to be filled on the G1)
  - **Recommendation:** Correct it. The pose at capture fixes the turn and also the 0.15 m forward step made during 0.5 s of walking. Rejection would starve E3's re-aiming during the first turn toward the target. The governor caps yaw at 0.3 rad/s, so motion blur is negligible.
- **[E] Live detections with no age_s (the nav image on the robot does not have E1 yet): how should go_to_object treat them?**
  - Options: Reject them as stale (go_to_object answers stale_detection until the nav image is rebuilt); Accept them as fresh, with a logged warning, until the rebuild
  - **Recommendation:** Reject them, and rebuild the nav image before the next session. The rebuild is needed anyway for E5–E8 and 4.5. If the MVP stand demo comes before a rebuild is possible, accept them temporarily and remove that branch right after.
- **[E] Nav2 is active but the cmd_vel gate is closed. What should go_to_object do?**
  - Options: Fall back to the camera-only walk, and say so (via 'walk_to', reason gate_closed); Refuse, and ask for arm_navigation first
  - **Recommendation:** Fall back. That is today's verified behaviour, and the gate stays a human decision that go_to_object never makes.
- **[E] Stale-perception policy while navigating on depth (the camera is the only obstacle sensor, and Humble's collision monitor ignores a stale cloud)**
  - Options: SAFETY_STALE_POLICY=stop for nav2-depth sessions, enforced by a preflight block (no bridge code); Make 'stop' the global default; Keep 'slow' (0.4×) everywhere
  - **Recommendation:** Use 'stop' for nav2-depth via the preflight block. Keep the global default at 'slow' for now, since gantry bring-up before the vision container still relies on it (safety.py:107-110).
- **[E] arm_navigation (opens the Nav2 cmd_vel gate) is T1 'high' and is offered to the model in chat mode (back/src/agent/runtime.ts:197-205). Once E4 lets go_to_object use Nav2, the model could open the gate and drive with Nav2 itself. Should that stay possible?**
  - Options: Keep it model-callable in chat mode; Make it operator-only (a console button or a T2-style Confirmar), never callable by the model
  - **Recommendation:** Make it operator-only before E4 walks the robot. This is outside WP E's backlog items (it belongs with the safety work), but E4 makes that path real.
- **[F] mcp 1.30 reaps an idle stateful session after 30 min (404 afterwards). apps/back's client treats that as session lost and fails that call once, and the call can be console PARAR. Which do you want?**
  - Options: (a) GovernedMCP(..., session_idle_timeout=None): today's behaviour, one line; (b) Keep the 30 min default and make apps/back/src/bridge/client.ts reconnect and retry once on HTTP 404 'session not found' (the server never ran the request, so a retry is safe for every tool); (c) Keep the default and accept one failed call after a long idle
  - **Recommendation:** (a) now, inside F4. The bridge is loopback-only and max_sessions=10000 still bounds the CVE's memory growth. Do (b) later as a separate apps/back change: from reading the code (not run), the first call after a `c3po restart` already fails the same way today.
- ✋ **[F] CI triggers: which pushes should run CI?**
  - Options: Every push on every branch + pull_request, with concurrency cancel; push to main and integrate/** and feat/** + pull_request; Keep push: main + pull_request (feat/autonomy runs only once a PR is open)
  - **Recommendation:** Every push plus pull_request with concurrency cancel. The jobs are cheap, and that is what 9.3 asks for.
- **[F] Which voice tests must CI require?**
  - Options: Only the 9 stop-path tests (vosk + piper); the 4 whisper tests stay robot-only; All 13, which adds --extra stt (ctranslate2, PyAV) and a ~145 MB faster-whisper 'base' model download per cache miss
  - **Recommendation:** The 9 stop-path tests. They guard the safety path, while whisper is transcription quality and gets benchmarked under 6.7.
- **[F] robot_session.sh defaults: which ssh host, and what C3PO_ROBOT_HOST should the console get?**
  - Options: host = $C3PO_SSH_HOST or `c3po` (as robot_tunnel.sh), C3PO_ROBOT_HOST=localhost (teleop over the tunnel + Quest via adb reverse, as quest_setup.sh:275 suggests); host = unitree@192.168.123.164 (the cable, as used on 2026-11-03), C3PO_ROBOT_HOST=192.168.123.164 (headset dials the robot directly on the LAN); Add a `c3po-cable` alias to your ~/.ssh/config yourself and default to it
  - **Recommendation:** The first option. It matches robot_tunnel.sh and the loopback-only teleop path, and both values can be overridden through env. Adding an ssh alias is your call; I won't edit ~/.ssh/config.
- **[F] If the never-run test_listen.py:102 cases (spoken 'pará', 'alto', 'stop' must stop) fail once CI runs them, what then?**
  - Options: Re-point the test at the configured phrases (emergencia / para para / detener) and add an explicit test that bare 'pará' does not stop, which is the documented behaviour; Change DEFAULT_STOP_PHRASES so 'pará' stops; Leave them failing until there are real recordings
  - **Recommendation:** The first option. The phrase list is a safety decision that needs recordings of real people (listen.py:85-88), so CI should test what is actually configured, not change it.
- **[F] The sudoers rule matches exact argv. docs/MONDAY-RUNBOOK.md uses `sudo systemctl stop c3po-bridge` (no .service). Widen the rule or fix the doc?**
  - Options: Fix the doc to `.service` / `c3po down` / `c3po up core`; the rule stays exactly 3 lines; Also allow the bare `c3po-bridge` forms (6 lines)
  - **Recommendation:** Fix the doc. Fewer granted command lines, and all three scripts already use the `.service` form.
- **[G] D1. Where and when does the 'nightly' T0 eval run? The TIC AI gateway answers only on campus, so GitHub Actions and cloud routines cannot reach it, and a laptop is rarely on campus at night.**
  - Options: On demand: `bun run eval:t0` against the 8011 stub, as a step in test-day/return-checklist.md before each robot session; A crontab or launchd job on a machine that stays on the campus network; Point the eval at another OpenAI-compatible model that is reachable off campus, at the cost of no longer measuring tic-chat; Skip scheduling for now
  - **Recommendation:** On demand before each robot session (G2), plus a crontab example in the README for when a campus machine exists. No CI or launchd file now.
- **[G] D2. While Nav2 is down (the lidar is faulted), may the agent fall back from a refused navigate_to_landmark('cocina') to recall_landmark + walk_to? That walk is a straight line across the room with no obstacle avoidance.**
  - Options: No fallback: report the refusal and its how_to_fix (strict prompt; the eval scores navigate_to_landmark); Fall back to walk_to only within go_to_object's 4 m limit, after telling the operator there is no obstacle avoidance; Always fall back
  - **Recommendation:** No fallback. A model-chosen straight walk across a room without the lidar is exactly what Nav2 exists to prevent. go_to_object already covers 'walk to what you see' within 4 m.
- **[G] D3. When Gemini is unavailable, what does verify return?**
  - Options: Keep error 'vlm_unavailable' and attach the detector's objects and note; Return answer 'unsure' plus degraded and objects; Answer yes or no from the detector when the question names a detector class
  - **Recommendation:** Keep the error with the objects attached. Its docstring tells the model that 'unsure' means 'move or turn and verify again', which would invite motion loops, and a detector-based yes/no would claim a check that never happened.
- **[G] D4. Flip works_real=True for look now? Gemini look was verified on the robot on 2026-11-03 (BACKLOG §0). Today the prompt tags look, find_object and verify 'unavailable'.**
  - Options: look only now; find_object and verify after vlm.md steps 3-9; All three now; None until the whole vlm.md checklist is run
  - **Recommendation:** Flip look only, in its own commit with the date. Leave the others until they are watched working.
- **[G] D5. How far should 7.2 telemetry go?**
  - Options: A: one JSON stdout line per turn from onEnd, with no dependency and no service; B: @ai-sdk/otel + OTel SDK + a local collector or Jaeger in docker (new dependencies); C: Langfuse, hosted or self-hosted (new service; prompts and camera text leave the machine unless recordInputs is false)
  - **Recommendation:** A now (G7). Revisit B only when someone needs traces across back and the bridge. Avoid C while prompts carry camera and speech text and the Gemini free-tier data terms (BACKLOG §12) are unresolved.
- **[G] D6. 11.8 (a failed turn leaks its controller) is not in this package, but G6's test exposes it, and the fix is one line in the same function. Fix it here?**
  - Options: Fix it in G6 together with the test; Ship G6 with the error-path check marked todo, and leave the fix to whichever package owns 11.8
  - **Recommendation:** Fix it in G6. The probe confirmed the leak, the fix is one line, and a test that is known to fail should not be merged as todo.
- ✋ **[H] Which push-to-robot recipe should OPERATIONS §5 document? It isn't recorded anywhere in the repo.**
  - Options: `git push <robot>:c3po feat/autonomy:refs/heads/incoming`, then on the robot `git merge --ff-only incoming` (what the task says worked on 2026-11-03); Wait for BACKLOG 8.3 (branch on GitHub) and keep `git pull` on the robot through robot_internet.py's proxy; Document both: push for the cable/no-internet case, pull once 8.3 lands
  - **Recommendation:** Option 1 now, with 'never --force; if ff-only refuses, stop', plus a one-line note that 8.3 will allow a plain pull. It needs no robot internet and refuses to clobber a robot checkout that has diverged.
- **[H] With no SAFETY_CONFIRM_SECRET the PARAR latch can never be cleared from the console. Should the docs describe the manual recovery (writing ~/.c3po/run/stop_acknowledged over ssh)?**
  - Options: No: document only «Reanudar», and 'no secret = stays latched, set the secret and restart back + bridge'; Yes: as an operator-only last resort in OPERATIONS
  - **Recommendation:** No. Claude Code sessions read these docs and have ssh, and un-stopping must stay an operator action (the PARAR invariant). The fix for a missing secret is setting the secret.
- **[H] OPERATIONS §6 says the robot holds no model credentials, but GEMINI_API_KEY lives in apps/bridge/.env. How should the docs resolve this?**
  - Options: Update the doc: the robot holds GEMINI_API_KEY (vision only) and SAFETY_CONFIRM_SECRET; the agent and DB keys stay off it; Keep the rule and add a backlog item to move the Gemini calls into apps/back
  - **Recommendation:** Update the doc now (WP H only fixes docs), point to BACKLOG §12's paid-key and face-blur note, and add 'move vlm to back' to the backlog only if you want the stricter rule back.
- **[H] How should 'verified 2026-11-03' be marked in the test-day docs?**
  - Options: Inline '✅ 2026-11-03' in each row's Expect cell, plus a one-line status under each title; A separate results file per day
  - **Recommendation:** Inline. No new file, and the checklist stays the single place to look.
- **[H] Reorder return-checklist.md so the camera-pitch check (§4, nothing moves) runs right after bring-up?**
  - Options: Yes: §0 → pitch → PARAR → go_to_object → arms; Keep today's order
  - **Recommendation:** Yes. go_to_object's stop distance, the person rule and the arm-reach block all depend on the pitch, and today ranges read about 2× long.
- **[H] What was the 'sudo password dialog' you used today?**
  - Options: Typing the unitree password in your own Terminal over `ssh -t` (what run_c3po and take_camera already print); A macOS askpass/GUI dialog
  - **Recommendation:** Document `ssh -t` in the operator's own terminal, and mention that BACKLOG 1.11 (a narrow sudoers rule for c3po-bridge only) removes the need.
- **[H] Should test-suite counts in the docs be exact numbers?**
  - Options: Replace them with '0 failed' (the audit's advice); Keep exact counts and refresh them each time
  - **Recommendation:** Use '0 failed'. Counts go stale on every commit (906, 277 and 841 are all stale now).
- **[I] 5.3: may the bridge, the process that owns the stop, load compiled IK (pin/pink/coal, about 100 MB of wheels)?**
  - Options: Pure Python first: capsules (I1) plus palm rows (I2); pin/pink/coal only if those fall short (I3 stays gated); Add pin+pink+coal now as an optional `ik` extra, in-process via asyncio.to_thread; Run pin+pink+coal in a separate planner process the bridge calls
  - **Recommendation:** Pure Python first. I1+I2 give collision refusal and roll control with no dependency. kinematics.py:4-6 already argues against compiled deps in the stop process, and FK is proven against pinocchio anyway.
- **[I] 5.4: how do the Menagerie G1 assets (about 36 MB) reach tests and CI, and is a locomotion twin in scope?**
  - Options: Fetch the pinned commit 98a70c569c4c into a gitignored cache; arms only (I4); locomotion deferred; Vendor the MJCF and meshes into the repo; Also build a locomotion twin now (needs a walking policy such as unitree_rl_lab or holosoma, plus unitree_mujoco DDS)
  - **Recommendation:** Fetch pinned, arms only. Locomotion without a policy cannot walk, and SIM_MODE=mujoco_local is a dead profile (Isaac's rt/run_command).
- **[I] 7.1: which tools go through in-stream approval, and what happens to the old refuse→Confirmar path?**
  - Options: T2 only; keep the refusal path for the legacy and realtime voice engines; T2 plus 1.3's arm_navigation and start_walking_waist in typed chat (approval enforced in back only, since the bridge has no T1 grant); Replace the refusal path everywhere (needs approvals in voice/loop.ts and realtime.ts too)
  - **Recommendation:** T2 plus the two 1.3 tools in typed chat; keep the refusal path for legacy and realtime voice. The bridge's T2 grant stays the real limit; the 1.3 tools get a back-side human gate, which is what 1.3 asks for.
  - **Superseded by one-press Confirmar (76995ad, 2026-11-05).** `back` keeps the refused call (owner, chat, exact arguments, 2 min), and one Confirmar runs it once with those arguments, in /chat, on /voice and in the headset (also a spoken «confirmo» there). The model is told not to retry. Whether to keep a native approval UI as an optional extra is open.
- **[I] 6.3: where does better STT go after silence trimming?**
  - Options: Stay on tic-transcript with I7's trimming; decide after measuring and after the 6.7 benchmark; OpenAI Realtime transcription proxied through back (cloud, audio leaves campus, OPENAI_API_KEY already used by realtime.ts); WhisperLive (faster-whisper) self-hosted on a LAN GPU
  - **Recommendation:** Stay on tic-transcript and measure first (I7). Build streaming (I9) only if release-to-text is the dominant delay, and pick the provider after 6.7.
- **[I] 7.6: where does scene memory live, and what may leave the robot?**
  - Options: Bridge JSONL plus keyword and recency (I11), offline, captions only from looks that already happen; apps/back Postgres + pgvector on Neon with embeddings (tic-embed is advertised but was absent from GET /models; extension not installed); No captions at all; object memory only (I10)
  - **Recommendation:** I10 now, I11 later. pgvector only when the log no longer fits a prompt. Captions of people go through Gemini, so use a paid key or blur faces first (BACKLOG §12).
- **[I] 3.5: which YOLOE-26 engine?**
  - Options: Keep text-prompt vocab.txt, add per-class thresholds now (I12), compare on the 3.7 set (I13); Switch to the prompt-free -pf engine (4,585 built-in names); Trim the vocabulary to an indoor list and re-export
  - **Recommendation:** Keep text-prompt and add per-class thresholds now. Choose an engine only with I13's precision numbers on our own frames.
- **[I] 4.7: Jazzy now or later?**
  - Options: After 4.2 (camera-only avoidance) runs on Humble and the stale-scan fail-open is shown to matter; Now, in parallel (I15 first step only); Never; stay on Humble until a JetPack change
  - **Recommendation:** After 4.2 on Humble. The lidar is faulted, and Jazzy adds unverified driver, DDS and Docker risks for fixes we cannot exercise yet.
- **[I] 4.6: may unitree_slam run while c3po is up, for mapping and relocalisation only (never 1102)?**
  - Options: Evaluation only, with c3po down (I16); no production use until a motion-owner arbitration exists; Allow it alongside c3po with 1102 unregistered, and relax OTHER_COMMANDER_PATTERNS; Skip the vendor SLAM entirely (slam_toolbox/FAST-LIO only)
  - **Recommendation:** Evaluation only, with c3po down. The one-commander invariant stays, and two owners of the Mid-360S are untested.
- **[I] 5.6: which Kimodo checkpoint, and are replayed gestures T2?**
  - Options: Kimodo-G1-SEED-v1 (open data), gestures T2 via danger 'medium'; Kimodo-G1-RP-v1 (proprietary data, 'commercially friendly' per NVIDIA, unverified); Keep firmware preset gestures only
  - **Recommendation:** SEED-v1 after reading the repo licence. Replayed arm_sdk gestures are T2 (they are free-form arm motion), and that follows from danger_level='medium' with no special case.

#### 🟢 A1: PARAR latch keeps an in-process copy of the stop (11.2) `S`

- **Backlog:** 11.2 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/bridge/src/bridge/estop.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/conftest.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_estop_cross_process.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_safety.py`
- **Change:** estop.py: add a module global `_signalled_at = 0.0`. signal_stop() sets it to `now` BEFORE the try/write, so a stop that fails to write still latches this process. last_stop_at() returns max(file mtime or 0.0, _signalled_at). This is the OR with the file, and it also covers the file being deleted after a good write. stop_is_standing(), safety.stop_latch() and the /safety/ack 409 check all go through last_stop_at(), so they pick it up with no other change. record_ack() is unchanged: a successful ack file write is newer than the in-process stamp and clears the latch. A failed ack leaves it standing, which is the safe direction. Change the sentinel_write_failed log from warning to error and add `latched_in_process_only=True`. The teleop process is not covered: it never calls signal_stop and reads only the file. 11.3 covers that (a different WP).
- **Tests:** conftest.py isolated_run_dir: add `monkeypatch.setattr(estop, "_signalled_at", 0.0)` so one test's stop does not leak into the next. test_estop_cross_process.py::test_signalling_never_raises_on_an_unwritable_path: change the assertion to `last_stop_at() >= before` and `stop_is_standing() is True`. That test currently pins the fail-open behaviour. New test in test_safety.py: with an unwritable run dir, signal_stop() makes tool_refusal('walk_velocity', T1 meta) refuse with 'PARAR' and _locomotion.send_velocity send zeros. Then point DEFAULT_RUN_DIR back to a writable dir, call record_ack(), and the latch clears. Rewrite test_resume_refuses_a_stop_newer_than_the_one_the_operator_saw to use two real signal_stop() calls about 10 ms apart, not os.utime backdating. Backdating the file below the in-process stamp no longer models 'a newer stop'. Keep green: the whole bridge suite (`uv run pytest`), especially test_teleop_estop.py, test_object_memory_tools.py and test_stop_everything.py.
- **Done when:** With an unwritable C3PO_RUN_DIR, stop_everything still leaves /safety/status stop_standing=true. Every non-T0 MCP tool except damp is refused with the PARAR text, and send_velocity emits zeros until a successful /safety/ack. The rest of the bridge suite passes unchanged.
- **Risk:** If both the sentinel and the ack writes fail (read-only filesystem), this process stays latched until the bridge restarts, and the restart forgets the stop. That is the safe direction while it runs and the old behaviour after a restart. The module global needs the conftest reset, or unrelated tests start latched.
- _apply (major)_: After A1 the tests that guard the cross-process contract stop testing it. test_estop_cross_process.py::test_stop_everything_signals_the_sentinel (:82-97), test_a_stop_is_visible_afterwards (:37-40) and test_a_second_stop_is_distinguishable_from_the_first (:43-48) all read estop.last_stop_at() in the process that just signalled. Once last_stop_at() is max(file, _signalled_at), all three pass even if stop_everything never writes the file. That is the 2026-08-20 regression (teleop kept turning 27 deg after PARAR), and nothing would catch it coming back. The plan only updates the unwritable-path test. **Fix:** Have last_stop_at() call a file-only `_file_stop_at()`, and switch those three tests to assert on `estop.sentinel_path().stat().st_mtime` (or `_file_stop_at()`) >= before. Keep the subprocess test unchanged. Add one test that, with a writable dir, checks the in-process stamp and the file agree.
- _apply (major)_: The A1 robot step proves nothing as written, and the honest version is a hazard. c3po-bridge.service runs as User=unitree, which owns ~/.c3po/run. `chmod a-w ~/.c3po/run` only blocks creating new entries. `stop_everything` already exists from earlier stops, so write_text (O_TRUNC on the file) and os.utime still succeed, and the check passes for the wrong reason. If the step is fixed so the write really fails, the teleop process (which reads only the file, teleop/server.py:243/405, _locomotion.send_velocity) never sees the PARAR, and a headset that is driving keeps driving. **Fix:** Either drop the robot step (the unit tests cover it), or make the files themselves read-only (`chmod a-w ~/.c3po/run/stop_everything ~/.c3po/run/stop_acknowledged` as well). Do it only after `stop_teleop`, with the robot damped or on the gantry. Restore the permissions before Reanudar.
- _apply (minor)_: record_ack() returns `now` on OSError (estop.py:125-127). safety_ack (mcp_server.py:3842-3848) then answers 200 {acknowledged:true} even when nothing was recorded. After A1 the latch still stands at that point, so the console is told 'resumed' while every tool is refused. The plan also says 11.3 covers the teleop-process gap. It does not: 11.3 sends the headset's own stop over the teleop channel, and a console or agent PARAR whose sentinel write failed still never reaches the teleop process. **Fix:** signal_stop/record_ack return whether the write landed. safety_ack returns 500 {error:'ack_not_recorded', **stop_latch()} when stop_standing is still true. stop_everything adds `sentinel_written: false` to its result, so the operator learns the teleop process may not have stopped. List the teleop gap as still open after both A1 and 11.3.

#### 🟢 A2: Bind a T2 grant to the canonical arguments (11.1, bridge) `S`

- **Backlog:** 11.1 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/bridge/src/bridge/safety.py`, `apps/bridge/src/bridge/mcp_server.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_safety.py`
- **Change:** mcp_server.py: add `_canonical_args(tool, arguments) -> str` = json.dumps(tool.fn_metadata.arg_model.model_validate(tool.fn_metadata.pre_parse_json(arguments or {})).model_dump(mode='json'), sort_keys=True, separators=(',',':')). This runs the same pydantic model FastMCP itself validates with (func_metadata.call_fn_with_arg_validation in mcp 1.27.1), so defaults are filled, '0.5' and 0.5 match, and 10 and 10.0 match. I checked this in stub mode: set_hand {side,closure:0.5} and {closure:'0.5',side} give the same string. The hash is computed only on the bridge, so web and back never canonicalise anything. GovernedMCP.call_tool: when safety.needs_confirmation(tool.meta), compute the canonical args. On a pydantic ValidationError raise ToolError('invalid arguments for X: ...') (fail closed). Pass the args to tool_refusal(name, meta, args_key). safety.py: add `needs_confirmation(meta)`, which returns tier == 'T2' for now (A4 widens it). Key `_grants` on (tool, sha256(canonical).hexdigest()). grant_confirmation(tool, args_key, *, via) and _consume_confirmation(tool, args_key) take the key. Log a 12-hex-char prefix. status()['pending_confirmations'] stays a sorted list of tool names, so the console contract is unchanged. safety_confirm: read body {'tool', 'args'}. args defaults to {}. A non-dict args, or args that fail validation, returns 400 {'error':'invalid_arguments'}. A non-confirmable tool returns 400 'not_confirmable' (renamed from 'not_a_t2_tool'). Grant with the digest. The refusal text adds 'retry once with exactly the same arguments'. Keep the substring "needs an operator's confirmation": the web matches on it (tool-part.ts:117).
- **Tests:** test_safety.py: add a helper that grants through the real canonicaliser. Update test_a_confirmation_is_one_shot_and_per_tool, test_an_expired_confirmation_is_refused, test_t2_is_refused_with_a_person_in_reach_even_when_confirmed and test_a_stop_voids_pending_confirmations to the new signature. New: a grant for set_hand{closure:0.5} does not let set_hand{closure:0.9} run, and does let {closure:'0.5', side:'right', shape:None} run, once. The confirm route returns 400 for args='x' and for closure='abc'. The route test with {'tool':'set_hand'} and no args still returns 200 (defaults) with pending == ['set_hand']. test_nothing_on_the_tool_surface_can_grant_a_confirmation must still find only safety_confirm. Keep green: the bridge suite.
- **Done when:** Within 30 s, a grant made for one argument set cannot be used by a call with different arguments. The call with the confirmed arguments (in any key order, with defaults written out or left out) runs exactly once. /safety/status still lists pending tool names.
- **Risk:** It relies on FastMCP internals (Tool.fn_metadata.arg_model / pre_parse_json, mcp 1.27.1). Backlog 1.12's bump to mcp >= 1.30 must keep the canonical-equality test green. Deploy order: a new bridge with an old back (body {tool} only) grants only the default arguments, so Confirmar dead-ends for tools with non-default arguments. Ship A2 and A3 together.
- _apply (minor)_: Signature and contract details. tool_refusal is called with two arguments in test_safety.py:327-337 and test_teleop_estop.py:249. Once grants are keyed by (tool, digest), status()['pending_confirmations'] can list the same tool twice. Canonicalising inside GovernedMCP.call_tool before tool_refusal means a call with invalid arguments made while PARAR stands gets 'invalid arguments' instead of the PARAR text. **Fix:** Make `args_key: str | None = None` keyword-optional (None never matches a grant). Build pending with sorted({t for (t, _), exp in ...}). Wrap the canonicalisation so a ValidationError passes args_key=None and tool_refusal still reports PARAR first. Raise 'invalid arguments' only when there would otherwise be no refusal.
- _apply (minor)_: The plan's notes say that since 3b0cae6 'only safety.py and state.py changed among the gate files'. That is wrong: `git diff --stat 3b0cae6..HEAD` shows mcp_server.py +144, skills.ts +9, tool-part.ts +33 and tool-card.svelte +48 (8c623df added the Confirmar card). The conclusions still hold, but the evidence statement is wrong. **Fix:** Correct the note. Re-read those diffs before editing, because A2, A3 and A5 touch exactly those hunks.

The audit row carries the args. web vr-voice.test.ts: pendingConfirmation(msgs) returns {tool:'reach', input:<the refused part's input>}. The voiceMenu tests are unchanged. chat-logic.test.ts needsConfirmation is unchanged. Run `bun test` in apps/back and apps/web, and `bun run check` in apps/web (Eden body type).

The plan's reason ('the card already shows part.input in Tool.Input') does not hold by default. tool-part.ts:197-201 gives the 'Requiere confirmación' state defaultOpen:false, and Tool.Input sits inside the collapsed CollapsibleContent (tool-card.svelte:104-111). The headset menu label is only 'Confirmar reach'. The audit (README.md:92) asks to 'show the pending call in the console'. **Fix:** Render a one-line summary of part.input (for example `JSON.stringify(part.input)`, truncated) next to the Confirmar button, or set defaultOpen:true for that state. In the VR menu, add a short argument summary to the label.
- _apply (minor)_: The headset's stale-selection check compares only the tool name. If a newer refused call of the same tool with different arguments arrives between building the menu and the click, the click grants arguments the operator never saw. **Fix:** Key the menu item by toolCallId (VOICE_CONFIRM + toolCallId). At click time, look up that exact part and post its input. Grant nothing if it is no longer the last pending part.

#### 🟢 A4: High-danger T1 needs a confirmation on the bridge (1.3, bridge) `S`

- **Backlog:** 1.3 · **Depends on:** A2
- **Files:** `apps/bridge/src/bridge/safety.py`, `apps/bridge/src/bridge/mcp_server.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_safety.py`, `apps/bridge/scripts/teleop_smoke_test.py`
- **Change:** safety.needs_confirmation(meta): tier == 'T2' or (tier == 'T1' and danger_level == 'high'). Today that is exactly arm_navigation and start_walking_waist, and any future high-danger T1 joins automatically. tool_refusal: the PARAR latch first (unchanged). The person-in-reach rule stays T2-only (it is about arm reach). Then `if not needs_confirmation(meta): return None`. The same SAFETY_T2_CONFIRM switch covers both. The refusal names the tool as 'a T2 tool' or 'a high-danger tool' and keeps "needs an operator's confirmation", so the existing chat Confirmar card (tool-part.ts needsConfirmation) and the VR menu offer the button with no web logic change. safety_confirm already uses needs_confirmation (from A2), so it now grants these two as well. The gate is on the bridge, so every MCP caller that does not hold the secret is the agent: typed chat, both voice engines and Claude Code are all covered by one rule. teleop_smoke_test.py's restore step (it calls prepare and start_walking_waist over MCP at ~:453) prints the tool's refusal text when isError is set, so the operator knows to run the headset bring-up.
- **Tests:** test_safety.py: the confirmable set from the live catalogue is exactly {move_arm, set_hand, reach, pick, place, handover, arm_navigation, start_walking_waist}. mcp.call_tool('start_walking_waist', {}) and arm_navigation({reason:'test arm', seconds:5}) are refused with "needs an operator's confirmation". After a confirm-route grant with the same arguments each runs once. With SAFETY_T2_CONFIRM off they run unconfirmed. A person at 0.6 m does not block start_walking_waist (no reach rule for T1). test_t0_and_t1_tools_are_not_gated (walk_velocity) stays green. Keep green: test_arm_navigation.py and test_mcp_catalogue.py.
- **Done when:** No MCP client without the secret can run arm_navigation or start_walking_waist unconfirmed while SAFETY_T2_CONFIRM is on. The refusal shows the existing Confirmar button in chat. Every other T1 tool is unaffected.
- **Risk:** On its own this breaks the headset bring-up (start_walking_waist via /invoke) until A5 lands, so ship A4 and A5 together. Claude Code and teleop_smoke_test.py lose unconfirmed start_walking_waist and arm_navigation; they must confirm with the secret (curl /safety/confirm) or use the console. SAFETY_T2_CONFIRM=off now also opens these two, so update its comment and docs.
- _apply (major)_: The plan breaks the invariant that a model can never grant itself anything. A4's risk text says Claude Code 'must confirm with the secret (curl /safety/confirm)', and A7 repeats a curl-with-secret hint in c3po_preflight. Claude Code is a model. Also, apps/back/.env on the Mac holds the same SAFETY_CONFIRM_SECRET (apps/back/.env.example:26-30), and the bridge is reachable at 127.0.0.1:8001 through the tunnel. So the protection the safety_confirm docstring relies on ('as long as it cannot read the robot's .env', mcp_server.py:3786-3788) does not hold for an agent with a shell on the Mac. The claim 'no MCP client without the secret' is weaker than stated. **Fix:** Agent-facing text and docs say only that the agent asks a human to press Confirmar. The secret curl stays in the human checklist (safety.md), marked operator-only. Add a Claude Code deny rule for reading apps/*/.env, or record this as a known limit in safety.md. Keep 'You cannot give it' in the new refusal text.
- _apply (minor)_: Comments and tests that describe the old rule go stale: skill_meta.py:50-52 ('T1 — ... free'), the safety.py module docstring :26-30 (only T2 gated), runtime.ts:195-197 and runtime.test.ts:139 ('A high-danger T1 (arm_navigation) has no such gate'). teleop_smoke_test.ensure_walk_program (:453) has already been broken since the PARAR latch landed (prepare is refused after stage 6 until /safety/ack). Printing the refusal helps, but restoring now needs an ack and a confirm. **Fix:** Update those comments in A4. In the smoke test, say that the restore needs Reanudar and then the console bring-up (A5 path), not MCP.

#### 🟢 A5: Console and headset bring-up keep working: an admin's /invoke is the confirmation (1.3, back + web copy) `S`

- **Backlog:** 1.3 · **Depends on:** A3, A4
- **Files:** `apps/back/src/routes/skills.ts`, `apps/back/src/routes/skills.test.ts`, `apps/back/src/agent/runtime.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/chat/tool-part.ts`
- **Change:** skills.ts /:name/invoke runs after the admin check and the Ajv validation. When `skill.tier !== 'T2' && skill.dangerLevel === 'high'`, it first calls grantOnBridge(name, args, user) from A3, which forwards the secret and writes the audit row. If that is not ok it returns the bridge's status and body (503 confirmation_not_configured, 403, 400) and does NOT dispatch. Otherwise it dispatches as today. T2 /invoke is left unchanged: arms keep the explicit two-step Confirmar, and no console button invokes T2 directly. runtime.ts buildSystemPrompt (:249): tag `/needs-operator-confirmation` when tier T2 or dangerLevel high, so the model asks first. tool-part.ts:199: change the note from 'Movimiento de brazo o mano: …' to a generic 'Un operador debe confirmarlo (una llamada, estos argumentos, 30 s).', which is also correct for arm_navigation.
- **Tests:** skills.test.ts: an admin /invoke of a fixture {dangerLevel:'high', tier:'T1'} POSTs /safety/confirm with {tool, args} before callTool. If the grant returns 503 or 403, callTool is never called and the status passes through. A low or medium skill and a T2 skill never trigger a confirm forward. A non-admin still gets 403 before any forward. runtime.test.ts: buildSystemPrompt tags arm_navigation (operator mode) with needs-operator-confirmation. Keep green: `bun test` in apps/back and apps/web, and `bun run check` in apps/web.
- **Done when:** The headset bring-up damp → prepare → start_walking_waist works with no extra click. An agent's arm_navigation in typed chat is refused with a Confirmar card, and after Confirmar the agent's retry arms the gate once. Every bring-up pre-grant leaves a safety_confirm audit row.
- **Risk:** SAFETY_CONFIRM_SECRET becomes required in apps/back/.env for the bring-up. Without it start_walking_waist returns 503, a regression on test day if the secret is missing (it is already needed for Reanudar and T2). If the dispatch fails after a successful pre-grant, a 30 s grant bound to those exact arguments stays pending. The admin intended that call anyway, and PARAR or Reanudar clears it. The predicate is duplicated in TS and Python: a drift fails closed and visibly.
- _apply (minor)_: The predicate `skill.tier !== 'T2' && skill.dangerLevel === 'high'` fails open: catalogue.ts:79 makes tier optional. A catalogue entry without tier would give the T2 high tools (move_arm, reach, pick, place, handover) an automatic pre-grant on /invoke, which skips the two-step Confirmar the plan says T2 keeps. The predicate is also duplicated in TS and Python. **Fix:** Use `skill.tier === 'T1' && skill.dangerLevel === 'high'`. Better: have skill_meta.to_meta emit `needs_confirmation` (one Python function, used by safety.needs_confirmation as well), and have back read that flag for both /invoke and the buildSystemPrompt tag.

#### 🟢 A6: Refuse arm motion and long locomotion below 20% battery (1.10, bridge part) `S`

- **Backlog:** 1.10 · **Depends on:** A4
- **Files:** `apps/bridge/src/bridge/sdk/state.py`, `apps/bridge/src/bridge/safety.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_safety.py`
- **Change:** state.py: add `LOW_BATTERY_PCT = 20` (the vendor threshold already used at :451, which now uses the constant) and `existing_sampler()`, which returns _sampler_singleton without creating it, mirroring perception_link.existing_link. safety.py tool_refusal, after the PARAR check and before the person and confirmation checks, so a grant is kept: if the tool drains the battery (tier T2, or classification locomotion with expected_duration_s >= 15 or danger high; that is walk_to, go_to_object, navigate_to, navigate_to_landmark, look_around, arm_navigation), read battery_pct from existing_sampler().get_state(). If it is below LOW_BATTERY_PCT, refuse: 'refused: battery is N% (below 20%), so X does not run: arm motion and long walks can stop mid-task. walk_velocity, turn, open_hands, release_arm_control and stop still work. Tell the operator to charge the robot.' An unknown battery (stub, Isaac with no BMS, no message yet, no sampler) is allowed, consistent with state.py's 'null is not evidence'. Add the comment `shortcut: checked at call time only; a task already running is not stopped when the pack crosses 20%, add a check in the skill loops if that matters`.
- **Tests:** test_safety.py: put a StateSampler with fresh lowstate and _bms soc=15 into state._sampler_singleton via monkeypatch (same construction as test_state_battery._sampler). Then walk_to and move_arm are refused with 'battery'. walk_velocity, turn, start_walking_waist and open_hands are not battery-refused. soc=25 is allowed. soc None and no sampler are allowed. A move_arm grant survives a battery refusal. Pin the drain set from the live catalogue so a new long locomotion tool shows up in review. Keep green: test_state_battery.py and the bridge suite.
- **Done when:** With battery_pct < 20, the agent and the console cannot start arm motion or long walks, and the refusal says why and what still works. With the battery unknown or >= 20, nothing changes. Stop, release and short teleop moves are never battery-refused.
- **Risk:** It edits the same function as A2 and A4, so land it after them. The headset teleop arms and base run in the teleop process and are not covered (not an MCP path). look_around is included as 'long'; drop it if the user disagrees (see the decisions).
- _apply (major)_: The drain set leaves out `dance` (gesture/low/T1, expected_duration_s=20, a whole-body vendor routine), the longest motion in the live catalogue. Backlog 1.10 says 'refuse long tasks or arms'. The wording 'tier T2, or classification locomotion with expected_duration_s >= 15 or danger high' can also be read as including start_walking_waist (posture/high), which contradicts the plan's own test that start_walking_waist is not refused. **Fix:** State the predicate exactly, without classification: non-T0 and (tier == 'T2' or expected_duration_s >= 15 or (classification == 'locomotion' and danger_level == 'high')). Against the live catalogue that refuses the 6 T2 tools plus walk_to, go_to_object, navigate_to, navigate_to_landmark, look_around, dance and arm_navigation. It leaves start_walking_waist, turn (12 s), walk_velocity, short presets, open_hands, release_arm_control and damp free. Pin that set in the test.
- _apply (minor)_: The plan cites state.py the wrong way round: state.py:93-95 and :452-453 say null must NOT be read as healthy, but the plan quotes them in support of allowing unknown. There is no staleness check either: _BmsSnapshot keeps the last soc forever, so a dead BMS subscription freezes battery_pct (a stale 25% allows forever, a stale 15% refuses forever). sampler.get_state() builds the whole state dict and returns battery None whenever lowstate has not arrived. **Fix:** Keep 'unknown is allowed', but say it is a decision, not something state.py backs. Log `safety.battery_unknown` when SIM_MODE=real. Read soc_pct and received_at directly (a small locked accessor on StateSampler). Treat age > 60 s as unknown and log it. Wrap the read in try/except → unknown.

#### 🟢 B1: Bridge acts on the spoken stop for the legacy engine too `S`

- **Backlog:** 1.2 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/bridge/src/bridge/skills/listen.py`, `apps/bridge/src/bridge/mcp_server.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_mic_listener.py`
- **Change:** listen.py MicListener: add `clear_on_stop(on_stop)`, which sets `_on_stop = None` only if `self._on_stop is on_stop`, so a stale disconnect cannot disarm a newer subscriber. mcp_server.py: move the existing `stop_locally` closure (run_coroutine_threadsafe(stop_everything(), loop)) into a small `_local_stop(loop)` helper used by both routes. voice_audio_input keeps `listener.start(on_stop=cb)`. voice_events arms with `listener.set_on_stop(cb)` and passes cb to `_voice_event_body(request, listener, on_stop=None)`, which gains a `finally: listener.clear_on_stop(on_stop)`. `_voice_pcm_body` gets the same optional param, and its unconditional `set_on_stop(None)` becomes `clear_on_stop(on_stop)`. Both existing 2-arg call signatures stay valid.
- **Tests:** New in test_mic_listener.py: (a) calling voice_events with get_mic_listener monkeypatched arms the listener, and invoking `lis._on_stop('emergencia')` runs a monkeypatched mcp_server.stop_everything without apps/back; (b) aclose() on the SSE body disarms; (c) arm A, arm B, clear A leaves B armed. test_voice_sse_emits_events_keepalives_and_stops_on_disconnect and both test_voice_pcm_* tests must stay green, plus the full bridge suite (`uv run pytest`).
- **Done when:** While apps/back's legacy loop holds /telemetry/voice/events open, a detected stop phrase calls stop_everything inside the bridge (the PARAR latch stands) even if apps/back is blocked or dead. After the subscriber disconnects, the callback is cleared. A reconnect within 0.5 s stays armed. Realtime behaviour is unchanged.
- **Risk:** A mis-decode now halts the robot on the legacy engine too (the cheap direction, and the same opt-in the loop's actOnStopPhrase already defaults to). stop_everything runs twice (bridge-local plus back's event handler). It is idempotent, but check that the two audit rows are not confusing. If Starlette never iterates the body (instant disconnect), the callback stays armed until the next clear, which fails safe.
- _apply (minor)_: Arming happens in the voice_events route, but disarming happens in the generator's finally. An async generator that is never started never runs its finally: Starlette can cancel before the first iteration. In that case the callback stays armed outside any session, so crowd noise at the stand latches PARAR with no voice session running. The risk note calls this fail-safe, but it also contradicts the MicListener contract. **Fix:** Pass on_stop into `_voice_event_body` and arm as the first statement inside its try (`if on_stop: listener.set_on_stop(on_stop)`), with `clear_on_stop(on_stop)` in finally, so arm and disarm are always paired. Do the same in `_voice_pcm_body` for realtime. Test (b) then covers both.
- _apply (minor)_: MicListener.start() calls `self.set_on_stop(on_stop)` unconditionally with a default of None (listen.py:825). Any `listener.start()` with no argument therefore disarms a live subscriber. That includes the `listen` tool's restart when the thread is not running (mcp_server.py:2480). Separately, `_run` reads `self._on_stop` twice: it checks `is not None`, then calls it. If clear_on_stop runs between the two reads, the call raises TypeError, which the `except Exception` swallows as on_stop_failed, and the stop is not acted on. **Fix:** In start(), set the callback only when one is passed (`if on_stop is not None`). Realtime always passes one, and the boot-time slot is already None. In _run, read once: `cb = self._on_stop; if cb is not None: cb(hit)`. Add a test that start() with no argument keeps an armed callback.
- _apply (minor)_: The callback is a single slot. If A arms, then B arms, then B clears, A is still connected but disarmed. back still acts in that case, so this only falls back to today's behaviour. The double stop has a timing edge: back's own stop_everything can land after a fast «Reanudar» and latch PARAR again. That is the safe direction, but it will surprise operators. Several docstrings also go stale: the MicListener docstring ('REPORTED, not acted on'), the listen tool comment (mcp_server.py ~2499) and voice.ts:115-118 ('only the legacy loop does'). **Fix:** Document the single-slot limit with a `shortcut:` comment, or use a counter. Add the re-latch case to the B1 robot step. Update the three docstrings in the same change.

#### 🟢 B2: Legacy voice: pump events separately, stop at once, abort the turn `S`

- **Backlog:** 1.1 · **Depends on:** B1
- **Files:** `apps/back/src/voice/loop.ts`, `apps/back/src/voice/loop.test.ts`, `apps/back/src/voice/conversation.ts`, `apps/back/src/voice/conversation.test.ts`, `apps/back/src/routes/voice.ts`
- **Change:** loop.ts runEvents: the for-await no longer awaits turns. A `stop` event is handled inline: `stopEpoch++`, abort the current turn's AbortController, abortAllTurns('PARAR (voice)'), then `await callTool('stop_everything')` with its error recorded in lastError instead of reconnecting the stream. A `speech` event is chained onto a `turnTail` promise, so turns still never overlap. It is skipped if `stopEpoch` changed since it was heard, and runs with a fresh per-turn AbortController. Turn errors are caught per turn and logged as voice.turn_failed, so they no longer tear down the SSE stream. stop() also aborts the in-flight turn and awaits only the pump. VoiceLoopDeps.runAgent becomes `(utterance, signal?)`. conversation.ts: `turn(utterance, signal?)` passes the signal to `complete(messages, signal)`, the queued `speak` closure returns early once aborted, and `signal?.throwIfAborted()` runs after the stream loop, so an aborted partial reply is neither spoken nor remembered. routes/voice.ts: complete passes `abortSignal: signal` to runAgentChat, and runAgentOnUtterance forwards the signal. The polling `tick()` path is untouched: production always injects `events`.
- **Tests:** loop.test.ts additions: (1) a stop event arriving while runAgent blocks until its own signal aborts calls stop_everything and aborts that signal with no wait; (2) speech heard before a stop is not run after it; (3) runs never overlap on the event path, with two speeches back to back; (4) stop() resolves while a turn is still in flight. conversation.test.ts: an aborted turn makes no further speak calls and leaves history() unchanged. All existing loop and conversation tests stay green. `bun test` in apps/back has a baseline of 78 pass across voice/agent/mounted/safety.
- **Done when:** Test (1) passes with a runAgent that never resolves by itself. POST /voice/stop returns without waiting for the agent turn. A model error no longer reconnects the bridge event stream. Speech said during a turn is still answered afterwards, in order.
- **Risk:** An aborted turn's `say` already sent with wait_for_completion still finishes on the robot, because a bridge call cannot be cancelled from back. Speech said before a stop is now dropped on purpose. The depends_on is merge order only: B1 is the real safety net, so ship it first.
- _apply (major)_: A PARAR from the console or headset does not abort the loop's per-turn signal, so the partial reply is still spoken and kept in history. skills.ts:234 abortAllTurns() aborts the controller that runAgentChat creates inside beginTurn (turns.ts:16-19). It does not abort the `outer` AbortController that B2 creates in loop.ts and passes through conversation.turn -> complete -> runAgentChat. On abort, streamText closes textStream normally (abort chunk, dist/index.js:9347-9363) and `signal.throwIfAborted()` does not throw. Result: conversation.ts:187 speaks the pending sentence and :195 stores the partial reply. The acceptance line 'an aborted partial reply is neither spoken nor remembered' therefore fails for the most common abort source. **Fix:** Create the loop's per-turn signal with the existing registry: `const { signal, done } = beginTurn()` from agent/turns (loop.ts already imports that module), pass `signal` to runAgent, and call done() in a finally. abortAllTurns then aborts it from anywhere. Add a loop.test.ts case: abortAllTurns() while runAgent is blocked aborts the signal runAgent received, and conversation.test.ts covers that nothing more is spoken.
- _apply (major)_: After a stop/start, two turns can dispatch to the robot at once, which breaks the 'turns never overlap' invariant the plan says it keeps. With B2, stop() returns while a turn is still in flight and only aborts its signal. Tool execute never receives the abort signal (runtime.ts:310-321 calls callTool with no signal; walk_to runs up to 300 s), so an aborted turn can still be inside a walk. If an operator ends and restarts the conversation (/voice/start resets and l.start() makes a new pump with a fresh turnTail), the first new turn can send motion while the old walk_to is still running. Before B2 this could not happen, because stop() awaited the whole turn. **Fix:** Keep `turnTail` on the VoiceLoop instance and do not reset it on stop/start, so new turns chain after the old, aborted one. Alternatively, have start() await the previous tail. Add a test: stop() resolves, start() runs, the old runAgent stays blocked until released, and the new turn's runAgent does not start before then.
- _apply (minor)_: Speech chained during a long turn is unbounded and answered one item at a time. The polling path joins utterances into one turn for exactly this reason (loop.ts:233-237). Also, MicListener records the stop event first and then usually transcribes the same words ('para para') as a speech event with a later seq. That speech counts as heard after the stop, so it is not skipped and starts an agent turn while PARAR stands. **Fix:** Join the speech items still queued when a turn finishes into one utterance before running the next turn. Optionally drop a speech event whose normalised text is only the stop phrase. Add one test for each.

#### 🟢 B3: Agent turn registry: done on every outcome `S`

- **Backlog:** 11.8 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/back/src/agent/runtime.ts`, `apps/back/src/agent/runtime.test.ts`
- **Change:** runAgentChat: wrap everything from beginTurn to streamText in try/catch, calling `done()` and rethrowing on a throw. After streamText, replace `onEnd: done, onAbort: done` with `result.steps.then(done, done)`, plus a one-line comment that ai 7.0.140's flush skips onEnd when the first step fails, while `steps` settles on success, abort and error. The steps getter consumes a tee'd branch, so the UI stream is unaffected. Add a test-only `model?: LanguageModel` option (default provider.chatModel(MODEL)) and skip the API_KEY guard only when a model is injected. This needs a seam because API_KEY and provider are captured at module load, and bun shares one module registry across test files.
- **Tests:** runtime.test.ts, reusing its listSkills mock, with MockLanguageModelV4 from 'ai/test' (installed): a first model call that throws, a one-step text success, and an outer AbortSignal abort all end with activeTurnCount() === 0 after draining textStream. A pre-stream failure (listSkills mockImplementationOnce rejects) makes runAgentChat reject and leaves the count at 0. turns.test.ts and the skills.test.ts PARAR test stay green. This also covers half of 11.9 ('runAgentChat really calls done').
- **Done when:** No code path leaves a controller in activeTurns after its turn ends. PARAR still aborts turns in flight.
- **Risk:** Low. The consumeStream branch buffers if the UI consumer is slow (the turn already runs to completion unless request.signal aborts). The `model` option exists only for tests and must not be exposed through the route.
- _apply (minor)_: `result.steps` calls `this.consumeStream()` (ai dist/index.js:10185-10186), so a tee'd branch drains the model stream eagerly. The agent loop then loses backpressure from the HTTP consumer. Before, a client that stayed connected but stopped reading (for example a Quest tab suspended when the headset sleeps) paused the loop. Now the loop keeps dispatching skills. The risk note 'the turn already runs to completion unless request.signal aborts' is therefore not accurate. Also, replacing onEnd/onAbort drops a backstop: on the internal `controller.error` path (~9331), _steps may never settle. **Fix:** Keep `onEnd: done, onAbort: done` and add `result.steps.then(done, done)`; done is an idempotent Set.delete. State the backpressure trade-off in the comment. Request.signal and PARAR still abort, so this is acceptable once written down.
- _apply (minor)_: The new tests assert `activeTurnCount() === 0` on the module-global Set in turns.ts. Bun runs all test files in one process, and turns.test.ts and the skills.test.ts PARAR test also touch that Set, so a turn leaked by another file makes these assertions flaky. **Fix:** Assert a before/after delta, or call abortAllTurns() in beforeEach.

#### 🟢 B4: Bound the agent's model context (headset and console) `S`

- **Backlog:** 7.3 · **Depends on:** B3
- **Files:** `apps/back/src/agent/runtime.ts`, `apps/back/src/agent/runtime.test.ts`
- **Change:** An exported `boundHistory(messages)` in runtime.ts: `messages.slice(-40)`, then convertToModelMessages(..., { ignoreIncompleteToolCalls: true }), then `pruneMessages({ reasoning: 'before-last-message', toolCalls: 'before-last-10-messages', emptyMessages: 'remove' })`. runAgentChat uses it instead of the inline convert. Add a comment of the form `shortcut: count-based window, not tokens; upgrade to a token budget if the gateway starts rejecting long chats`. Persisted chats are untouched; this changes only the model input. Keeping the last 10 model messages' tool parts preserves the refused T2 call's arguments for the «Confirmado: podés ejecutar X» retry.
- **Tests:** runtime.test.ts: 100 UI messages with tool parts give a model input from at most 40 UI messages. No tool-call or tool-result survives outside the last 10 model messages, the last turn's call and result are kept, and no orphan tool result remains.
- **Done when:** A long headset session sends bounded context. The T2 confirm flow from the headset and the console still re-issues the same call.
- **Risk:** The model forgets old perception and tool results. That is intended, since they are stale. A turn longer than 10 model messages after a refused T2 call would lose its arguments, which is rare because the model stops at the refusal.
- _apply (minor)_: Pruning tool parts interacts with backlog 11.1. A T2 grant is consumed by tool name only (safety.py:325 _consume_confirmation). If pruning ever drops the refused call's arguments, the model can retry the same tool with invented arguments under the operator's name-only confirmation. The 10-message window makes this unlikely, but the plan treats it as UX only. **Fix:** In the risk note, say that a lost argument set is retried under a name-only grant until 11.1 lands. Add a test case where the refused T2 call sits exactly at the boundary of the last 10 model messages and survives.

#### 🟢 B5: apps/back listens on loopback by default `S`

- **Backlog:** 1.4 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/back/src/index.ts`, `apps/back/src/lib/env.ts`, `apps/back/.env.example`, `apps/back/src/routes/mounted.test.ts`, `docs/OPERATIONS.md`
- **Change:** env.ts adds `HOST: process.env.HOST || '127.0.0.1'`. index.ts uses `.listen({ port: env.PORT, hostname: env.HOST })` (Elysia 1.4.30 listen takes Partial<Serve>). .env.example adds `HOST=127.0.0.1`, which the env contract test requires. In the OPERATIONS.md §2 port table, apps/back becomes 'loopback (HOST)', with HOST=0.0.0.0 only for a LAN box behind TLS. All client URLs keep `localhost`: Node 26 (the Vite SSR runtime) and browsers fall back from ::1 to 127.0.0.1, and adb reverse already uses IPv4.
- **Tests:** Structural test in mounted.test.ts: index.ts's listen call passes `hostname: env.HOST`, and env.ts defaults HOST to '127.0.0.1'. env.test.ts stays green with HOST documented.
- **Done when:** `lsof -nP -iTCP:3000 -sTCP:LISTEN` shows 127.0.0.1:3000 only. The console at localhost:3001 and the Quest via scripts/quest_setup.sh still work.
- **Risk:** Any LAN client of :3000 stops working. None was found: the console's Vite is already 127.0.0.1-only, the Quest uses adb reverse, and the robot never calls back.
- _apply (minor)_: `HOST` is a risky name. zsh defines $HOST as the machine's hostname (here Olivers-MacBook-Pro-2.local). It is not exported by default, but a dotfile or tool that exports it would make Bun bind the LAN address and drop loopback: the fix would silently invert and localhost:3000 would break. The house convention is BRIDGE_HOST (mcp_server.py:76, scripts/robot/c3po-bridge.service:46). Also, turbo runs tasks in strict env mode, so a shell-set value would not reach back through `bun run dev`. **Fix:** Name it BACK_HOST (default '127.0.0.1') in env.ts and .env.example, and say it is set in apps/back/.env, not in the shell. Point the structural test at BACK_HOST.
- _apply (minor)_: OPERATIONS.md documents apps/back on an Ubuntu LAN box that serves a remote console (diagram line 26; §5 'apps/back → Ubuntu LAN box', lines 242-260). The plan updates only the §2 port table, so the next deploy of the compiled binary would quietly stop answering LAN clients. Also, the listen call is at index.ts:97, not :93. **Fix:** Add BACK_HOST=0.0.0.0 to the §5 deploy env notes and the per-deploy checklist there, next to the existing 'verify per deploy target' bullets.

#### 🟢 B6: Close public sign-up; create operators from a CLI `S`

- **Backlog:** 1.4 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/back/src/lib/auth.ts`, `apps/back/scripts/create-user.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/auth-client.ts`, `apps/web/tests/auth-params.test.ts`, `docs/SESSION-CHECKLIST.md`
- **Change:** auth.ts: `emailAndPassword: { enabled: true, disableSignUp: true }`, plus `disableSignUp: true` on the github and google configs. That flag is ORed in sign-in.mjs:197, so a client's requestSignUp cannot bypass it. New scripts/create-user.ts: `bun scripts/create-user.ts <email> <name> [--admin]` reads the password with Bun's prompt() and calls `auth.api.createUser({ body: { email, password, name, role } })`. Server-side without headers this needs no session and is not blocked by disableSignUp (better-auth 1.7.7 plugins/admin/routes.mjs:153-154), so it creates the first admin with no restart. admin-bootstrap stays: it still promotes existing ADMIN_EMAILS accounts, and its unverified-email risk is gone once nobody can self-register. Web AUTH_ERRORS adds EMAIL_PASSWORD_SIGN_UP_DISABLED: 'El registro está cerrado. Pedile una cuenta a un admin.' SESSION-CHECKLIST §3c replaces the sign-up curl with the script.
- **Tests:** Back: POST /api/auth/sign-up/email through auth.handler returns 400 EMAIL_PASSWORD_SIGN_UP_DISABLED. The check comes before any DB access (sign-up.mjs:145); if importing auth.ts needs a live DB in the test env, assert the config structurally instead. Web: an auth-params.test.ts case for the new code. The script is a thin wrapper, verified by hand.
- **Done when:** Public sign-up (email and OAuth) is refused with a Spanish message on /signup. `create-user.ts --admin` gives an account that can log in and drive. Existing accounts are unaffected.
- **Risk:** Nobody without terminal access can get an account; that is the point. Existing unknown accounts (e.g. backlog 8.5 operador@c3po.local) survive and must be banned or deleted in /settings.
- _apply (minor)_: The test premise is wrong. sign-up.mjs:144 wraps the disableSignUp check in runWithTransaction, which runs `runtimeSchemaCheckFor(adapter)` and `adapter.transaction(...)` before the check (@better-auth/core dist/context/transaction.mjs:54-66). So 'the check comes before any DB access' is false, and a handler test without Postgres will hang or return 500 rather than 400. **Fix:** Assert the config structurally: `auth.options.emailAndPassword.disableSignUp === true` and `disableSignUp` on github/google, with drizzle mocked the way safety.test.ts does it. Run the live 400 check by hand in the B6 robot/Mac step.
- _apply (minor)_: admin createUser checks only `assertPasswordNotTooLong` (plugins/admin/routes.mjs ~195), not a minimum length, so the CLI can create an admin with a 1-character password. Bun's prompt() also echoes the password on screen. apps/back/README.md:59-62 still tells people to create accounts with POST /api/auth/sign-up/email. **Fix:** In create-user.ts, enforce a password of at least 8 characters, read it without echo (stdin or an env var), and map USER_ALREADY_EXISTS to a clear message. Update apps/back/README.md alongside SESSION-CHECKLIST §3c.

- **Files:** `apps/back/src/routes/agent.ts`, `apps/back/src/routes/voice.ts`, `apps/back/src/routes/skills.ts`, `apps/back/src/routes/agent-voice-admin.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/chat/chat-error.ts`, `apps/web/tests/chat-logic.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/robot/vr-voice.svelte.ts`
