#### ⚪ B8: The robot speaks the headset's agent reply `S`

- **Backlog:** 6.1 · **Depends on:** B7, B2
- **Files:** `apps/back/src/voice/conversation.ts`, `apps/back/src/voice/conversation.test.ts`, `apps/back/src/routes/agent.ts`
- **Change:** conversation.ts exports `speakReply(parts, speak)`. It joins the reply's text parts (the same text the headset panel shows as 'Robot: …'), splits it with the existing splitForSpeech (500 chars, the say tool's max_length), and awaits speak per chunk in order. In agent.ts, the toUIMessageStreamResponse onEnd, before `if (!chatId) return`, calls `if (mode === 'voice' && !isAborted) void speakReply(responseMessage.parts, (text) => auditedCall('say', { text, language: 'spanish', wait_for_completion: true }, { userId: user.id, source: 'voice' })).catch((e) => console.warn('[agent] voice reply not spoken', e))`. wait_for_completion is needed because each say mints a new stream_id that barges in. The call is not awaited, so persistence and the HTTP stream do not wait on speech. The bridge needs no change: the existing Spanish say already runs Piper to PlayStream.
- **Tests:** conversation.test.ts: text parts are joined and tool parts ignored; a reply over 500 chars goes out as ordered sequential chunks; empty text makes no call; a failing chunk rejects. The route wiring (two lines) is verified on the headset.
- **Done when:** A headset push-to-talk turn is heard from the robot's speaker once the turn ends. An aborted turn (PARAR) or an operator-mode chat speaks nothing.
- **Risk:** PARAR pressed after the turn ends does not cut speech already playing (stop_everything has no stop_play). The speaker is shared with gemm. If Piper is missing on the robot, say returns spanish_tts_unavailable, which is logged only. Latency is the turn time plus synthesis. The depends_on is merge order only.
- _apply (minor)_: Gating on `!isAborted` is too weak. handleUIMessageStreamFinish (ai dist/index.js:7245-7262) reports a headset disconnect as isCancelled with status 'unknown' and isAborted false, and a turn that failed mid-way as outcome 'failed'. Both would speak a partial reply. Also, say is T0 and so allowed while a stop stands (safety.py:299), so a PARAR after the turn ends does not stop chunks 2..n. **Fix:** Speak only when `outcome.status === 'completed' && mode === 'voice'`. Run speakReply under `beginTurn()` and check `signal.aborted` before each chunk, with done() in finally, so abortAllTurns ends the remaining chunks. Test: abort after chunk 1 means chunk 2 is not sent.
- _apply (minor)_: The route branch (mode plus outcome gate) is new non-trivial logic, but the plan verifies it only on the headset, which breaks the 'every non-trivial branch gets a test' rule. **Fix:** Extract `shouldSpeak({ mode, outcome })` next to speakReply and unit-test it, or cover it in the B7 route harness with auditedCall mocked.

**Critic on WP B** (verdict: needs_changes)

- _missing:_ Docstring updates after B1, which flips 'reported, not acted on' for legacy sessions: the MicListener class docstring (listen.py ~751-758), the listen tool's stop_heard comment (mcp_server.py ~2497-2499), voice.ts:115-118, and the VoiceLoop actOnStopPhrase doc (setting it to false no longer prevents a stop once the bridge acts).
- _missing:_ Web: apps/web/src/lib/robot/voice-loop.ts 403 mapping and its test (B7). apps/back/README.md sign-up instructions (B6). OPERATIONS.md §5 LAN-box env (B5).
- _missing:_ Verified as stated: 78/78 back tests pass (src/voice, src/agent, mounted, safety); bridge tests/test_mic_listener.py gives 40 pass (the full bridge suite was not run); the listed versions are ai 7.0.140, better-auth 1.7.7 and elysia 1.4.30; flush skips onEnd when no step was recorded (dist 9263-9266); pruneMessages keeps call/result pairs together (no orphans); OAuth signup_disabled is already mapped in apps/web/src/routes/login/auth-params.ts:86; 6.8 is done as claimed.
- _missing:_ B8 robot pre-check: install_piper.sh fetches from github.com and huggingface.co with curl, so the step must export https_proxy/HTTPS_PROXY from scripts/robot_internet.py first. The script is idempotent and writes only under ~/.local/share/piper (C3PO's own path, not gemm's).
- _missing:_ No plan item opens a path where the robot moves after PARAR, weakens the latch, opens the cmd_vel gate, or lets a model grant T2. The two B2 majors are invariant breaks, one about the voice turn and one about turn overlap, not latch bypasses.

**Robot/headset checks for WP B:**

- [ ] B1 (robot, VOICE_ENGINE=legacy): start voice from the console. Say the stop phrase during a slow walk_velocity, then again with apps/back stopped. The robot stops both times, the bridge log shows mic_listener.stop_phrase plus stop_everything, PARAR stands, and console «Reanudar» clears it. Kill and restart back within 1 s and confirm the phrase still stops the robot (re-armed).
- [ ] B2 (robot): ask the legacy loop for a walk ('caminá hasta la puerta'), say 'para' mid-walk. The stop arrives within about 1 s, the console shows the turn aborted, the partial reply is not spoken, and «Terminar conversación» returns at once. Backlog §2 'spoken para during a walk' runs here.
- [ ] B8 pre-check (robot): as admin, POST /skills/say/invoke {"text":"Hola, soy C3PO"}. If it returns spanish_tts_unavailable, run `bash ~/c3po/apps/bridge/scripts/install_piper.sh` on the Jetson through the robot_internet.py proxy (do not touch gemm files). Confirm the speech is audible.
- [ ] B8 (Quest): push-to-talk 'hola, ¿cómo estás?'. The panel shows the reply and the robot speaks it. Press PARAR during a turn and nothing is spoken.
- [ ] 6.8 (Quest + console): after a headset conversation, open /chat on the Mac. The conversation is listed under its first transcript, with its tool cards.
- [ ] B5/B7 (Mac + Quest): after scripts/quest_setup.sh, localhost:3001/vr-control loads, logs in and voice works with an admin. A non-admin gets 'Se requiere una cuenta admin'. `lsof -nP -iTCP:3000 -sTCP:LISTEN` shows 127.0.0.1. From another LAN machine, `curl http://<mac-ip>:3000/health` fails.
- [ ] B6 (Mac): `bun apps/back/scripts/create-user.ts you@x "You" --admin` creates an account that logs in on the console and the headset. /signup shows 'registro cerrado'. Review /settings users and ban unknown accounts (backlog 8.5).

_Notes:_ I edited nothing. The plan comes from the feat/autonomy worktree only, after graphify queries and reading the cited lines. The worktree was clean at read time. Baseline: `bun test` over apps/back src/voice, src/agent, mounted.test.ts and safety.test.ts gives 78 pass, 0 fail. I did not run the bridge suite or any web tests.

Suggested order: B1 > B2 > B3 > B4 > B5 > B6 > B7 > B8, safety first. B1 is the real stop fix: once it lands, a spoken stop reaches the robot at the bridge whatever apps/back does, and B2 is left to end the planning and not answer stale speech.

Two items are smaller than the backlog says:

- 6.8 is already done: the headset Chat persists through /agent and the console lists every chat.
- 6.1 needs no new TTS: Spanish `say` already runs Piper es_AR-daniela to PlayStream at 16 kHz. Only back needs to call it for voice-mode replies.

On 7.3: legacy voice is already bounded and realtime keeps its context on OpenAI's side, so the unbounded path is /agent (headset and console). B3 also covers half of 11.9 ("runAgentChat really calls done").

Every change keeps the invariants:

- PARAR is not weakened; B1 adds a second stop path.
- The cmd_vel gate is untouched.
- T2 confirmation is untouched; `say` is T0.
- No gemm files are touched.

Run `graphify update` on the worktree after each change.

Left out and not checked:

- PARAR from the console does not cut speech already playing; stop_everything never calls stop_play. A small follow-up.
- The unused polling path in VoiceLoop is left alone.
- /agent and /voice still run the auth check twice per request, because they sit inside the outer guard (the double check mounted.test.ts warns about).
- I did not check that importing auth.ts works without a database in the test environment (B6 test) or how the console Chat shows the 403 body (B7).
- Piper's presence on the robot after the reflash is unknown.

#### 🟢 C1: PARAR works before hydration: a no-JS <form action> fallback `S`

- **Backlog:** 1.9 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/lib/components/stop-button.svelte`, `apps/web/src/routes/(protected)/stop/+page.server.ts`, `apps/web/src/routes/(protected)/stop/+page.svelte`, `apps/web/tests/estop.test.ts`
- **Change:** stop-button.svelte: wrap the button in `<form method="POST" action="/stop" style="display:contents">` and change it to type="submit". press(e) calls e.preventDefault() FIRST, before the `if (estop.busy) return` guard, so a hydrated click (or a busy one) never navigates. That matters most on /vr-control, where a navigation would end the XR session. Both mounts (dashboard-topbar.svelte:105 and vr-control/+page.svelte) get the form for free. New (protected)/stop/+page.server.ts with a single `actions.default`: `createApi(fetch, request.headers.get('cookie')).skills({ name: 'stop_everything' }).invoke.post({}, { fetch: { signal: AbortSignal.timeout(STOP_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS) } })`, wrapped in .catch exactly like (protected)/+layout.server.ts:42-44. The result goes through the existing classifyStopResponse (estop.ts:118), and the action returns `{ phase, code }`. The cookie is forwarded explicitly, the same pattern as the layout load. New +page.svelte renders estopMessage(form.phase, form.code) (estop.ts:337) inside the existing Callout, with its own <h1> and a link back. On a plain GET it explains that the stop lives in the topbar. No new helper: classification and copy are reused as they are. SvelteKit's default CSRF origin check stays on.
- **Tests:** Add source-contract tests to tests/estop.test.ts, in the readFileSync pattern of vr-headset-menu.test.ts: stop-button.svelte has the form with action "/stop" and type="submit", and press() calls preventDefault before the busy check; stop/+page.server.ts invokes 'stop_everything' and goes through classifyStopResponse. The existing estop.test.ts classification tests already cover the outcomes. Keep `bun test` (622 pass) and `bun run check` green.
- **Done when:** With JavaScript disabled in Chrome DevTools, PARAR on /dashboard POSTs /stop, back writes a tool_call_log row (source direct, stop_everything), and /stop shows 'Parando…' (or the right alert when the bridge is down). With JS on, a PARAR click causes no request to /stop and no navigation (Network tab), and the topbar flow behaves as before.
- **Risk:** If preventDefault is missing on a hydrated click, the page navigates, which kills a live XR session on /vr-control. The source test guards this. When the session has expired, a no-JS press is redirected to /login by the layout load and the result is lost (rare: pre-hydration and a lapsed session at once).
- _apply (minor)_: The server action, written as `.catch(() => ...)` like the layout, turns a fetch aborted by AbortSignal.timeout into `{threw:true}`, which is 'unreachable' ('the order did not go out'). The store explicitly reclassifies an aborted signal as noAnswer (estop.svelte.ts:97-106), because the order may have landed. Separately, estopMessage('accepted') says 'Esperando que la telemetría lo confirme', and without JS nothing will ever confirm it. Also, dashboard-topbar mounts StopButton at line 87, not 105. **Fix:** Mirror the store: `.catch(() => (signal.aborted ? {timedOut:true} : {threw:true}))`, then `if (signal.aborted && phase !== 'accepted') phase = 'noAnswer'`. On /stop, add one fixed line under an accepted result: 'Sin JavaScript esta página no puede confirmarlo: mirá el robot.'
- _apply (minor)_: SvelteKit runs actions BEFORE the (protected) layout load, so the layout's `if (!locals.user) redirect` does not guard the action. Auth then rests on back alone. That is correct today (back's auth guard, skills.ts:76), but the plan does not say so. **Fix:** Add a one-line comment in +page.server.ts saying that back's session guard is the authority and that a 401 classifies as 'expired'. No extra check is needed.

#### 🟢 C2: Battery warning under the topbar: warn under 25 %, strong alert under 15 % `S`

- **Backlog:** 1.10 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/lib/robot/format.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/components/shell/battery-banner.svelte`, `apps/web/src/routes/(protected)/+layout.svelte`, `apps/web/tests/robot-format.test.ts`
- **Change:** format.ts: add a pure `batteryWarning(pct: number | null, simulated: boolean): 'warn' | 'alert' | null`. It returns null for null, NaN or simulated readings, 'alert' below 15 and 'warn' below 25. New shell/battery-banner.svelte reads getRobotLive() the way robot-status.svelte does: only a current reading (`online ? batteryPct(...) : null`), and none from the stub. It renders the existing Callout: tone warn for 'warn', tone danger with live=true for 'alert' (role=alert). The Spanish copy names the percentage and the action: cargar ya; under 15 %, sentar o colgar el robot porque puede apagarse y caer. Mount it in (protected)/+layout.svelte right after <EstopBanner />. The chip's colour bands are unchanged (see decision).
- **Tests:** tests/robot-format.test.ts gets a batteryWarning table: null, NaN, 30 -> null; 24.9 -> warn; 15 -> warn; 14 -> alert; any value with simulated -> null. Existing batteryLevel and classifyBattery tests stay green.
- **Done when:** On the real robot at 23 % the console shows an amber banner on every page. At 12 % it shows a red role=alert banner. Neither shows offline, on the stub, or on Isaac (battery_pct null).
- **Risk:** The banner flickers when the reading bounces across 25 or 15, and the alert is announced again each time. That is low risk because SoC is an integer and falls while discharging. No hysteresis on purpose (shortcut comment). Thresholds are a user decision.
- _apply (minor)_: Callout with live=true is role=status (warn) or role=alert (danger), and the plan puts the percentage in the text. Every 1 % drop then re-announces the banner, assertively below 15 %. robot-status.svelte:18-22 records removing exactly this chattiness. Also, the banner feeds batteryPct (which rounds) into batteryWarning, so 24.6 shows no warning while the unit test asserts 24.9 -> warn. **Fix:** Keep the band word in the live title ('Batería baja' / 'Batería crítica') and render the percentage outside the live region, or key the Callout on the band so it only re-renders on a band change. Pass the raw battery_pct to batteryWarning.

#### 🟢 C3: XR camera texture: upload once per new picture, not per eye per frame `S`

- **Backlog:** 6.4 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/lib/webxr/camera-layer.ts`, `apps/web/tests/camera-layer.test.ts`
- **Change:** CameraLayer.draw(opaque, eye, now = performance.now()) gets a third parameter with a default, so drawPerEye's signature in xr-teleop.ts does not change. New fields: `#texHolds: 'img' | 'card' | null` and `#uploadedAt`. Upload the picture only when the texture holds something else, or when `now - #uploadedAt >= UPLOAD_MIN_INTERVAL_MS` (25 ms, at most 40/s, well above the 15 fps feed). Upload the placeholder card only when #ensureCard just repainted it, or when it replaces the picture. Reset `#texHolds = null` in setStreamUrl, in the texImage2D catch, and in dispose(), so a new source or a failed upload is retried on the next draw. The second eye of a frame (now - last < 25) only binds and draws. Rewrite the header note (lines 24-26) that calls per-frame uploads 'cheap enough not to matter'.
- **Tests:** tests/camera-layer.test.ts: the existing 'counts uploads so the page can tell live from frozen' passes explicit times (0, then 30) and still expects 2. New: two draws at the same `now` (two eyes) make one texImage2D call; a draw at +10 ms does not upload, one at +30 ms does; the card with an unchanged reason over three draws makes one RGBA upload, and a setReason change makes a second; card -> picture uploads at once whatever the interval; a throwing upload is retried on the next draw. All other camera-layer tests stay green.
- **Done when:** In a stubbed-GL test at 90 Hz for 1 s with a picture, texImage2D runs <= 40 times (it was 180). On the Quest, the picture still updates and the no-signal card still appears and changes text.
- **Risk:** If the feed ever runs above 40 fps, the headset caps it at 40. A missed card repaint would show an old reason; the 'repaints when the reason changes' test covers that.
- _apply (minor)_: The approach is sound. The existing test 'a real frame takes over' (camera-layer.test.ts:697-716) draws again with the implicit `now`. Under the throttle that draw does not upload, which is fine because the test only checks canvas texts. But it and other draw-twice tests now depend on wall-clock timing, which is a flake risk if the CI machine stalls for 25 ms between two calls. **Fix:** Pass an explicit `now` in every existing test that draws more than once, not only in 'counts uploads'.

#### 🟢 C4: VR push-to-talk on an AudioWorklet instead of ScriptProcessor `S`

- **Backlog:** 6.2 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/lib/robot/mic-capture.worklet.js`, `apps/web/src/lib/robot/vr-voice.svelte.ts`, `apps/web/tests/vr-voice.test.ts`
- **Change:** New plain-JS worklet, mic-capture.worklet.js, about 12 lines: registerProcessor('mic-capture', class extends AudioWorkletProcessor). It keeps a boolean set by port.onmessage, posts `inputs[0][0].slice()` (a copy, because the engine reuses the array) only while recording, and always returns true. vr-voice.svelte.ts: `import workletUrl from './mic-capture.worklet.js?url'`. This is Vite's documented worklet pattern: a hashed asset in the build, served as-is in dev. enableMic() builds `new AudioContext()` FIRST, synchronously inside the tap, then awaits getUserMedia, then `await ctx.audioWorklet.addModule(workletUrl)`, then `new AudioWorkletNode(ctx, 'mic-capture')`, connected source -> node -> ctx.destination (the output stays silent; connecting keeps Chromium pulling it, the same reason the old code connected the ScriptProcessor). port.onmessage pushes chunks and keeps the MAX_TALK_S check. #begin posts true; #finish and cancelTalk post false. On any failure after getUserMedia, stop the tracks and close the context, so a stream is never leaked. destroy() disconnects the node. Delete the `shortcut:` comment and the #proc field.
- **Tests:** tests/vr-voice.test.ts: stub globalThis.AudioWorkletProcessor (with a port) and globalThis.registerProcessor, dynamic-import the worklet, then check: it posts nothing while idle; after `true` each process() posts a copy (mutating the input afterwards does not change what was posted); after `false` it stops; process() always returns true. Source contract: vr-voice.svelte.ts no longer contains 'createScriptProcessor', and `new AudioContext(` appears before `getUserMedia`. The WAV tests stay green. Run `bun run check` so the `?url` import type-checks; vite/client types come through SvelteKit.
- **Done when:** On Chromium desktop, «Activar micrófono» then a B/Y hold (keyboard stand-in) records, encodes and transcribes as before. There is no ScriptProcessor deprecation warning in the console, and `bun test` and `svelte-check` are green.
- **Risk:** Chromium could fail to process a worklet that is not connected to the destination, so it stays connected. AudioWorklet needs a secure context: localhost through adb reverse is fine (quest_setup.sh:12-16), and http://<mac-ip> fails, which already holds for getUserMedia. 375 small messages/s, but only while B/Y is held.
- _apply (major)_: `bun run check` will fail. SvelteKit's generated tsconfig turns on allowJs and checkJs (node_modules/@sveltejs/kit/src/core/sync/write_tsconfig/utils.js:24-25), apps/web/tsconfig.json includes `src` and `tests`, and lib.dom.d.ts declares neither AudioWorkletProcessor nor registerProcessor (checked in the TS 7 lib). A plain .js worklet under src/lib is type-checked and fails with 'Cannot find name'. The tests also run under svelte-check, against the minimal bun-test.d.ts shim. **Fix:** Put `// @ts-nocheck` on the worklet's first line, with a one-line reason (AudioWorkletGlobalScope has no TS lib). In the test, stub the globals through `(globalThis as any)`. The `?url` import itself is fine: @sveltejs/kit/types/index.d.ts references vite/client.
- _apply (major)_: The worklet as described posts `inputs[0][0].slice()`. Chromium passes an input with zero channels (inputs[0] is []) before the MediaStream delivers audio, and after the track ends or is muted. An exception in process() kills the processor for good (processorerror), and from then on capture fails silently and every utterance reads as 'Muy corto'. **Fix:** `const ch = inputs[0]?.[0]; if (this.on && ch) this.port.postMessage(ch.slice()); return true;`. Add a test that process([[]]) with recording on neither throws nor posts, and returns true.
- _apply (minor)_: Two gaps. Port messages already in flight when #finish posts false arrive after the clip has been concatenated. And because the AudioContext is now created before getUserMedia, a denied permission leaves it open. The plan only closes it 'on any failure after getUserMedia'. **Fix:** Keep the `if (!this.#recording) return` guard in port.onmessage. Close the context in the catch block too, including when getUserMedia itself rejects.

#### 🟢 C5: Mic lifecycle in the headset: resume on the «Entrar en VR» tap, drop the clip on visible-blurred, recover from an ended track `S`

- **Backlog:** 6.2 · **Depends on:** C4
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/lib/robot/vr-voice.svelte.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/webxr/xr-teleop.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/robot/vr-control.svelte.ts`, `apps/web/src/routes/(protected)/vr-control/+page.svelte`, `apps/web/tests/vr-session-lifecycle.test.ts`
- **Change:** vr-voice.svelte.ts: add `prime(): void { void this.#ctx?.resume(); }`. Add `track.onended`, which calls the same teardown destroy() uses for the mic (stop tracks, close the context) and sets micReady=false, so the 2D button comes back; that keeps one stream. vr-control/+page.svelte: VrHero onenter calls voice.prime() synchronously, then ctl.enterVr(...), so the resume rides the same user activation that requestSession needs (xr-teleop.ts:806). xr-teleop.ts: XrTeleopCallbacks (line 179) gets `onVisibility?: (state: XRVisibilityState) => void`, and #start registers `session.addEventListener('visibilitychange', ...)` next to 'end' (line 896). vr-control.svelte.ts #startVrSession: `onVisibility: (v) => { if (v !== 'visible') this.releaseAllControls(); }`. releaseAllControls already cancels the talk and a held walk, and keeps the grips so PARAR still fires (lines 774-799).
- **Tests:** tests/vr-session-lifecycle.test.ts source contracts: xr-teleop registers 'visibilitychange' on the XR session and forwards session.visibilityState; vr-control's onVisibility calls releaseAllControls for anything but 'visible'; the page calls voice.prime() before ctl.enterVr in the same handler; vr-voice registers an 'ended' handler on the audio track. The existing lifecycle and menu tests stay green.
- **Done when:** In Quest Browser: hold B, press the Meta button (system menu, visible-blurred), come back. No utterance is sent, and the panel is back at idle. The AudioContext reads 'running' inside VR on the first B/Y press after entering, so there is no 'Muy corto' from a suspended context.
- **Risk:** releaseAllControls also stops a held walk on visible-blurred. That is intended: the operator cannot see the controls. Whether Quest fires visibilitychange for every system overlay is only known on the headset.
- _apply (major)_: Under visible-blurred, head-yaw steering and the arm mirror keep running. xr-teleop.ts onFrame (about lines 984-994) calls onSample on every frame whatever session.visibilityState is, so #lastYawSampleAt stays fresh. #computeVyaw (vr-control.svelte.ts:802) and buildFrame (stream.ts:166-185) then keep deriving `enabled` from yaw and `arms` from armsRequested. releaseAllControls (774-799) only cancels the talk and the walk. So the operator opens the Meta menu, looks across the system panels, and the robot turns. The plan claims to handle 6.2 'visible-blurred' but closes only the push-to-talk half. **Fix:** In onFrame, skip onSample when `active.visibilityState !== 'visible'` (draw first, as now). In onVisibility, also set #lastYawSampleAt = 0 so yaw and arms go to zero at once, not 800 ms later (VR_STALE_MS). The HTTP loop and the stream both stop through their existing freshness gates. Add a test: a fake session with visibilityState 'visible-blurred' produces no onSample. Keep releaseAllControls for the talk and the walk.
- _apply (minor)_: track.onended is told to call 'the same teardown destroy() uses', but destroy() also calls #chat.stop(), which would abort a running agent turn because the mic dropped. Also, the premise that the context is suspended is unverified: Chromium's autoplay rule uses sticky activation, and the context is created after the «Activar micrófono» tap. prime() is harmless, but the acceptance should measure the state, not assume it. **Fix:** Factor out a #releaseMic() (disconnect the node, stop the tracks, close the context, micReady=false, cancelTalk()) and use it in both onended and destroy(). Log ctx.state on the first #begin in VR, as evidence.

#### 🟢 C6: The headset PARAR also goes straight to the bridge over the teleop socket `M`

- **Backlog:** 11.3 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/bridge/src/bridge/teleop/protocol.py`, `apps/bridge/src/bridge/teleop/server.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_teleop_protocol.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_teleop_estop.py`, `apps/web/src/lib/teleop/stream.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/robot/vr-control.svelte.ts`, `apps/web/tests/stream.test.ts`, `apps/web/tests/vr-headset-menu.test.ts`
- **Change:** protocol.py: an optional v1 field `estop`, parsed as `payload.get('estop') is True` and appended as `estop: bool = False` on TeleopFrame; document it in the module docstring. It stays v1 on purpose: a version bump would make an old bridge refuse every frame, while an old bridge ignoring the field still gets the stop through back. server.py: TeleopSession.raise_stop() does `self._seen_stop_at = signal_stop()` (the bridge-wide sentinel, so GovernedMCP refuses T1/T2, _locomotion.send_velocity:128 zeroes velocity, and arm_sdk.engage:335 refuses), `self.stopped = True`, `self._release_since = None`, and logs teleop.estop source=headset. ingest() calls it BEFORE the seq ordering check, because a stop is never stale. In handle_client's FrameError branch, a size-capped JSON whose top-level estop is literally true still raises the stop. The next 20 Hz tick sends zero velocity and request_release() freezes the arms through the existing wants_motion path. Clearing is unchanged: the session clears with the dwell, the robot only with /safety/ack. stream.ts: TeleopHandle gets `stop()`, which sends `{v, seq: seq++, t, ...idlePayload(), estop: true}` at once when the socket is OPEN, ignoring the bufferedAmount skip, and does nothing otherwise; TeleopFramePayload gets `estop?: boolean`. vr-control.svelte.ts: #haltForEstop() (line 597) calls `this.#teleop?.stop()`, which covers the grip chord, the overlay button and Shift+Space, since every press goes 'sending' and estopHalts fires (vr-logic.ts:437). Remove the shortcut comment at line 1166.
- **Tests:** Bridge, test_teleop_protocol.py: estop true parses; missing, false or "true" (a string) read as no stop. test_teleop_estop.py, reusing the frame(), session and sent fixtures: an estop frame makes stop_is_standing() True and session.stopped True, and the next _dispatch_once sends (0,0,0,STAND_HEIGHT); an estop frame with an old seq is still honoured; a malformed frame carrying estop:true still stops; the dwell clears only the session latch and stop_is_standing() stays True until record_ack(). Web, stream.test.ts with the FakeSocket: stop() sends one frame with estop:true at once even when bufferedAmount is over 4096, and is a no-op on a closed socket. vr-headset-menu.test.ts: #haltForEstop's body contains `#teleop?.stop()`. Suites: bridge `uv run pytest` (teleop subset 73 pass today), web `bun test`.
- **Done when:** With the stream connected and apps/back stopped, the grip chord stops head-yaw/stick motion within one dispatch tick (bridge log teleop.estop source=headset). ~/.c3po/run/stop_everything is touched, and after restarting back the console shows PARAR ACTIVO until Reanudar.
- **Risk:** This is a partial stop. In the MCP process, running gestures and arm skills are not cancelled until back's stop_everything lands (see decision). Without the stream there is no direct path: the shortcut comment moves to stream.ts saying so. The socket has no auth yet (1.5), so any LAN client could raise PARAR; that is the safe direction. While back is down the console says 'La orden de parada no salió' even though the teleop stop went out; changing that copy is a follow-up.
- _apply (minor)_: The plan says `_locomotion.send_velocity:128 zeroes velocity` while the sentinel stands. That check sits inside `if SIM_MODE == "real"` (_locomotion.py:116-133). On sim and Isaac, an MCP walk keeps moving after a teleop-raised stop, and the teleop session itself drives again after the 1 s dwell even though stop_is_standing() is True. Also, connectTeleop's early-return handle `{ close: () => {} }` (stream.ts:221) needs `stop` too. And the FrameError re-parse is close to dead code: the stop frame is built from idlePayload() and always parses. **Fix:** Scope the acceptance to real hardware and note the sim gap (it predates this change and also holds for the console's PARAR). Add `stop: () => {}` to the early-return handle. Treat the FrameError fallback as optional; if kept, skip it when the error was the size cap, as planned.

#### 🟢 C7: Teleop lease only while the headset is actually driving, and agent walks yield when it takes over `S`

- **Backlog:** 11.6 · **Depends on:** C6
- **Files:** `apps/bridge/src/bridge/teleop/server.py`, `apps/bridge/src/bridge/skills/walk_to.py`, `apps/bridge/src/bridge/skills/turn.py`, `apps/bridge/src/bridge/estop.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_teleop_lease.py`
- **Change:** server.py _dispatch_once: drop the unconditional renew_teleop_lease() at line 553. After `commanding` is final (after the deadman zeroing), `if commanding or get_driver().engaged: renew_teleop_lease()`; on the falling edge (session.moving and not commanding, and arms not engaged), release_teleop_lease(), so an agent call right after the operator lets go is not refused for the 2 s TTL. The handle_client finally (line 830) still releases. walk_to.py loop (lines 134-177) and turn.py loop (lines 131-170): each iteration checks `teleop_conflict()` and on a hit ends with status 'failed', phase 'teleop_takeover' and an error that says the operator took over (do not retry). It skips stop_motion, because the headset is already sending velocity and a zero burst would stutter its command. go_to_object inherits this through walk_to. walk_velocity keeps its start-time check; its walks are bounded at 3 s (MAX_DURATION_S, walk_velocity.py:53). estop.py: update the lease comment ('renewed while a session commands motion or holds the arms'). Invariants untouched: PARAR, the GovernedMCP tiers and the cmd_vel gate.
- **Tests:** test_teleop_lease.py, with the test_teleop_estop fixtures: a connected idle session (enabled:false) does not hold the lease after _dispatch_once; walk=1 holds it; letting go releases it on that tick; an engaged arm driver (fake with engaged=True) holds it; walk_to whose fake send renews the lease on its 3rd setpoint returns phase 'teleop_takeover' and sends no zero afterwards; the same for turn. The existing refuse-at-start tests (test_teleop_lease.py:80-111) and test_teleop_estop/test_teleop_server/test_walk_to stay green.
- **Done when:** With the stream connected and nobody touching anything, go_to_object('silla') from the headset's voice walks. Pushing the stick mid-walk ends the walk with teleop_takeover and the headset drives. Within ~0.1 s of letting go, a new agent walk is accepted.
- **Risk:** For up to one walk_to loop period after the operator takes over, both write velocity. In this transport the head yaw alone counts as driving, so looking around kills an agent walk unless C8 is done. Keeping the lease while the arms are engaged keeps agent walks off a body whose arms the headset holds (arm weight 1.0 in mode 801 ignores velocity, per BACKLOG §12).
- _apply (major)_: The plan skips stop_motion when a walk ends with teleop_takeover. That is unsafe when the takeover comes from the arm mirror: the plan renews the lease on get_driver().engaged even at zero velocity, and the teleop server sends velocity only while commanding or on a falling edge (server.py:575-577). In that case nobody zeroes the agent's last setpoint. On real hardware the robot keeps that setpoint for VELOCITY_DURATION_S = 1.0 s (_locomotion.py:56) while arm_sdk ramps the weight up, which is the mode-801 shuffle that BACKLOG §12 names. On sim it holds the velocity with no limit (walk_to.py:226-229 says so). A head flick that crosses the deadzone for a single tick ends the same way. **Fix:** On takeover keep the existing `task.phase = "stopping"; await stop_motion(height)` in walk_to.py and turn.py, and only add the phase and error. A 0.4 s zero burst mixed into the headset's 20 Hz commands is a stutter, which is the safe direction. Change the test to expect stop_motion to be called.
- _apply (minor)_: Three smaller gaps. teleop_conflict()'s text (_locomotion.py:251-256) tells the model and the operator to 'disconnect the headset stream', which is wrong once idle no longer holds the lease. The new phase 'teleop_takeover' has no entry in PHASE_TEXT (apps/web/src/lib/robot/skill-outcome.ts:61), and that file is not in the plan. And the page's precautionary 0.5 s zero pulse on stream open (vr-control.svelte.ts, connectStream onState 'open') used to be refused through the lease and will now run as a walk_velocity task. It is harmless, but it is logged as a walk command, and the 'means us' comment at vr-logic.ts:375 becomes stale. **Fix:** Reword the conflict message to 'the operator is driving right now; wait until they let go'. Add `teleop_takeover: "El operador del visor tomó el control"` to PHASE_TEXT, with a skill-outcome test row. Update the vr-logic.ts:375 comment.

#### 🟢 C8: (If decision D4 = b) The headset's head yaw does not steer while its own voice turn is running `S`

- **Backlog:** 11.6 · **Depends on:** C7
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/lib/robot/vr-voice.svelte.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/robot/vr-control.svelte.ts`, `apps/web/src/routes/(protected)/vr-control/+page.svelte`, `apps/web/tests/vr-headset-menu.test.ts`
- **Change:** vr-voice.svelte.ts: expose `get busy()` (the existing private #busy: chat submitted or streaming, which spans the tool call). VrControlOptions.voice (vr-control.svelte.ts:130) gets `busy: () => boolean`, and the page passes `() => voice.busy`. In vr-control, #computeVyaw (line 802, the HTTP loop) returns 0 while busy, and #buildTeleopFrame (line 1402) passes yawErrorRadians 0 while busy, so the stream's `enabled` only comes from walk input or arms. The re-arm check at line 1028 keeps reading the real yaw. Stick or walk buttons still take over (C7).
- **Tests:** Source contracts in vr-headset-menu.test.ts: #computeVyaw and #buildTeleopFrame read the voice busy flag; the re-arm check does not. tests/stream.test.ts already proves buildFrame with yaw 0 and walking null gives enabled:false.
- **Done when:** With the headset, «andá hasta la silla», then looking left and right during the walk: the robot keeps walking to the chair. Pushing the stick takes over.
- **Risk:** While busy, head.yaw is sent as 0, so arm retargeting (which uses head yaw) is off if the arms are mirrored during an agent walk, a combination never tested. A console-chat agent walk is not covered, and there head yaw still takes over.
- _apply (major)_: When the voice turn ends, `busy` goes false and head-yaw steering resumes at once at whatever angle the head is at. If the operator is looking 30 degrees left when go_to_object returns, the next stream frame carries enabled:true and the robot turns at the full 0.30 rad/s with no deliberate act. Every other re-arm in this code needs the head back at centre first (vr-control.svelte.ts:1022-1031, and the dwell in check_estop). **Fix:** On busy's falling edge, latch yaw steering off until |#yawErrorRadians| < YAW_DEADZONE_RAD, either with the same rule as the deadManTripped re-arm or with a #yawNeedsCentre flag read in both #computeVyaw and #buildTeleopFrame. Add a test: busy true->false with yaw at 0.5 rad gives yaw 0 until the head is centred. Also document that busy covers every voice turn, questions included.

**Critic on WP C** (verdict: needs_changes)

- _missing:_ The headset operator never sees the C2 banner: the WebXR dom-overlay shows only overlayRoot, and the banner sits in the layout. vr-control already pushes a menuAlert into the headset (vr-control.svelte.ts:331, 628, 1346), so a battery band there is a few lines and covers the operator most likely to be driving when the battery dies.
- _missing:_ C5 should state that visible-blurred also freezes inputSources: grips read 0 (the PARAR chord cannot fire) and hands go untracked, while armsRequested stays true. That is why the sample gate in the C5 issue is needed, not only releaseAllControls.
- _missing:_ Run a baseline `bun run check` (svelte-check --tsgo) before C4. The plan skipped it, and checkJs makes it fail as designed.
- _missing:_ C7 should list apps/web/src/lib/robot/skill-outcome.ts and tests/skill-outcome.test.ts, for the teleop_takeover copy.
- _missing:_ C8 must define what happens to the re-arm and recentre when busy ends (see the C8 issue). Decision D4 option b is only safe with that rule.
- _missing:_ The baseline holds: I ran the bridge teleop subset (test_teleop_estop, test_teleop_lease, test_teleop_protocol, test_teleop_server) read-only: 73 passed, as the plan says.
- _missing:_ Verified as correct, with no change needed: SvelteKit 3.0.1 still supports form actions (runtime/server/page/actions.js), and its CSRF origin check runs only in production builds. The /vr-control StopButton renders only while vrActive (+page.svelte:216-226), so the no-JS form only matters for the topbar. engage() sets _engaged immediately (arm_sdk.py:354), so the C7 lease covers the arm ramp-up. Idle teleop sessions send no zero velocity (server.py:572-577), so freeing the lease does not make the stream fight agent walks.

**Robot/headset checks for WP C:**

- [ ] C1 (no robot motion needed, bridge in real mode on the gantry): in Chrome DevTools disable JavaScript, open /dashboard, press PARAR. /stop shows 'Parando…', bridge log shows stop_everything, the stop sentinel is touched. Re-enable JS: the StopLatchChip shows PARAR ACTIVO. Reanudar clears it.
- [ ] C2: during a normal session, watch the battery cross 25 % (amber banner on every page) and 15 % (red alert). Confirm neither shows on the stub or with the link down.
- [ ] C3 on the Quest: enter VR with the vendor 1080p feed and then the :8081 vision feed. The picture keeps updating, judder is gone (OVR Metrics Tool or chrome://inspect frame timing before and after), and the no-signal card still appears and changes text when the feed dies.
- [ ] C4/C5 on the Quest: «Activar micrófono», «Entrar en VR», hold B, speak, release: transcription arrives. Do 10 utterances in a row with a single mic indicator. While holding B press the Meta button and come back: nothing is sent and the panel is idle. Check in chrome://inspect that the AudioContext state is 'running' on the first press.
- [ ] C6 on the gantry: connect the stream, drive by head yaw, stop apps/back (Ctrl-C), grip chord. The robot stops within ~50 ms and the bridge logs teleop.estop source=headset. Restart back: the console shows PARAR ACTIVO. The headset cannot drive again until the dwell release plus Reanudar.
- [ ] C7/C8 in the room, robot on a long cable: stream connected and idle, say «andá hasta la silla». go_to_object walks. Look left and right during the walk: it keeps going (with C8). Push the stick: the walk ends with teleop_takeover and the headset drives. Release, ask again: accepted at once.

_Notes:_ Baselines I ran read-only: apps/web `bun test` gives 622 pass / 0 fail across 28 files. The bridge teleop subset (test_teleop_estop, test_teleop_lease, test_teleop_protocol, test_teleop_server) gives 73 pass. I reproduced 11.6 with an inline script, with C3PO_RUN_DIR pointed at the scratchpad folder /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-oliverjones-Developer-ORT---TIC-5th-year---TIC-Proyecto-Final-c3po/94d93d4c-23d3-4a2b-8ae9-22bdde8bdba8/scratchpad/run, so the robot's run dir was never touched.

Order: C1, C2 and C3 are independent and can ship in parallel. C4 comes before C5 (same file). C6 comes before C7 (both edit teleop/server.py). C8 only if decision 4 = b.

Every change is additive to the stop path. PARAR is never weakened, the cmd_vel gate is not touched, T2 is unchanged, and no gemm files are involved. C6 adds a second writer of the stop sentinel, which estop.py's docstring already allows ('anything may signal a stop'). C6 and C7 change apps/bridge and have to be pushed together with the web change (bridge_sync). An old teleop server ignores the estop field, and the back path still stops.

Cross-package notes:

- The 1.10 refusal of long tasks and arm use under 20 % is bridge work.
- The decision-3 watcher overlaps 11.2 (an in-process stop flag).
- When 1.5 adds auth to the teleop socket, it must still let an authenticated headset send estop.
- In the HTTP (no-stream) VR mode, the page's head-yaw walk_velocity and an agent walk_to can still both write velocity. C8 covers it during headset voice turns only; there is no lease on that path. Worth a backlog line.

After the code changes, run `graphify update` on the project root (AGENTS.md). The graph is stale for vr-voice: graphify query found no node for it.

What I skipped or did not check: svelte-check/`?url` typing, whether Chromium processes an AudioWorkletNode that is not connected, and when Quest actually fires XRSession visibilitychange. I did not read menu-layer.ts to see how a headset battery line would fit. Risk to know: C6 is a partial stop in the MCP process (running gestures and arm skills keep going until back's stop lands), and C7 without C8 makes head movement cancel every voice-started walk.

#### 🟢 D1: Persistent ~/.c3po/perception.env for every perception bring-up and build `S`

- **Backlog:** 3.3 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/perception/bringup/spec.py`, `apps/perception/bringup/cli.py`, `apps/perception/bringup/build.py`, `scripts/robot/c3po`, `apps/perception/tests/test_bringup_cli.py`, `apps/perception/README.md`, `docs/autonomy/test-day/perception.md`
- **Change:** spec.py: add PERSISTENT_ENV_KEYS = VISION_ENV_PASSTHROUGH + ("C3PO_LIDAR_SOURCE",) next to VISION_ENV_PASSTHROUGH, with a one-line reason: an explicit list, so a persisted C3PO_TAKE_OVER=1, C3PO_VISION_FAKE=1 or C3PO_NAV_* never becomes a silent default. cli.py: add read_env_file(path) -> Dict[str,str]. It is stdlib and 3.8-valid: shlex.split(line, comments=True) per line, drops a leading 'export', and accepts one KEY=VALUE word. A missing file returns {}. A malformed line raises ValueError naming the line number. Add operator_env(environ) -> Dict[str,str] = {**allowlisted keys from $HOME/.c3po/perception.env, **environ}, so the shell still wins; keys outside the list are dropped with warn(). main() passes env=operator_env(os.environ) into bring_up, info()s the keys taken from the file, and returns 2 on a ValueError before anything runs. build.py main(): run_build(..., env=operator_env(os.environ)) so `c3po perception build engine` builds the persisted model. scripts/robot/c3po usage(): one line naming the file. Docs: replace 'export ... before c3po up' (perception.md §2, §4, §5 and README ~412) with the file.
- **Tests:** test_bringup_cli.py, new cases: (1) a file value reaches the vision container's -e flags (bring_up dry_run with env=operator_env(...), HOME=tmp_path); (2) a shell export beats the file; (3) a missing file gives {}; (4) `export C3PO_CAMERA_PITCH_DEG=47.6  # URDF` and a quoted path parse; (5) an unbalanced quote makes main return 2 and names the line, and docker is never touched; (6) C3PO_TAKE_OVER=1 / C3PO_VISION_FAKE=1 in the file are ignored with a warning; (7) C3PO_LIDAR_SOURCE=driver from the file reaches lidar_source:=driver. Existing test_bringup_spec.py:205-224, test_bringup_build.py and the rest of the suite (315 pass today) stay green; ruff py38 stays clean.
- **Done when:** With no C3PO_* exported, `c3po perception up perception --dry-run` on a host whose ~/.c3po/perception.env holds C3PO_ONNX/C3PO_ENGINE/C3PO_LABELS prints -e flags for all three. `C3PO_LABELS=x c3po perception up perception --dry-run` prints x. A typo line refuses with its line number. Ships to the robot with a git pull, no image rebuild.
- **Risk:** Low. shlex cuts a '#' inside an unquoted word (A=b#c gives b), where bash keeps it. Perception values are paths and numbers, so mark it `shortcut:` and quote if ever needed. bridge/env_file.py already parses this correctly, but bringup must stay stdlib/3.8 and independent of apps/bridge, so it is not imported. Allowlisting means someone who puts C3PO_NAV_SLAM in the file is told it was ignored, which is intended.

#### 🟢 D2: Default camera extrinsic derived from the URDF d435_joint, pinned to the bridge's kinematics by a test `S`

- **Backlog:** 3.1 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/perception/vision/c3po_vision/grounding.py`, `apps/perception/vision/c3po_vision/detector.py`, `apps/perception/tests/test_grounding.py`, `apps/perception/README.md`, `docs/autonomy/test-day/perception.md`, `docs/autonomy/test-day/manipulation.md`
- **Change:** grounding.py: DEFAULT_CAMERA_EXTRINSIC = CameraExtrinsic(x_m=0.05366, y_m=0.01753, z_m=0.47387, pitch_deg=47.6, yaw_deg=0.0). The comment above it gives the derivation: pelvis -> waist_yaw(0) -> waist_roll_joint xyz(-0.0039635,0,0.044) -> waist_pitch(0) -> torso_link -> d435_joint xyz(0.0576235,0.01753,0.42987) rpy(0,0.8307767,0)=47.6 deg, from apps/bridge/src/bridge/assets/g1_29dof_rev_1_0.urdf. It is CAD, not a tape measure, and C3PO_CAMERA_* still override it. Frame: base_link here is the URDF root `pelvis`, which is what the nav container's base_link stands for (g1_odom_tf.py:86-88). The D435 rides torso_link above the three waist joints, so the constant is exact only at waist (0,0,0). Waist yaw adds straight onto every bearing, waist pitch adds onto the pitch and moves the lens about 4 cm per 0.1 rad, and gait pitch/roll of the pelvis is not modelled. Write that as a `shortcut:` comment (upgrade: feed waist_q from rt/lowstate if the robot shows bearing drift with the waist off zero). The bridge's arm path is unaffected: kinematics.camera_to_torso already uses torso_link. Update the CameraExtrinsic docstring frame line and the module's 'MEASURE IT' paragraph (grounding.py:80-92). detector.py: reword the camera_extrinsic_from_env docstring, and change the start log (detector.py:1075) to extrinsic_source='env' when any C3PO_CAMERA_* is set, else 'URDF d435_joint (CAD)'. Docs: README Stage 6 line ~289, perception.md §4 'Camera pitch' and manipulation.md §6 now say verify rather than set 47.6.
- **Tests:** test_grounding.py, new: (1) DEFAULT_CAMERA_EXTRINSIC equals the composition of bridge.kinematics._path('d435_link', 'pelvis') at zero waist angles (position to 1e-6, pitch to 1e-6 deg, yaw 0, rotation has no roll). bridge src is already on sys.path through conftest.py:74. (2) For 3 optical points, grounding.camera_to_base(p, DEFAULT) equals kinematics.camera_to_torso(p) plus the torso->pelvis offset taken from the URDF, so the vision container and the arm IK agree on where a pixel is. Update the LEVEL_CAM comment (test_grounding.py:64-71): the default is now CAD, and a tape measurement goes in perception.env, not the default. Run apps/perception (uv run pytest) and apps/bridge tests touching kinematics (test_kinematics*/test_manipulation.py) green.
- **Done when:** `uv run pytest` in apps/perception passes with the 2 new tests. A detector started with no C3PO_CAMERA_* logs extrinsic x=0.054 y=0.018 z=0.474 pitch=47.6 source='URDF d435_joint (CAD)'. On the robot, a floor object 1.0 m ahead (tape from the pelvis projection) reports range_m within about 5 cm.
- **Risk:** Shipping needs a vision image rebuild (code is baked into c3po/perception-vision; spec.py:356-394 mounts no source). Until then D1's perception.env carries the same four numbers. Synthetic-mode ranges (SyntheticSource, `fake` stage) shift with the new pitch. Bearing signs do not change and no test pins those ranges. The lateral bias (colour optical origin about 15 mm from d435_link, audit §3.2 low) is kept on purpose so the bridge and vision agree; the robot check decides. The nav container's base_in_body_xyz placeholder [-0.10,0,0.45] disagrees with the URDF pelvis-in-mid360 (0.028,0,0.472) by about 13 cm in x. That belongs to Navigation and is not changed here.

- **Backlog:** 3.2 · **Depends on:** D2
- **Files:** `apps/perception/tests/test_depth_and_tracking.py`
- **Change:** Tests only; the fix is D2's pitch. Add a pitched_scene(surface) helper that ray-casts a 640x480 depth frame (mm, list of rows, no numpy) for the DEFAULT_CAMERA_EXTRINSIC rotation, written out by hand in the test (xb = cos p - yo sin p, zb = -sin p - yo cos p) so it does not reuse camera_to_base. It builds either a horizontal table 0.5 m below the lens or a fronto-parallel wall 1.0 m ahead in base_link. No change to grounding.py.
- **Tests:** New: (1) a glass with no depth (or read through to 3 m) on the table at the real pitch -> ground_box(..., extr=DEFAULT_CAMERA_EXTRINSIC) returns depth_source 'plane' with depth within 3 cm of the ray-cast contact point; (2) the strip under the box is the wall -> the plane is refused (tilt about 90 deg), so the result is None with no own depth, or 'direct' when the glass has its own depth; (3) a regression pin: plane_tilt_deg of the fitted table normal is about 0 with pitch 47.6 and about 47.6 with pitch 0, recording the inversion the audit found. The existing LEVEL_CAM plane tests (76-114) stay as they are and stay green.

- **Risk:** Low. Only risk is a scene helper sign error. Assert the ray-cast table's base z equals -0.5 m for one pixel as a self-check.

#### 🟢 D4: Detector resets a wedged D435 once (VIDIOC_S_FMT errno=5) and retries `S`

- **Backlog:** 3.4 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/perception/vision/c3po_vision/detector.py`, `apps/perception/tests/test_realsense_source.py`
- **Change:** RealSenseSource.start: wrap `profile = self._pipeline.start(config)` (detector.py:272-273). On a RuntimeError whose text contains 'VIDIOC_S_FMT' and matches r'errno=5\b', and only once: log('camera.wedged', error=...). Then devs = rs.context().query_devices(); if none, re-raise the original. Take serial = devs[0].get_info(rs.camera_info.serial_number), call devs[0].hardware_reset(), sleep about 2 s, then poll rs.context().query_devices() for that serial for up to about 15 s. If it is not back, raise RuntimeError naming the serial. Then log('camera.reset', serial=...), build a fresh rs.pipeline() and start once more. A second failure propagates, so the container exits as it does today. Any other error, errno=16 (busy: videohub_pc4 or another process owns the node) included, never resets. Module constant RESET_WAIT_S = 15.0, not env-tunable.
- **Tests:** New test_realsense_source.py: a fake `pyrealsense2` module put in sys.modules with monkeypatch (same pattern as test_nav2_launch_flags.py:35-56), with config/pipeline/align/context/camera_info/stream/format stand-ins and a profile that answers get_device().first_depth_sensor().get_depth_scale() and get_stream().as_video_stream_profile().intrinsics. detector.time.sleep is patched to a no-op. Cases: (1) errno=5 once -> exactly one hardware_reset, a second pipeline started, intrinsics set; (2) errno=16 -> no reset, raises; (3) errno=5 twice -> one reset, then raises; (4) the device never comes back -> raises naming the serial; (5) no device listed -> the original error. The rest of the perception suite stays green, and c3po_vision/**init** stays import-light (pyrealsense2 stays a lazy import inside start).
- **Done when:** All 5 cases pass. On the robot, a wedge after a container restart shows camera.wedged -> camera.reset -> camera.ready in `docker logs c3po-perception-vision` with no manual reset.
- **Risk:** Re-enumeration inside the container depends on the /dev, /dev/bus/usb and /run/udev bind mounts (spec.py:385-393). It is designed for replug but has not been seen to survive a reset in-container, so the robot step must confirm it. Startup can take up to about 17 s longer, and bringup's 20 s port check (cli.py:495-517) only warns. Resetting while another process holds the camera would yank it from them. Matching only errno=5 is what prevents that: keep it narrow. Needs a vision image rebuild to ship.

#### 🟢 D5: Detections carry their capture age through the nav container to the bridge `S`

- **Backlog:** 3.6 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/perception/vision/c3po_vision/detector.py`, `apps/perception/nav/ws/src/c3po_perception/c3po_perception/world_model_publisher.py`, `apps/bridge/src/bridge/skills/manipulation.py`, `apps/perception/tests/test_report_shape.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_manipulation.py`
- **Change:** detector.py:1178: build_report(objects, omitted, now, unranged). `now` is the tick start, taken just before source.read(), so the stamp is the grab, not the publish. Add a `shortcut:` comment: up to one frame period off; use the frameset's global_time timestamp if the robot shows lag. world_model_publisher.py: add self._objects_stamp = 0.0 in **init**. In _on_objects, parse stamp = float(payload.get('stamp_unix') or time.time()) inside the existing try (trust boundary: a bad stamp becomes a note) and store it. Add a module-level pure helper _aged(objects, stamp_unix, now) -> [dict(o, age_s=round(max(0.0, now - stamp_unix), 1)) for o in objects]. _emit uses "objects": _aged(self._objects, self._objects_stamp, now) if detector_online else []. The dict stays a literal, so the AST key test holds, and the report's own stamp_unix stays the emit time (envelope). Bridge, the consumer the audit names: manipulation.detected_target skips an object when age + float(obj.get('age_s') or 0) > FRESH_DETECTION_S. world_model already turns age_s into 'stale' and shows it to the model (world_model.py:79-80, 221).
- **Tests:** test_report_shape.py: load world_model_publisher.py with stubbed rclpy / rclpy.node.Node / rclpy.qos / nav_msgs.msg / sensor_msgs.msg / std_msgs.msg (the sys.modules pattern from test_nav2_launch_flags.py) and test _aged. Cases: age = now - stamp, clamped at 0 for a future stamp, the input dicts not mutated, and the result round-tripping through world_model.from_report to Observation.age_s. test_published_keys_are_exactly_build_kwargs and test_a_stale_detection_is_reported_stale stay green. apps/bridge/tests/test_manipulation.py: a cup with age_s=1.5 in a fresh report is not a grasp target (PlanError), while age_s=0.2 still is. test_stale_or_depthless_detections_are_not_grasp_targets stays green. Run the perception suite and `uv run pytest` in apps/bridge.
- **Done when:** `ros2 topic echo /c3po/world_summary` shows objects with age_s of about 0.1-0.4 while the detector runs, rising toward 1.5 after `docker stop c3po-perception-vision`, then objects [] with detector_online false. `pick` refuses a target re-emitted more than 1 s after capture.
- **Risk:** The detector and nav containers must share the host clock. Both run --network host on the same kernel, so they do, and max(0,..) covers small skew. Shipping needs both a vision and a nav image rebuild (the nav rebuild is already owed, backlog §2 and 4.5); the bridge side ships with a bridge restart. Until the nav image is rebuilt, age_s stays absent and the bridge behaves as today. Importing the publisher with stubbed ROS modules sits against test_report_shape's 'read as source' note. Acceptable because no Node is constructed, only a module-level pure function is called.

In a fresh shell with nothing exported, `c3po perception up perception --dry-run` must show -e C3PO_ONNX/C3PO_ENGINE/C3PO_LABELS. Then `c3po up`, and `docker inspect c3po-perception-vision` shows them. Until the vision image is rebuilt with D2, also put C3PO_CAMERA_PITCH_DEG=47.6 C3PO_CAMERA_X_M=0.0537 C3PO_CAMERA_Y_M=0.0175 C3PO_CAMERA_Z_M=0.4739 in the file, and remove them after the rebuild.

Baseline: apps/perception `uv run pytest` gives 315 passed, 1 skipped, run read-only. Order: D1 first, because the robot can use it after a git pull with no rebuild, and through it the pitch and the YOLOE settings stop being hand-exported today. Then D2 -> D3. D4 and D5 are independent. Rebuilds: D2, D4 and the detector half of D5 need `c3po perception build vision`; the publisher half of D5 needs the nav image rebuild; the bridge half of D5 needs `c3po restart`. Invariants: nothing touches PARAR/estop, the cmd_vel gate or T2. D4 resets the camera only on EIO, never on EBUSY. The perception.env allowlist keeps C3PO_TAKE_OVER (which stops gemm containers) out of persistence. No gemm file is touched. After each code change run `graphify update` on the worktree root (AGENTS.md). Risks you need to know: the shlex parser cuts an unquoted '#' inside a value; whether the camera re-enumerates inside the container after hardware_reset is unverified until the robot test; and the CAD pitch is not a tape measurement.
