#### 🟢 E1: 11.5 (nav side): every object in the world summary carries its age `S`

- **Backlog:** 11.5 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/perception/nav/ws/src/c3po_perception/c3po_perception/world_model_publisher.py`, `apps/perception/tests/conftest.py`, `apps/perception/tests/test_world_model_publisher_age.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_world_model_report.py`
- **Change:** In WorldModelPublisher._on_objects, store self._objects_stamp = float(payload.get('stamp_unix') or time.time()). This is the detector's own stamp. Both containers use --network host on the same kernel clock, so it compares directly with time.time(). Add a module-level pure _aged(objects, stamp, now) returning [dict(o, age_s=round(max(0.0, now - stamp), 2)) for o in objects]. In _emit, use it inside the existing literal dict: "objects": _aged(self._objects, self._objects_stamp, now) if detector_online else []. No schema bump: age_s is already part of the Observation wire shape, and the bridge's world_model._observation_from already reads it. Put a small helper in conftest.py that loads an rclpy-importing nav module with stub rclpy, nav_msgs, sensor_msgs and std_msgs modules, using the pattern from test_nav2_launch_flags._load_launch. E6 reuses it.
- **Tests:** New test_world_model_publisher_age.py tests _aged (age from the detector stamp, clamped at 0, other fields kept). test_report_shape.py must stay green because the key set is unchanged. A bridge test_world_model_report case checks that a report with age_s > 2 s yields Observation.age_s and the existing stale note. Perception suite (315 passed today) and bridge suite stay green.
- **Done when:** On the Mac both suites are green. On the robot, with the detector live, /c3po/world_summary objects show age_s between 0.0 and 0.3. After `docker stop c3po-perception-vision`, age_s climbs toward 1.5 and then the objects drop out with detector_online false.
- **Risk:** Takes effect only after a nav image rebuild, since the package is baked in at Dockerfile:202-216. describe_surroundings starts showing age_s per object, which is a small change in agent-visible text. Overlaps 3.6 (report stamp_unix=now): coordinate so that one change does both.
- _apply (minor)_: Two problems. First, float(payload.get('stamp_unix')) sits outside _on_objects's try (world_model_publisher.py:180-193). A bad stamp raises inside the rclpy callback and can kill the node, which the comment at 184-190 explicitly forbids. A stamp from the future clamps age to 0, so stale objects read as fresh. Second, the stub-rclpy conftest helper goes against the suite's documented convention. **Fix:** Parse and sanity-check the stamp inside the existing try: |now - stamp| < 5 s, otherwise use the receipt time and add a note. Put _aged in a pure module (world_model_publisher already imports the pure scan_ring) and test it without stubs.

#### 🟢 E2: 11.5 (bridge): reject live detections older than 0.5 s and apply the bearing to the pose at capture time `S`

- **Backlog:** 11.5 · **Depends on:** E1
- **Files:** `apps/bridge/src/bridge/sdk/state.py`, `apps/bridge/src/bridge/skills/go_to_object.py`, `apps/bridge/src/bridge/mcp_server.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_state_pose.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_go_to_object.py`
- **Change:** state.py: in _on_odom and _on_sim_state, under the existing lock, append (received_at, x, y, yaw) to a collections.deque(maxlen=1000), about 2 s at 500 Hz. Add StateSampler.pose_at(t) returning the newest sample at or before t as (x, y, yaw), or None when the history is empty or t is older than it. go_to_object.py: LIVE_MAX_AGE_S = 0.5. resolve() gains live_age_s, the report's own age. It keeps a live match only if age_s is present (otherwise per decision 2) and age_s + live_age_s <= 0.5, and returns captured_at = now - total_age for live targets. A match that exists but is too old returns error 'stale_detection' with in_view, telling the model to look again. mcp_server.go_to_object: read live objects from get_link().latest_report(), the raw report as manipulation.detected_target does, instead of surroundings_snapshot()['objects'], because the D7 snapshot loses the difference between absent and zero age. For live targets, world_target uses get_sampler().pose_at(captured_at); memory targets keep using the current pose. Add 'stale_detection' to typical_failure_modes. Per decision 1 there is no turning rule, because the correction removes the error it guarded against.
- **Tests:** test_state_pose: pose_at picks the right sample and returns None when empty or too old, for both the odom and sim callbacks. test_go_to_object: a fresh object is accepted; age 0.6 s gives stale_detection; report age adds to object age; absent age_s follows decision 2. If the robot turned 30 degrees and stepped 0.15 m since capture, world_target from the capture pose gives the same world point as a still robot. test_go_to_object, test_walk_to, test_mcp_catalogue and test_perception_link stay green (69 passed today in the subset).
- **Done when:** On the robot, calling go_to_object right after a `turn` (still yawing at the 0.3 rad/s cap) aims within about 0.2 m of the target computed with the robot still. A detection more than 0.5 s old is refused with stale_detection.
- **Risk:** Under decision 2 'reject', every live target is stale_detection until the nav image with E1 is on the robot, and go_to_object is the demo skill. The append sits in a 500 Hz DDS callback but is tiny. pose_at must use the same clock as received_at (time.time on the Jetson); the containers share it.
- _apply (major)_: Decision 2 makes the verified demo skill depend on a nav image rebuild that is not done (BACKLOG 4.5). The slam_toolbox apt layer (nav/Dockerfile:192) sits before the c3po_perception COPY (202), so that rebuild needs internet, not just the cache. 'Reject' turns every live go_to_object into stale_detection until then. Accepting age-less detections is exactly today's verified behaviour, so it is not a regression. **Fix:** Default to accepting detections that have no age_s, log once per call, and keep the old current-pose aiming for them. Turn on rejection only once the nav image with E1 is deployed. A simpler alternative: the bridge reads rt/c3po/objects directly through perception_link's existing domain-42 participant and String_ type. That gives the detector's own stamp_unix with no nav rebuild.
- _apply (major)_: LIVE_MAX_AGE_S=0.5 is too tight for this pipeline. Object age at emit can reach 0.1 s (10 Hz detector). Report age at the bridge can reach 0.25 s (4 Hz emit), and the bridge measures it from receipt (perception_link._report_at). The detector stamps at publish time, not capture (detector.py:1178 passes time.time() after inference), which hides another ~40–100 ms. That is ~0.45 s worst case with no jitter. E5 adds load to the same single core (cpuset 5). The result would be intermittent stale_detection refusals and lost E3 candidates. With pose_at correcting the motion, an age near the 2 s history is safe. **Fix:** Set LIVE_MAX_AGE_S to about 1.0 s, inside the pose history. Stamp the objects report at capture: take t right after source.read() and pass it to build_report. That is a one-line change in run() and also covers the 3.6 overlap.
- _apply (minor)_: Three details are unstated. (a) What go_to_object does when pose_at returns None. (b) The history uses time.time(). The Jetson has no RTC, and NTP steps the wall clock, which is why perception_link uses time.monotonic. A step makes captured_at land on the wrong sample. (c) Leaving surroundings_snapshot drops build()'s detector_online check (world_model.py:206-209). **Fix:** Store time.monotonic() in the deque and set captured_at = monotonic() - total_age. When pose_at is None, return stale_detection and never fall back silently to the current pose. Require report.get('detector_online') before using raw objects, the way safety.person_proximity does. Test each case.

#### 🟢 E3: 4.3: closed-loop go_to_object (re-aim about once a second, abort when the target is lost) `M`

- **Backlog:** 4.3 · **Depends on:** E2
- **Files:** `apps/bridge/src/bridge/skills/walk_to.py`, `apps/bridge/src/bridge/skills/go_to_object.py`, `apps/bridge/src/bridge/mcp_server.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_walk_to.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_go_to_object.py`
- **Change:** walk_to.run gains an optional retarget: Callable[[], tuple[float, float] | None] | None = None. Inside the existing loop, timed with time.monotonic(), it is called every RETARGET_PERIOD_S = 1.0. A tuple replaces target_x and target_y. None ends the loop with status 'failed' and phase 'target_lost' through the normal stop_motion path. With retarget=None, behaviour is byte-for-byte today's. go_to_object.py: a small pure Follow(target_xy, now, abort_when_lost) with update(candidates_xy, now) -> xy | None. It takes the candidate nearest the current target if within JUMP_M = 1.0 m, otherwise keeps the old target, and returns None once now - last_seen > LOST_AFTER_S = 3.0 and abort_when_lost is set. Memory targets get abort_when_lost=False: they were out of view to begin with, so they only re-aim if seen. mcp_server.go_to_object builds a sync closure from the same fresh-report filter plus pose_at as E2, matching the resolved label, and passes retarget= to run_walk_to. Update the shortcut comment in go_to_object.py and the tool docstring.
- **Tests:** test_walk_to: retarget is called about once a second; a moved target is followed (scripted _FakeSampler); None gives phase target_lost and stop_motion is awaited; all existing walk_to tests stay green with the default None. test_go_to_object: Follow picks the nearest candidate within the gate, ignores a jump over 1 m, is lost after 3 s with an injected clock, and in memory mode is never lost.
- **Done when:** On the robot, if the chair is moved 0.5 m sideways mid-walk, the robot re-aims (go_to_object.retarget in the log) and still stops about 0.7 m short. Covering the chair mid-walk stops it with phase target_lost within about 4 s. PARAR still stops it at once.
- **Risk:** walk_to is shared with the walk_to tool, so the hook must stay inert when None. A second object with the same label within 1 m can capture the target. 'target_lost' is a new phase string, so check how the console and back display task phases.
- _apply (major)_: Follow counts 'not detected' as 'lost', which mixes up 'absent' and 'not looked at', the rule D7 and object_memory are built on. Near the target, the pitched head camera (47.6° after 3.1, colour vertical FOV ~42°) cuts the object out of the frame. During the yaw-gated turn toward a target 20° off-axis, the object can leave the ~69° horizontal FOV. walk_to keeps commanding the 0.3 cap until the stop distance (KP_LIN=0.8 saturates). So a correct approach can abort with target_lost just before arriving. **Fix:** Count a miss only when the target's predicted range and bearing from the current pose fall inside the camera's view. Reuse object_memory's in-view and FORGET_MIN_RANGE_M logic rather than writing new rules. Within ~1.2 m of the last fix, keep walking to the last target instead of aborting. Test that an out-of-view target is not lost and an in-view missing one is.
- _apply (minor)_: JUMP_M is measured against the CURRENT target. A chain of false detections, each within 1 m of the last, can walk the target anywhere at 1 m per update, with no obstacle avoidance on this path. Also unstated: retarget raising goes through walk_to's generic except, which ends with phase 'exception' and stop_motion. That is safe, but it should be tested. **Fix:** Also bound the total drift from the first fix (for example ≤ 1.5 m) and check MAX_RANGE_M again from the current pose on every retarget. Log go_to_object.retarget with the old and new target. Add a test where a retarget raises and stop_motion is awaited.
- _apply (minor)_: The plan, and robot step 'never faster than 0.3 m/s', assume a 0.3 m/s cap. safety.govern clamps vx and vy separately (safety.py:209-211), and walk_to commands lateral velocity up to the cap (vy = KP_LIN*dy_body*gate, walk_to.py:174). Camera-only walks can therefore reach ~0.42 m/s diagonally, and retargeting adds more sideways motion. Nav2 is unaffected (max_vel_y 0). **Fix:** Clamp the linear vector norm in govern (scale vx and vy together to MAX_LINEAR_MPS) with one test. Or correct the claim in the plan and the acceptance criteria.

#### 🟢 E4: 4.3: go_to_object prefers a Nav2 goal when Nav2 is active and a person armed the gate `S`

- **Backlog:** 4.3 · **Depends on:** E2
- **Files:** `apps/bridge/src/bridge/mcp_server.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_go_to_object.py`
- **Change:** After resolve and the too_far check: if _nav_refusal() is None (relay reporting, nav2_ready, gate already open), take pose = nav_pose_from_status(get_nav_link().latest_status()[0]) (skills/landmarks.py:135). Compute the goal with g.world_target(pose['x'], pose['y'], pose['yaw'], max(0.0, range - stop_distance_m), bearing), then return await _start_navigation(gx, gy, None, pose['frame'], {'kind': 'object', 'label': ..., 'source': ...}). That returns a task_id at once, and Nav2 plans around obstacles. Otherwise, per decision 3, run the E3 camera-only walk and add 'via': 'walk_to' and 'nav2_refused': reason so the model knows nothing avoided obstacles. The goal uses Nav2's own pose, so the vendor-odometry and Nav2 frames are never mixed. go_to_object never calls link.enable or arm_navigation. Update the docstring for the two result shapes.
- **Tests:** Patch _nav_refusal to None with a fake nav status: _start_navigation is called with the stop-short goal in the nav frame. Each refusal reason takes the walk_to path and says why. get_link().is_enabled() is unchanged after both paths, so the gate is never opened. Already within the stop distance completes without a goal. This also covers part of 11.9 (tool-body tests).
- **Done when:** With nav2-depth active and the gate armed by the operator, go_to_object('silla') returns status 'started' and the robot walks around a box placed between it and the chair. With the gate closed, the same call walks camera-only and reports via 'walk_to' with reason gate_closed.
- **Risk:** One tool with two result shapes (a blocking task dict, or 'started' plus a task_id); the agent must poll navigation_status. arm_navigation is model-callable in chat mode (T1 high), so the model can arm the gate and then use this path; see decision 5. No re-aiming along the Nav2 path (one goal).
- _apply (major)_: Decision 5 is not new. It is BACKLOG 1.3 ('arm_navigation ... still T1 in typed chat: the model can open the Nav2 gate unconfirmed'). I verified that arm_navigation is danger 'high' and tier T1 (skill_meta.derive_tier returns T1 for locomotion), and that offeredInMode (back/src/agent/runtime.ts) offers it in chat. go_to_object is 'medium' and is offered on VOICE. After E4, any gate armed for something else, by an operator or by the model itself, turns overheard speech or a model choice into Nav2 driving for the whole TTL. **Fix:** Make BACKLOG 1.3 a hard dependency of E4: arm_navigation goes through Confirmar when an agent calls it. Until then, take the Nav2 branch only when the caller is not voice, or ship E4 behind a flag that is off by default. Add a unit test that the Nav2 branch never changes get_link().is_enabled() (already planned) and a test that stop_everything cancels the navigate_to task go_to_object created.
- _apply (minor)_: The Nav2 goal applies range and bearing to the relay heartbeat pose, which can be up to 0.5 s old, and not to the pose at capture. That brings back on the Nav2 path the 11.5 error E2 fixes. **Fix:** Compute the target relative to the robot NOW from the vendor-odometry change (pose_at(captured_at) to the current sampler pose). Apply that relative vector to the Nav2 pose, so no absolute frames are mixed. Or take the Nav2 branch only while the robot is still.

#### ⚪ E5: 4.2: the vision container publishes a downsampled depth PointCloud2 on domain 42 `M`

- **Backlog:** 4.2 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/perception/vision/c3po_vision/grounding.py`, `apps/perception/vision/c3po_vision/ros_idl.py`, `apps/perception/vision/c3po_vision/detector.py`, `apps/perception/tests/test_depth_cloud.py`
- **Change:** grounding.py, pure and stdlib-only as the module promises: depth_points(depth, intr, depth_scale, stride=8, min_m=MIN_VALID_DEPTH_M, max_m=3.0) returns a list of (x, y, z) in the colour optical frame. It accepts a list of rows or a numpy array, like sample_depths_m, reuses deproject_pixel_to_camera and drops zeros. pack_xyz32(points) returns bytes using struct '<f'. ros_idl.py: hand-write Time_, Header_, PointField_ and PointCloud2_ with exactly the shape and typenames of unitree_sdk2py's generated IDL (builtin_interfaces.msg.dds_.Time_, std_msgs.msg.dds_.Header_, sensor_msgs.msg.dds_.PointField_, sensor_msgs.msg.dds_.PointCloud2_ with data: types.sequence[types.uint8]). The vision image has no unitree_sdk2py, only cyclonedds 0.10.2. Update the 'one struct' docstring. detector.py: DEPTH_TOPIC='rt/c3po/depth_points', CLOUD_FRAME='camera_color_optical_frame', and on only when C3PO_VISION_CLOUD=1. ObjectsPublisher.start adds a second Topic and DataWriter on the same participant, held as attributes because of Entity.**del**, with the same reliable QoS. Add publish_cloud(data, n, stamp); StdoutPublisher.publish_cloud only logs the count. In run(), build the cloud INSIDE the tick's try (at line 1149) on every second tick (about 5 Hz), so a cloud failure fails the whole tick. No heartbeat then goes out and the detector reads offline, which the stale policy can act on (decision 4). Publish it right after the objects heartbeat at line 1178, stamped with the read time. Python 3.8 syntax only.
- **Tests:** New test_depth_cloud.py on hand-built list-of-rows frames: the centre pixel gives (0, 0, d); off-centre pixels match deproject_pixel_to_camera; zeros and depths beyond max_m are dropped; the stride sets the point count; pack_xyz32 round-trips through struct.unpack. A source-text contract asserts that the ros_idl typenames are the ROS ones and that CLOUD_FRAME equals nav2_depth.yaml's sensor_frame (once E7 exists). Perception suite and ruff py38 stay green.
- **Done when:** On the robot with C3PO_VISION_CLOUD=1, `ros2 topic hz /c3po/depth_points` inside the nav container shows about 5 Hz, roughly 4-5k points, frame camera_color_optical_frame. The detector's 'alive' mean_tick_ms rises by less than 10 ms.
- **Risk:** A wrong typename or field order delivers nothing and raises no error (ros_idl.py:9-16 warns about this). The vision container has a single core (cpuset 5) shared with the MJPEG stream. Needs a vision image rebuild, but only the last COPY layer (Dockerfile:243). Correct geometry on the robot depends on 3.1.
- _apply (major)_: The cloud writer copies the objects writer's QoS (Reliable with a 1 s max_blocking_time, KeepLast 10; detector.py:909-917). That writer sends ~57.6 KB samples from the same thread that publishes the person-detection heartbeat. The Nav2 costmap and collision monitor subscribe with SensorDataQoS (best effort), so reliability buys nothing, and a full writer history can block write() for up to 1 s and delay the heartbeat. Also, the plan puts publish_cloud 'right after the heartbeat at line 1178', which is outside the try. A DDS write error there escapes run() and kills the container without the logged give-up path. **Fix:** Make the cloud writer BEST_EFFORT, KeepLast(1). Build and publish the cloud inside the tick's try, before the heartbeat, so any cloud fault becomes a counted failed tick and never an uncaught exception. Note: sequence[uint8] serialises through PlainCdrV2SequenceOfPrimitiveMachine (struct write_multi), so bytes from pack_xyz32 are fine. 4800 points fit under the domain-42 MaxMessageSize of 65500 B.

#### ⚪ E6: 4.2: odometry in the nav container from the G1's own state estimator (no FAST-LIO) `S`

- **Backlog:** 4.2 · **Depends on:** E1
- **Files:** `apps/perception/nav/ws/src/c3po_perception/c3po_perception/g1_odom_tf.py`, `apps/perception/nav/ws/src/c3po_perception/config/odom_domain_bridge.yaml`, `apps/perception/tests/test_g1_odom_tf.py`, `apps/perception/tests/test_fake_stack_contract.py`
- **Change:** New odom_domain_bridge.yaml, copied from lidar_domain_bridge.yaml: from_domain 0, to_domain 42, exactly one named topic /state_estimator/odom_pelvis (nav_msgs/msg/Odometry, reliable, keep_last, depth 5). No wildcard. g1_odom_tf gains a parameter odom_source ('fastlio' default | 'g1'). In 'g1' mode it subscribes to /state_estimator/odom_pelvis, skips _publish_static (there is no camera_init, body or livox_frame), and skips the Rx(pi) re-expression because the vendor frame is already x forward, y left, z up (ROBOT-API.md:1830-1836). It publishes a dynamic odom→base_link (full pose: x, y, z and quaternion, which the depth cloud needs to compensate pelvis pitch) plus the existing flat odom→base_footprint and /odom. Stamps come from the node clock on receipt, because the control board's clock domain is unverified. Twist forces the existing 'differentiate' branch.
- **Tests:** New test_g1_odom_tf.py loads the node with the E1 stub helper (stubs for rclpy, geometry_msgs, nav_msgs and tf2_ros that record calls) and feeds a SimpleNamespace Odometry. In g1 mode it sends odom→base_link (full) and odom→base_footprint (yaw only, z=0), sends no static broadcast, and stamps with the node clock. A fastlio-mode regression checks the y/z sign flip. Generalise test_the_domain_bridge_carries_named_topics_only to every config/*domain_bridge.yaml.
- **Done when:** On the robot with the lidar faulted: `ros2 topic hz /odom` in the nav container reads about 50 Hz. `tf2_echo odom base_footprint` follows a 1 m walk to within 5 cm of the get_state pose change. odom→base_link z reads about the pelvis height (0.7-0.8 m) when standing.
- **Risk:** The odom_pelvis semantics are unverified: z reference, frame ids, rate and whether it needs the lidar. Fallback if it fails on the robot: the bridge republishes its rt/odommodestate as nav_msgs Odometry_ on domain 42 (unitree_sdk2py ships the IDL). Restamping adds a few ms of latency, and the differentiated twist is the documented degraded path.
- _apply (major)_: odom→base_link takes its z from the estimator. Leg-odometry z drifts, and every depth point's height in odom moves with it. A downward drift of 5–8 cm against min_obstacle_height 0.08 makes low obstacles disappear: this fails open, not closed. The planned acceptance checks z only while standing. **Fix:** In g1 mode, publish odom→base_link with z set to a measured, fixed pelvis_height_m parameter. Take x, y, yaw and roll/pitch from odom_pelvis, the same way fastlio mode pins the floor with the static lidar_height_m. Put that math in a pure function and test it.
- _apply (minor)_: Two problems. First, QoS is copied blind. The domain_bridge entry is copied as 'reliable' from lidar_domain_bridge.yaml, and g1_odom_tf subscribes RELIABLE (g1_odom_tf.py:125-128), while the vendor publisher's QoS for odom_pelvis is unknown. A best-effort publisher would then deliver nothing, the QoS trap this repo already documents twice. Second, the testing approach: a stub rclpy/tf2_ros harness that records calls goes against the convention the suite documents (conftest: import only pure nav modules; test_report_shape reads source rather than faking rclpy). **Fix:** In g1 mode, subscribe BEST_EFFORT. Set the bridge's QoS from the `ros2 topic info -v` reading in S0. Put the g1-frame math (base_link full pose with fixed z, footprint yaw-only, restamping) in a pure module and test it directly, like scan_ring and costmap_png.

#### ⚪ E7: 4.2: nav2.launch.py sources:=depth, with a depth costmap overlay and a collision-monitor cloud source `M`

- **Backlog:** 4.2 · **Depends on:** E5, E6
- **Files:** `apps/perception/nav/ws/src/c3po_perception/launch/depth.launch.py`, `apps/perception/nav/ws/src/c3po_perception/launch/nav2.launch.py`, `apps/perception/nav/ws/src/c3po_perception/config/nav2_depth.yaml`, `apps/perception/tests/test_nav2_launch_flags.py`, `apps/perception/tests/test_depth_costmap_contract.py`
- **Change:** New depth.launch.py, installed by the existing launch/*.launch.py glob in setup.py:27-28. It runs domain_bridge with odom_domain_bridge.yaml (name c3po_odom_bridge), g1_odom_tf(odom_source g1), world_model_publisher, and two static_transform_publishers from a pure camera_tf_args(env): base_link→camera_link from C3PO_CAMERA_X/Y/Z_M, PITCH_DEG and YAW_DEG (defaults equal to grounding.DEFAULT_CAMERA_EXTRINSIC), and camera_link→camera_color_optical_frame with yaw −π/2, roll −π/2. nav2.launch.py: IncludeLaunchDescription(depth.launch.py, condition sources==depth). _navigation reads sources through _choice(real|fake|depth), appends nav2_depth.yaml LAST when the value is depth, and raises RuntimeError for slam != off with depth (slam_toolbox needs /scan). nav2_depth.yaml is layered key by key, like nav2_mppi.yaml. local_costmap: plugins [voxel_layer, inflation_layer]; VoxelLayer with origin_z 0, z_resolution 0.08, z_voxels 16, footprint_clearing_enabled. It has two sources on /c3po/depth_points with sensor_frame camera_color_optical_frame and expected_update_rate 0.5. depth_mark: marking, clearing false, min_obstacle_height 0.08, max 1.30, obstacle_max_range 2.5. depth_clear: clearing, marking false, min −0.20 so floor points ray-trace free space, raytrace_max_range 3.0. Two sources are needed because of the existing note at nav2_params.yaml:146-152: height-filtering a cloud deletes its clearing rays. global_costmap gets the same two sources. collision_monitor: observation_sources ['depth'], type pointcloud, min_height 0.10, max_height 1.30. It reuses PolygonStop and PolygonSlow, with max_points raised for a dense cloud. FollowPath max_vel_x and max_speed_xy become 0.30, and velocity_smoother max_velocity [0.30, 0.0, 0.40]. These are advisory; the governor already enforces 0.3.
- **Tests:** test_nav2_launch_flags: with sources:=depth, nav2_depth.yaml is layered last on every Nav2 node; slam:=localization with depth raises; DEFAULTS gains sources: real and every existing test stays green. New test_depth_costmap_contract: load depth.launch.py with the stub-launch harness, compose the camera_tf_args TFs numerically and assert they equal grounding.camera_to_base for several optical points at pitch 47.6 and yaw 5; assert the defaults equal DEFAULT_CAMERA_EXTRINSIC. Regex checks on the YAML: each depth source topic is the ROS name of detector.DEPTH_TOPIC; sensor_frame equals detector.CLOUD_FRAME; mark min > 0 and clear min < 0; collision monitor type is pointcloud; max_vel_x <= 0.30. test_fake_stack_contract still reads nav2_params.yaml only and stays green.
- **Done when:** On the robot, `c3po up nav2-depth`, then the lifecycle manage_nodes command 0: all seven nodes go ACTIVE with the gate closed. /c3po/costmap (console live map) shows a 30 cm box at 1 m and no phantom floor arc out to 2.5 m. Removing the box clears it within a few seconds.
- **Risk:** Humble's parameter spellings for VoxelLayer and the pointcloud source are only proven at activation, and a wrong one takes Nav2 down (nav2_params.yaml:401-403). A wrong extrinsic either marks the floor (fails closed: nothing plans) or misses obstacles (fails open), so 3.1 must land first. There is a blind zone of about 0.45 m in front of the feet under a 47.6-degree head, and the waist sway is not modelled. Humble's collision monitor ignores a stale cloud and fails open; decision 4 covers that.
- _apply (blocker)_: Nothing tells the bridge whether the depth cloud is arriving. Humble's collision monitor skips a stale source (source_timeout 1.0, nav2_params.yaml:413), and the audit (audit/README.md:95) already flags this fail-open with /scan. Decision 4 only covers the detector heartbeat: safety.person_proximity (safety.py:141-166) looks at report age and detector_online, never at the cloud. If the cloud stops reaching the nav container while the heartbeat goes on, Nav2 drives on its only obstacle sensor with nothing in the costmap, at full governed speed. Causes include a typename or QoS mismatch, a dropped large sample, or a starved costmap/monitor reader. Only robot step 4 (a manual `ros2 topic hz`) would catch it. **Fix:** In depth mode, world_model_publisher subscribes to /c3po/depth_points. It reports detector_online=false, plus a note, once the cloud is more than 1 s old. That routes cloud loss through the existing stale_policy=stop path with no bridge change and no change to the report keys. Add a nav-side test of the rule (pure helper). Add a preflight line that runs `docker exec c3po-perception-nav ... ros2 topic hz /c3po/depth_points` with a timeout and blocks if no cloud arrives.
- _apply (major)_: The tests check that the depth sources exist. They do not check that the MERGED config has no lidar source left. A /scan or /cloud_registered_body source still in the local or global obstacle_layer observation_sources, or in collision_monitor observation_sources, never updates with the lidar faulted, and the controller aborts. nav2.launch.py:196-208 documents exactly this failure. 'Global_costmap gets the same two sources' is also ambiguous. A 2-D ObstacleLayer raytraces through low obstacles to the floor points behind them and clears them; that is the reason VoxelLayer is used at all. **Fix:** Add a merged-YAML test: load nav2_params.yaml, then nav2_depth.yaml with later-wins semantics. Assert that every observation source topic of local_costmap, global_costmap and collision_monitor is /c3po/depth_points, with no livox_frame or base_footprint scan source left. Use VoxelLayer in the global costmap too, and say so explicitly.
- _apply (minor)_: The costmap check (robot step 6) is done standing still. The waist joint is not in TF, and the gait pitches the pelvis. With a 47.6° head, 1–2° of unmodelled torso pitch moves floor points at 2.5 m by 4–9 cm, about min_obstacle_height. The new limits keep max_vel_theta at 0.40 while the governor caps yaw at 0.30 rad/s (safety.py:82), so DWB plans turns that are not executed. **Fix:** Repeat step 6 while the robot steps in place on the gantry, and consider obstacle_max_range ≈ 2.0 m. Set FollowPath max_vel_theta and the smoother's max and min theta to ±0.30.
- _apply (minor)_: Nothing in the mechanism enforces 'nobody walks nav2-depth before 3.1'. DEFAULT_CAMERA_EXTRINSIC has pitch 0 and z 0.55, unmeasured (grounding.py:188-194), and depth.launch.py would use those defaults without saying anything. **Fix:** depth.launch.py raises unless C3PO_CAMERA_PITCH_DEG is set, until 3.1 makes the measured value the default. The preflight blocks the same way. Test it.

**Critic on WP E** (verdict: needs_changes)

First, c3po_preflight is advisory: nothing makes anyone run it before arm_navigation, and the model can call arm_navigation in chat. Second, the stale policy does nothing when SAFETY_PERSON_RULE is off: speed_factor returns (1.0, None) before it ever reads STALE_POLICY (safety.py:171-172). A preflight that checks only stale_policy == 'stop' can pass while stale perception has no effect. **Fix:** Check it in the bridge as well. arm_navigation refuses, with a how_to_fix, when the running perception stage is nav2-depth (health.py already reads the stage label) and stale_policy != 'stop' or person_rule is false. The same check goes in the preflight. Test both refusal branches.

Also, BACKLOG 4.2 names rt/odommodestate, the source already proven with the lidar down, not odom_pelvis. Robot step 2 only checks (a) after both images are rebuilt and all of E5–E8 are written. **Fix:** Add a robot spike S0 before E5: run `ros2 topic echo --once` and `ros2 topic info -v` on odom_pelvis on domain 0 with the lidar faulted, and record frame, z, twist and publisher QoS. Then publish a 3-point PointCloud2_ from the vision image with cyclonedds-python and `ros2 topic echo` it in the nav container. If (a) fails, choose the odometry source before writing E6. Say in the plan why it departs from BACKLOG 4.2.

- _missing:_ Unit tests that stop_everything cancels a go_to_object Nav2 task and closes the gate, and that a raising retarget stops the walk. Today these exist only as robot steps.
- _missing:_ BACKLOG 7.5: a prompt update so the agent knows go_to_object's two result shapes (blocking task vs started+task_id), stale_detection, target_lost, and 'via walk_to: no obstacle avoidance'.

Bridge subset 69 passed. Perception suite 321 passed with the bridge venv. Not checked: Humble VoxelLayer clipping of rays to floor points below origin_z, and cyclonedds writer-history blocking under KeepLast. Both are robot or upstream questions.

**Robot/headset checks for WP E:**

- [ ] Rebuild the nav image (this also finishes the build that the battery cut short in backlog §2 and 4.5) and the vision image (last COPY layer only). Never modify or delete gemm's containers or files.
- [ ] With the lidar faulted, bring up nav2-depth. Inside the nav container: `ros2 topic hz /state_estimator/odom_pelvis` (expect ~51 Hz), `ros2 topic echo --once` it (record frame_id and z ≈ 0.7–0.8 m standing, and check the twist is filled), and `ros2 topic hz /odom`. If the topic is missing, switch E6 to the fallback (the bridge republishes rt/odommodestate).
- [ ] Confirm that 3.1 is applied (C3PO_CAMERA_PITCH_DEG=47.6 plus the measured height and offset, relative to the pelvis). `ros2 run tf2_ros tf2_echo base_footprint camera_color_optical_frame` should give a camera height of about the measured value.
- [ ] `ros2 topic hz /c3po/depth_points` ≈ 5 Hz, frame camera_color_optical_frame. The detector's 'alive' mean_tick_ms should not rise by more than about 10 ms.
- [ ] Gate CLOSED, robot standing or on the gantry: `ros2 service call /lifecycle_manager_navigation/manage_nodes nav2_msgs/srv/ManageLifecycleNodes "{command: 0}"`. All seven nodes must go ACTIVE; this validates the Humble VoxelLayer and pointcloud parameter spellings.
- [ ] Costmap check on the console live map: an open floor shows no phantom marks out to 2.5 m. A 30 cm box at 1 m is marked, and is cleared within a few seconds after removal. A 10 cm box at 0.8 m is marked. Tune min_obstacle_height and max_points if needed.
- [ ] Set SAFETY_STALE_POLICY=stop on the bridge and run c3po_preflight. It must pass, and must block after `docker stop c3po-perception-vision`.
- [ ] Person holding the remote, thumb on the e-stop. arm_navigation for 30 s, then navigate_to 2 m ahead with a box in the path: the robot plans around it, or the stop polygon halts it. Never faster than 0.3 m/s.
- [ ] During a Nav2 goal, stop the vision container: the bridge zeroes velocity within about 1 s (stale policy stop).
- [ ] go_to_object('silla') with the chair at 3 m and 20 degrees off-axis, gate closed: it re-aims about once a second and stops about 0.7 m short. Move the chair 0.5 m mid-walk and it follows. Cover the chair and it stops with target_lost within about 4 s.
- [ ] go_to_object right after a turn (still yawing): the target lands within about 0.2 m. A detection more than 0.5 s old answers stale_detection. describe_surroundings shows age_s per object.
- [ ] Gate armed by the operator: go_to_object returns 'started' via Nav2 and walks around a box placed between the robot and the chair.
- [ ] PARAR during each path (camera-only walk, the go_to_object Nav2 goal, navigate_to): motion stops, the latch holds, and nothing moves until Reanudar.

_Notes:_ The worktree is /Users/oliverjones/Developer/ORT - TIC/5th year - TIC/Proyecto Final/c3po-autonomy (feat/autonomy). I changed nothing. Read-only baseline: bridge subset (go_to_object, walk_to, state_pose, perception_link, world_model_report) 69 passed; perception suite 315 passed, 1 skipped.

How it fits together:

- The camera stays owned by the vision container. It publishes the depth cloud in its own optical frame.
- The extrinsic lives in TF (nav container), read from the same C3PO_CAMERA_* env and with the same defaults as grounding.py. A contract test proves the TF chain matches grounding.camera_to_base, so the costmap sees points exactly where the detector thinks they are.
- Odometry is the G1 estimator's odom_pelvis, carried over by one named domain_bridge topic (the existing pattern).
- Nav2 comes up UNCONFIGURED with autostart false, and the bridge gate stays closed by default. Every velocity still goes through perception_link's gate, send_velocity's estop check and safety.govern (0.3 m/s).
- go_to_object never arms the gate, and nothing touches T2.

A correction to the task's premise: the vision container does not use unitree_sdk2py. It writes with cyclonedds-python 0.10.2 and a hand-written IDL, so PointCloud2_ has to be hand-written to the same shape unitree_sdk2py generates. No new dependency is needed. The perception tests have no numpy or cyclonedds, so depth_points stays pure-Python like grounding, which also makes it testable with synthetic list-of-rows frames.

Dependencies on other work packages:

- 3.1 (camera extrinsic) is a hard prerequisite for E7 on the robot. 'base_link' must mean the pelvis, to match odom_pelvis.
- E1 partly overlaps 3.6 (the report stamp).
- E4 and E2 cover part of 11.9 (the go_to_object tool-body tests).
- 4.5 (nav image rebuild) is a robot prerequisite.
- 11.6 (headset teleop lease blocks voice 'andá hasta…') belongs to the VR work package but will bite go_to_object from the headset.

Suggested order: E1 → E2 → E3 → E4 (bridge track, usable with no Nav2 at all) in parallel with E5 → E6 → E7 → E8 (depth-nav track).

Not done on purpose:

- re-aiming along the Nav2 path (one goal only);
- a sensor-free depth rehearsal inside nav2-fake (the real nav2-depth with the gate closed and the lifecycle check covers it);
- combining lidar and depth costmaps for when the lidar returns;
- switching the bridge to rt/lf/odommodestate;
- walk_to's time.time() deadline (audit, low).

Not checked: the exact Humble 1.1.20 parameter names for VoxelLayer and the collision-monitor pointcloud source, and odom_pelvis's frame, z and clock. Both are robot-verified in the steps above.

Main risk: a wrong camera extrinsic makes the depth costmap fail open (missed obstacles), not just fail closed. Nobody should walk nav2-depth before 3.1 is measured and step 6 (the costmap check) passes.

#### 🟢 F6: robot_internet.py: only ports 80/443 to public addresses, no_proxy hint, self-test `S`

- **Backlog:** 11.7 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `scripts/robot_internet.py`, `scripts/tests/run.sh`
- **Change:** Standard library only. (1) Add `ALLOWED_PORTS = (80, 443)` and a pure `_refusal(ip: str, port: int) -> str | None`. It refuses a port outside ALLOWED_PORTS. It unwraps `ipv4_mapped`, then refuses `not addr.is_global` (loopback, RFC1918, 169.254/fe80 link-local, 100.64/10, fc00::/7, unspecified) and multicast. (2) Add `async _connect(host, port)`: strip `[]` from IPv6 CONNECT targets, resolve with `loop.getaddrinfo(..., type=SOCK_STREAM)`, refuse if ANY resolved address is refused, and connect to the checked IP. A second DNS lookup would allow rebinding. Use it at :59 and :70. (3) In _handle, catch the refusal before the existing `(OSError, ValueError)` → 502 and answer `HTTP/1.1 403 Forbidden` with the reason. (4) :100 also prints `no_proxy=localhost,127.0.0.1,::1`, with a one-line why. (5) Add a `--self-test` argument, handled like `--serve-only`: a table of _refusal asserts (8.8.8.8:443 allowed; 127.0.0.1, 10.1.2.3, 192.168.123.164, 169.254.1.1, 100.64.0.1, ::1, fe80::1, fc00::1, ::ffff:127.0.0.1, 0.0.0.0 and 8.8.8.8:22 refused) plus one end-to-end check: start the proxy on port 0, send `CONNECT 127.0.0.1:8001`, expect 403. No network needed. Print `ok`. Update the docstring's `shortcut:` line.
- **Tests:** In scripts/tests/run.sh, add an `== robot internet ==` section with `check "the Mac proxy refuses loopback, private and non-web destinations" "ok" "$(python3 "$repo/scripts/robot_internet.py" --self-test)"`. It runs in CI through @repo/robot-scripts on ubuntu python3, and locally on Mac python3 3.13.
- **Done when:** The self-test passes in the shell suite. On the robot through the proxy, Gemini, apt (:80) and pip/git (:443) still work. A CONNECT to 127.0.0.1 or 192.168.x through 127.0.0.1:18899 gets 403.
- **Risk:** Anything on the robot that needs a campus-private host through the proxy now gets 403. ia.ort.edu.ar is 'reachable from inside the ORT network only' (apps/back/.env.example:42), but only apps/back on the Mac calls it. Nothing in apps/bridge/src or apps/perception does. Egress on non-standard ports is refused; none is known.

#### 🟢 F7: scripts/robot_session.sh: one Mac command for a robot session `S`

- **Backlog:** 8.2 · **Depends on:** F6
- **Files:** `scripts/robot_session.sh`, `scripts/tests/run.sh`, `README.md`, `docs/OPERATIONS.md`
- **Change:** A new bash script that runs on macOS /bin/bash 3.2: no `wait -n`, and no empty-array expansion under `set -u`. `scripts/robot_session.sh [ssh-host]` takes the host from `$1`, else `$C3PO_SSH_HOST`, else `c3po`, as robot_tunnel.sh does. (1) Cable: `route -n get ${C3PO_ROBOT_IP:-192.168.123.164}` gives the interface, and `ipconfig getifaddr <iface>` must be 192.168.123.x. Otherwise print ✗ with the fix `sudo ipconfig set ${C3PO_ETH:-en11} MANUAL 192.168.123.99 255.255.255.0` (no router, per OPERATIONS §2). Never run sudo itself. The ✗ is non-fatal, because the ssh alias may use Wi-Fi. (2) Refuse to start if 3000, 3001 or 8899 is held, using robot_tunnel.sh's lsof line (robot_tunnel.sh already refuses 8001/8767). (3) Start three background jobs, logging to ${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/c3po-session/*.log: `scripts/robot_tunnel.sh "$host"`, `python3 scripts/robot_internet.py "$host"`, and `C3PO_ROBOT_HOST="${C3PO_ROBOT_HOST:-localhost}" bun run dev:robot` (D4). Add `trap 'trap - EXIT INT TERM; kill 0' EXIT INT TERM` with a `shortcut:` comment that it kills the whole process group. (4) After about 8 s, print a status table: cable; bridge through the tunnel (curl -m3 127.0.0.1:8001/telemetry/gate, robot_tunnel.sh's own probe); proxy (Mac :8899 listening, plus `ssh "$host" curl -s -m5 -x http://127.0.0.1:18899 -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/`); back (curl 127.0.0.1:3000/health); web (curl 127.0.0.1:3001). Then print the robot-side lines to paste: export http_proxy, https_proxy and no_proxy, and `c3po up`. Then loop `while kill -0 <pids>; do sleep 2; done` and name the child that exited, with its log. (5) `--print [host]` prints the cable verdict and the three commands, then exits; it exists for tests. Add one row to README.md's table (:66) and one line in OPERATIONS §2.
- **Tests:** In scripts/tests/run.sh, add an `== robot session ==` section. `--print` must contain `robot_tunnel.sh c3po`, `robot_internet.py c3po` and `C3PO_ROBOT_HOST=localhost bun run dev:robot`. The host argument must be honoured. With fake `route` and `ipconfig` scripts on a stub PATH, en11 at 192.168.123.99 must give '✓ cable', and en0 at 10.10.32.5 must give the `sudo ipconfig set` fix. The run.sh:80-89 and :518-528 globs syntax-check and shellcheck it automatically.
- **Done when:** On the Mac with the cable in: one command brings up the tunnel, proxy and console; the table shows ✓ for all five checks; Ctrl-C leaves no ssh/bun/python child (`pgrep -f 'ssh -N'` is empty). The shell suite is green.
- **Risk:** `kill 0` also kills a parent `bun run` if someone wraps the script; acceptable. Background ssh needs key auth, because a password prompt would hang in the background; the `c3po` alias is assumed key-based. Postgres for back is not checked, only /health.

#### 🟢 F8: Remove the wall-clock assumptions from test_mic_listener `S`

- **Backlog:** 9.2 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/bridge/tests/test_mic_listener.py`
- **Change:** Test-only, no product code. (1) drain() (:78-83): raise the default timeout from 3.0 to 10.0 s. It is a ceiling and returns as soon as the thread exits. Add `assert not listener.is_running(), f"listener still running after {timeout}s"`, so a slow or hung thread fails loudly instead of a partial poll() failing a later assert. (2) make() (:66-75): `kw.setdefault("playback_active", lambda: False)`, so no test depends on the process-global get_audio_msg_link(). (3) Replace fixed sleeps: :255 `time.sleep(0.6)` → `assert len(drain(lis)) <= 8`; :355 `sleep(0.3)` and :367 `sleep(0.2)` → `drain(lis)`; :286 and :303 ('let the generator register its bounded queue') → a small `_until(cond, timeout=5)` helper waiting for `lis._audio_subscribers`; :305 `sleep(0.05)` → `_until(lambda: not lis.is_running())`.
- **Tests:** The 40 tests in the file, run 50 times in a loop, plus the full bridge suite while every core runs a busy loop. 0 failures.
- **Done when:** test_a_raising_stop_callback_is_survived and its siblings pass under CPU load. No `time.sleep(<constant>)` is left as a synchronisation point in the file.
- **Risk:** drain() has 20 call sites, and all of them use finite sources, so every thread ends. Any test that relied on reading while the thread was still alive would now fail visibly; none was found.

_Notes:_ I did not edit the repo; `git status` in c3po-autonomy is clean. I used only the c3po-autonomy worktree (feat/autonomy). I ran these read-only: the full bridge pytest (951 passed, 13 skipped, 39 s), perception pytest (315 passed, 1 numpy skip), back bun test (179) and tsc, web bun test (622, 27.7 s) and svelte-check (0 errors), scripts/tests/run.sh (130 passed), ruff and mypy with no cache writes in the repo, `bun audit`, `bun audit fix --dry-run`, and `prettier --check`. For the API diff I downloaded the mcp 1.30.0 wheel into the session scratchpad (/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-oliverjones-Developer-ORT---TIC-5th-year---TIC-Proyecto-Final-c3po/94d93d4c-23d3-4a2b-8ae9-22bdde8bdba8/scratchpad/mcp130) and never imported it.

Biggest finding: CI as it stands would already fail on feat/autonomy, on three things that came in with recent commits: unformatted audit evidence .ts files, an annotation in perception build.py:209 that names `os` without importing it, and a wrong return annotation in arm_sdk.py:235. F1 fixes all three. CI also cannot run on this branch until it is pushed (backlog 8.3, your call).

Suggested order: F2 → F1 → F10 → F9. F3, F4, F5, F6 and F8 are independent; F7 after F6. Every subtask is S, and none needs the robot to build or test; the robot steps above verify on hardware. Per AGENTS.md, run `graphify update` on the project root after code changes.

Invariants kept: no change to the e-stop, PARAR latch, cmd_vel gate or T2 gate logic. F4 adds a protocol-level test that pins 'every MCP call goes through GovernedMCP.call_tool' across the mcp upgrade. F3 grants only three exact command lines that run code as unitree, never as root. Nothing touches the gemm team's files.

Left out on purpose: numpy in perception's dev group (6 decode tests stay skipped); fake timers for mjpeg-camera.test.ts (it is the 27 s of the web suite); Turbo $TURBO_ROOT$ inputs (CI has no shared turbo cache, so it doesn't matter there); a shared `bridge_systemctl` helper in _common.sh; hashing pyproject.toml together with uv.lock in bridge_sync's gate; an allowlist env for campus-private proxy destinations; and a `bun run` alias for the session script.

What I did not check: whether /usr/bin/systemctl exists on the JetPack rootfs (robot step); whether piper's x86_64 binary runs on ubuntu-latest; whether the never-run test_listen cases pass. I also could not reproduce the 9.2 flake, so its cause is inferred from the 3 s deadline and the fixed sleeps, not observed. Main risk for you: turning the voice tests on in CI may expose a real spoken-stop decode gap, and the plan treats that as a safety finding to escalate, not a test to delete.

It reaches typed operator turns, legacy voice and realtime voice, because all three use buildSystemPrompt (voice/realtime.ts:7). It says: (1) go_to_object(query) walks to something the camera sees now or saw in the last 10 min. It needs no map or Nav2, works up to 4 m, and walks a straight line; use it for 'andá hasta la silla'. (2) navigate_to_landmark(name) is for saved places and navigate_to(x,y) for Nav2 poses. Both need Nav2 and a gate an operator opened, and refuse otherwise. On a refusal, report it and its how_to_fix; whether walk_to may substitute follows decision D2. (3) walk_to and turn are short moves on odometry. (4) Only Nav2 avoids obstacles, and only while the snapshot's sources.lidar is 'ok'. When lidar is offline, say there is no obstacle avoidance before walking; go_to_object and walk_to only slow or stop for people. (5) A result containing 'refused: PARAR' means a person stopped the robot. Then stop the plan, call no other motion tool (they are refused too), tell the operator the robot is stopped and only they can resume with «Reanudar», and end the turn. Add a one-line comment pointing at safety.py:304 as the source of that string. VOICE_PREAMBLE stays as it is, since voice already gets SYSTEM_PREAMBLE.
- **Tests:** runtime.test.ts: a new test checks that buildSystemPrompt('operator') and ('voice') both contain 'go_to_object', 'navigate_to_landmark', 'obstacle avoidance', 'refused: PARAR' and 'Reanudar'. All existing buildSystemPrompt and offeredInMode tests stay green.
- **Done when:** `bun test src/agent` is green. In a G1/G2 run against the stub, t1-acercate-silla calls go_to_object and t1-ir-cocina calls navigate_to_landmark.
- **Risk:** The prompt gets a little longer for tic-chat. The PARAR sentence repeats a Python string that a TypeScript test cannot pin, so the comment has to carry it. The rest of the Walking text follows D2.

#### 🟢 G4: look / find_object / verify fall back to YOLOE-only, with an honest note, when Gemini is unavailable `S`

- **Backlog:** 7.7 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/bridge/src/bridge/mcp_server.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_vlm_tools.py`, `docs/autonomy/test-day/vlm.md`
- **Change:** In mcp_server.py, add one helper `_detector_only(exc) -> dict`. It returns {degraded: 'vlm_unavailable', detail: str(exc), objects, sources (from surroundings_snapshot()), note}. The note says the vision model is unavailable, these are the on-robot detector's fixed classes with range and bearing, and nothing unlisted should be described. look (:3312) returns {**_detector_only(exc), scene: None, frame_source}. find_object (:3358) matches the detector objects with bridge.object_memory.label_matches(o['label'], query), which already maps 'silla' to 'chair' and is what go_to_object uses. Each instance gets label, bearing_deg, range_m, range_from 'detector', and map_xy_m when there is a pose. Extract the existing map_xy arithmetic into a 3-line helper both paths use. The note adds that count 0 means 'not among the detector's classes in view'. verify (:3429), per decision D3: keep error 'vlm_unavailable' and attach _detector_only's objects and note, so the result is never read as verified. Detector labels are a fixed vocabulary, so they stay plain, not untrusted_text, the same as describe_surroundings. _grab_frame keeps running first, unchanged. Update vlm.md §8 with the new expected results. Optional, per D4: give look `skill_meta(**{**_VLM_META, 'works_real': True})` with a 2026-11-03 comment, so the prompt stops tagging it 'unavailable'. find_object and verify stay False until vlm.md step 9.
- **Tests:** test_vlm_tools.py, reusing the existing `server` fixture (cup at 1.5 m, 0.5°). Replace test_a_missing_key_is_a_clear_error_not_a_crash: with the key deleted, look returns degraded == 'vlm_unavailable', 'GEMINI_API_KEY' in detail, the cup in objects, and scene None. Offline (fake _post raises urllib.error.URLError): find_object('taza') gives count 1, range_m 1.5 and map_xy_m about [0.99, 3.5]; find_object('billetera') gives count 0 with the note; verify keeps error 'vlm_unavailable' and includes objects. The injection-guard tests stay as they are. The full bridge suite (`uv run pytest`) stays green.
- **Done when:** With no GEMINI_API_KEY or no uplink, look and find_object answer from YOLOE with `degraded` set, and verify says it cannot verify and shows what the detector sees. Nothing raises.
- **Risk:** The model may read a fixed-vocabulary 'count 0' as 'absent'; the note covers that. Detector labels are English, so the agent has to translate. The D4 flip changes how the model sees look on the real robot. It is backed by BACKLOG §0 ('Verified on the robot: ... Gemini look').

#### 🟢 G5: Tests for the go_to_object tool body `S`

- **Backlog:** 11.9 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/bridge/tests/test_go_to_object.py`
- **Change:** Tests only. Add a fixture in the style of test_vlm_tools.py's `server`: monkeypatch server.SIM_MODE='real', server.surroundings_snapshot and server._memory_pose. Also patch bridge.object_memory.get_object_memory, bridge.sdk.state.get_sampler and bridge.skills.walk_to.run. The tool imports those inside the function, so patches made at call time take effect. Cases: (1) pose read: a live chair 2 m away at bearing 90°, vendor pose (1, 1, yaw 0), so run is called once with target ≈ (1, 3), stop_distance_m=0.7, timeout_s=60, autonomous=True; the result has target.source 'live' and env 'real'. (2) Frame choice: no live object; the memory chair is at (12, 10) with perception pose (10, 10, 0°), frame 'odom'; vendor pose is (0, 0, π/2); the target is ≈ (0, 2). This pins 'relative from perception, steer on vendor odometry'. (3) A memory entry with frame 'odom_old' against _memory_pose frame 'odom' gives not_found, and run is not called. #### 🟢 G6: Test that runAgentChat registers and releases its turn, and fix the failed-turn leak `S`

- **Backlog:** 11.9, 11.8 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/back/src/agent/runtime.test.ts`, `apps/back/src/agent/runtime.ts`
- **Change:** Test: run runAgentChat in a fresh `bun -e` child, the client.test.ts:47-58 pattern. bun test shares one module registry, and realtime.test.ts imports runtime.ts statically, so in-process the provider and API_KEY (read at module load, runtime.ts:91) belong to whichever file loads first. The child gets AGENT_API_KEY=test, a dead BRIDGE_URL, and dummy DATABASE_URL, WEB_URL, BETTER_AUTH_URL and BETTER_AUTH_SECRET if unset (env.ts:16). It replaces globalThis.fetch: /chat/completions answers with canned OpenAI SSE, and anything else throws TypeError (bridge unreachable, so the catalogue is empty and worldSummary degrades). Three exit-code checks: (a) after an OK turn activeTurnCount() is 1, then 0 once fullStream is consumed; (b) with a hanging fetch that honours init.signal, abortAllTurns() returns 1, the stream ends with an 'abort' part, and the count is 0; (c) a gateway 400 must bring the count back to 0. Today (c) fails: the probe ended at 1. Fix for 11.8 in runtime.ts:406: `const result = streamText({...}); void result.steps.then(done, done); return result;`. steps settles on end, error and abort (all three probed). done is an idempotent Set.delete, so onEnd and onAbort can stay or go.
- **Tests:** The three child-process checks above. The full `bun test` in apps/back stays green, including skills.test.ts, which already uses beginTurn and activeTurnCount.
- **Done when:** All three checks exit 0. After a forced gateway failure, activeTurnCount() returns to 0, and abortAllTurns() no longer counts dead turns (routes/skills.ts:234 reports that number).
- **Risk:** The child-process test adds about 1 s. It runs the real openai-compatible provider over a stubbed fetch, so an SDK bump that changes the stream format breaks it. That is useful: it is the 'majors move together' trap in runtime.ts:28. Ownership of the 11.8 fix follows decision D6.

#### 🟢 G7: One structured log line per agent turn (zero-dependency telemetry) `S`

- **Backlog:** 7.2 · **Depends on:** G6
- **Files:** `apps/back/src/agent/runtime.ts`, `apps/back/src/agent/runtime.test.ts`
- **Change:** Per decision D5, option A. Add a pure function `turnSummary(event, {mode, chatId, startedAt})`. It returns {mode, chatId, model: MODEL, steps: event.steps.length, finishReason, inputTokens and outputTokens (event.totalUsage, null if the gateway sends none), ms, tools: the tool names called}. It records no prompt text, camera text or speech. streamText onEnd becomes `(e) => { done(); console.log('[agent] turn', JSON.stringify(turnSummary(e, ...))) }`. The G6 rejection branch logs `{mode, chatId, ms, error}` for failed turns, which onEnd never sees. Comment: `shortcut: stdout only; registerTelemetry(new OpenTelemetry()) from @ai-sdk/otel when a collector exists`. Per-tool latency stays in tool_call_log. No new dependency, no service.
- **Tests:** runtime.test.ts: turnSummary on a hand-built end event (2 steps, 1 tool call, usage {inputTokens: 10, outputTokens: 5}) returns the expected fields. It contains none of the message text, and gives null tokens when totalUsage is undefined.
- **Done when:** Each typed, voice or eval turn prints exactly one '[agent] turn {...}' line on back's stdout, and failed turns print theirs with `error`.
- **Risk:** Low. The TIC gateway may omit usage. The logs live only where back runs. Langfuse or OTel would be a later, separate change (D5).

**Robot/headset checks for WP G:**

- [ ] G3, typed console chat with the lidar still faulted: 'andá hasta la silla'. Expect: the agent says there is no obstacle avoidance, calls go_to_object('silla') (not navigate_to or walk_to), and stops about 0.7 m short. Check tool_call_log.
- [ ] G3: 'andá a la cocina' with a saved landmark. Expect navigate_to_landmark, refused nav_relay_offline or nav2_inactive, the agent reports how_to_fix, and no walk_to call (per D2).
- [ ] G3: press PARAR, then type 'andá hasta la silla'. Expect one motion call refused with 'refused: PARAR', no further motion calls in tool_call_log, and a reply that names «Reanudar». Repeat by voice through the headset's «Hablar con el robot» (🥽).
- [ ] G4: stop robot_internet.py (or blank GEMINI_API_KEY and restart the bridge). Then look() returns degraded 'vlm_unavailable' with the YOLOE objects. find_object('silla') returns a detector instance with range_m. verify(...) returns error 'vlm_unavailable' plus objects. Asked '¿qué ves?', the agent says it can only see the detector's classes and invents no scene. Update the vlm.md §8 results.
- [ ] G4/D4: after flipping look's works_real, the console's agent prompt (or GET /skills) shows look as 'real-only'.
- [ ] G2: with robot_tunnel.sh up, run `bun run eval:t0` with BRIDGE_URL=:8001. It must exit 1 and say the env is 'real', before any LLM call.
- [ ] G2 (optional, operator present, T0 only): run the T0 suite against the robot with --expect-env real, and record the success.human verdicts for t0-que-ves, t0-persona and t0-hay-taza next to runs.jsonl.

_Notes:_ Worked in the c3po-autonomy worktree (feat/autonomy) only. Read-only, except that the eval dry-run wrote its output to the scratchpad, not docs/. Probes ran as inline `bun -e` with fetch stubbed, so no bridge, DB or robot was touched.

Ran:

- `bun test scripts/eval/suite.test.ts src/agent`: 25 pass.
- `bun scripts/eval-agent.ts --dry-run --out <scratchpad>`: every trial passed.
- `uv run pytest tests/test_go_to_object.py tests/test_vlm_tools.py`: 20 pass.
- Stub-mode `mcp.list_tools()` to get every tool's tier, classification and works flags.

Turn-registry probe results:

- OK turn: count 1, then 0.
- PARAR abort: abortAllTurns returns 1, stream parts 'start,abort', count 0, steps rejected.
- Gateway 400: error part, steps rejected with NoOutputGeneratedError, count stays 1. This is the 11.8 leak.

Order: G5, G6 and G4 have no dependencies and can run in parallel. G3 waits on D2. G1 waits on D2, and G2 follows G1. G7 follows G6. They all fit in one or two short PRs. After the code changes, run `graphify update` in the worktree root.

Things found along the way, all outside this package:

1. The eval scores tool names, not results, so an errored or refused call still counts as 'called' (audit §5). A follow-up could treat results with `error`/`refused` as not called.
2. cancel_navigation and disarm_navigation are tier T1 by classification, so tool_refusal (safety.py:300) refuses them while PARAR stands. That is harmless because stop_everything already cancels and disarms, but it is surprising.
3. look_around, walk_velocity, find_object and verify are tagged 'unavailable' in the prompt (works_real=False and works_sim=False), while the preamble tells the model to use the vision tools.

Skipped or not checked: I did not read the full PLAN Phase 0.5 metrics (interventions, gate trips, p95), which G1 and G2 leave untouched. I did not run the full bridge or back suites. I did not test the exact SSE format beyond the probe.

Risks for the user: the README's ':8001 stub' recipe can point an LLM eval at the real robot whenever robot_tunnel.sh is up, so G2's --expect-env guard matters. Eval runs also write into the configured DB's tool_call_log.

#### 🟢 H2: OPERATIONS.md: how a session connects, deploys and gets internet `S`

- **Backlog:** 10 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `docs/OPERATIONS.md`
- **Change:** (a) In §2 replace :105-122. The cable path that worked on 2026-11-03: the Mac set by hand to static 192.168.123.99/24 with no router (the internal LAN has no DHCP, ROBOT-HARDWARE §1:59), the Jetson at 192.168.123.164, and the existing Wi-Fi-first service-order advice kept. Shrink the 192.168.77.x report to one [?] line pointing at ROBOT-HARDWARE. Add a caveat: on .99 the Mac sits on the robot's control LAN (DDS domain 0, the control board's TCP 9991, BACKLOG 1.8), so never turn on Internet Sharing for that adapter. Narrow :91 "Address the robot by name, never by IP" to Wi-Fi/DHCP, since 192.168.123.x is vendor-static. (b) A new paragraph on lending the robot the Mac's internet. `python3 scripts/robot_internet.py unitree@192.168.123.164` exposes robot 127.0.0.1:18899. Export http_proxy/https_proxy in the robot shell for apt and `c3po perception build` (bringup/build.py proxy_build_args adds --network host plus the build-args, 9a6063d). For Gemini, the bridge reads apps/bridge/.env into os.environ (env_file.py:109) and urllib honours it, so set `https_proxy` ONLY, plus `no_proxy=127.0.0.1,localhost`: an `http_proxy` there would send the camera relay's http://127.0.0.1:8081 fetches (sdk/camera_relay.py:64,318) to the Mac. Add port-table rows for 8899 (Mac loopback) and 18899 (robot loopback, alive only while the ssh -R is). Note BACKLOG 11.7 under §9. (c) In the §2 port table and the dev row: open the console as http://localhost:3001, never 127.0.0.1:3001. WEB_URL=http://localhost:3001 is back's only CORS origin (apps/back/src/index.ts:61) and Better Auth trustedOrigins (lib/auth.ts:47); Vite listens on 127.0.0.1 (apps/web/package.json `dev`), but the browser has to use the name. (d) Deploy, at §1 :49, §5 :279-282 and :522-531. The branch is pushed from the Mac, because feat/autonomy is not on GitHub (8.3) and the robot has no internet on the cable: `git push <robot>:c3po feat/autonomy:refs/heads/incoming`, then on the robot `git merge --ff-only incoming && ./scripts/robot/c3po install && c3po restart`. ff-only refuses a robot checkout that has diverged: stop and look, never force, and never touch ~/gemm*. Delete the stale "The robot's checkout was on `152e3a6` as of 2026-08-28" (:524). (e) sudo: install, restart and `systemctl start` need the unitree sudo password, so run them in the operator's own terminal (`ssh -t`); Claude Code cannot type it. This is what run_c3po:63 and take_camera:105 already print. BACKLOG 1.11 is the pending narrow sudoers fix.
- **Tests:** node_modules/.bin/prettier --check docs/OPERATIONS.md. `grep -c` for each of 192.168.123.99, refs/heads/incoming, robot_internet.py, no_proxy and localhost:3001 in docs/OPERATIONS.md is ≥1.
- **Done when:** From OPERATIONS alone, a new operator can cable the Mac, push the branch, lend the robot internet without breaking the camera relay, and open the console on the right origin. No remaining instruction tells the robot to `git pull` feat/autonomy from origin. prettier passes.
- **Risk:** The push recipe and the 'sudo password dialog' come from the session, not the repo, so they need the user's confirmation (decision 1 and 6). A wrong proxy line can silently break the camera relay: hence https_proxy only.

#### 🟢 H3: OPERATIONS.md: the 802 trap, the PARAR latch, the Mid-360S, secrets (plus 3 one-liners elsewhere) `S`

- **Backlog:** 10 · **Depends on:** H2
- **Files:** `docs/OPERATIONS.md`, `docs/ROBOT-HARDWARE.md`, `docs/SESSION-CHECKLIST.md`
- **Change:** (a) A new §5 subsection beside "When the robot ignores everything": "Accepts velocity, does not move: FSM 802". R2+A on the remote puts the robot in 802 (Run, ROBOT-API §4.1). SetVelocity then answers rpc 0 and nothing moves (unitree_sdk2_python #170, BACKLOG §12). Bring-up must end in 501: console «1 · Amortiguar → 2 · Preparar → 3 · Habilitar marcha» = damp → prepare → start_walking_waist. Read get_state fsm_id after every SetFsmId, because rpc 0 proves nothing (ROBOT-API §12, SetFsmId(99999)). Replace SESSION-CHECKLIST.md:302 "802 is probably not 'run'" with this observation and a pointer. (b) A §4 paragraph "PARAR latch and «Reanudar»". Every stop_everything (console PARAR or Shift+Space, both headset grips held 300 ms, the spoken stop, a Claude Code tool call) writes ~/.c3po/run/stop_everything (estop.signal_stop). While it stands: GovernedMCP refuses every non-T0 tool except damp (safety.tool_refusal), _locomotion zeroes every velocity, arm_sdk refuses to engage, and back aborts every agent turn (agent/turns.ts abortAllTurns). The latch survives bridge and teleop restarts. Only «Reanudar» clears it: admin only, from the console chip or the headset menu, through back POST /safety/ack to the bridge's /safety/ack with SAFETY_CONFIRM_SECRET; a newer stop gets 409 `newer_stop`. Re-engaging the headset does not clear it (993da12). With no secret configured it stays latched. (c) Mid-360S: change :34 `Livox Mid-360` to `Livox Mid-360S`, and add a §9 bullet: scan module faulted on 2026-11-03 (HMS 0x02100003, ROBOT-HARDWARE §4.6, BACKLOG 4.1); no FAST-LIO, /scan or Nav2 obstacle data; go_to_object is camera-only with no avoidance. Change the ROBOT-HARDWARE.md:58 table row to Mid-360S, leaving the §4 heading and its anchor. (d) §6 Secrets :764-779: add GEMINI_API_KEY (Robot ✅, vision only, see BACKLOG §12 data terms) and SAFETY_CONFIRM_SECRET (Back ✅ + Robot ✅, the same value). Reword :773 so the rule reads "no agent or database credentials on the robot". (e) The :641-645 teleop gate table: the arm row becomes "shoulder signs match the URDF (test-day manipulation.md §1, 2026-11-03); wrists unverified (BACKLOG 5.5)"; the hand row becomes "right BrainCo does not answer on any ttyUSB (BACKLOG 5.1)".
- **Tests:** node_modules/.bin/prettier --check docs/OPERATIONS.md docs/ROBOT-HARDWARE.md docs/SESSION-CHECKLIST.md. The grep for `802`, `Reanudar` and `Mid-360S` in OPERATIONS hits; the grep for "probably not \"run\"" in SESSION-CHECKLIST returns nothing.
- **Done when:** OPERATIONS names the 802 trap with its fix, describes the latch exactly as estop.py, safety.py and safety.ts implement it (no bypass described), lists the two secrets the robot really holds, and says the lidar is a faulted Mid-360S. prettier passes.
- **Risk:** Same file as H2, so do them in sequence. The latch text must not teach a way around it (decision 2).

#### ⚪ I1: Capsule self/table clearance check in the pure-Python arm planner `M`

- **Backlog:** 5.3 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/bridge/src/bridge/kinematics.py`, `apps/bridge/src/bridge/skills/manipulation.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_kinematics.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_manipulation.py`
- **Change:** In kinematics.py: add _seg_seg_dist(p0,p1,q0,q1) and clearance(side, q, other_q=None, table_z=None) -> (metres, what). Capsules are built from forward(side,q).joint_origins: upper arm from the shoulder_yaw origin to the elbow origin, forearm from the elbow to the wrist_roll origin, hand from the wrist_yaw origin to the TCP. Add a fixed torso capsule in torso_link and the other arm at its measured or rest q. Radii are constants with a `shortcut:` comment: unmeasured, because meshes are not vendored (assets/README.md). A table is the half-space z < table_z. In manipulation._solve, after the limit check at :139-143, raise PlanError('<name>: <what> within N cm') when clearance < COLLISION_MARGIN_M (0.03). plan_pick and plan_place take an optional table_z, passed only when the detection's depth_source == 'plane'; never invent a table. Update the kinematics.py docstring line 'What it does not model: self-collision'.
- **Tests:** Segment-distance unit tests: parallel, crossing, endpoints, degenerate. Rest pose and HANDOVER_POINT plans are clear. A hand-through-torso q is rejected. With table_z just under the grasp, the 0° tilt is rejected and 30/60° is chosen. pick and place refuse with the collision reason and FakeArm receives no command. The full bridge suite stays green (uv run pytest).
- **Done when:** pick/place return a refusal naming the collision before any arm motion. Plan success on docs/autonomy/audit/evidence/kinematics/ik_plan.py is reported before and after, in the commit message. No new dependency.
- **Risk:** The radii are guesses, so they can cause false refusals or false passes. This is a conservative add-on to the WORKSPACE box, not a guarantee. A per-waypoint check adds CPU to an already synchronous plan (audit: IK is on the event loop).

#### ⚪ I2: Palm-roll rows in the DLS IK (full 6-D hand task) `M`

- **Backlog:** 5.3 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/bridge/src/bridge/kinematics.py`, `apps/bridge/src/bridge/skills/manipulation.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_kinematics.py`
- **Change:** HandPose gains palm, the second axis of the wrist-yaw link (rot·(0,1,0) or (0,0,1); check which one is the Revo2 palm normal in the URDF hand mount). solve(..., palm=None) and _solve_from add 3 rows DIR_WEIGHT_M·a[k] with error cross(hp.palm, palm), the same form as the existing pointing rows at kinematics.py:309-316. manipulation._approach also returns a palm direction per tilt (palm toward the object's side for 0°, down-ish for 60°). This removes the audit's −102°..+113° roll spread.
- **Tests:** With palm=None, results are identical to today's (regression on fixed seeds). With palm given, roll converges within 10° on reachable targets. Record the IK p95 time on the Mac.
- **Done when:** Palm-roll spread across seeds is < 20° on the evidence grid, and plan success drops by no more than 5 points.
- **Risk:** A rank-6 task on 7 DoF shrinks the reachable set and lengthens the solve. Palm-axis convention unverified until 5.5/wrist signs.

#### ⚪ I3: pin + pink + coal behind an optional `ik` extra (only if I1/I2 fall short) `M`

- **Backlog:** 5.3 · **Depends on:** I1, I2
- **Files:** `apps/bridge/pyproject.toml`, `apps/bridge/uv.lock`, `apps/bridge/src/bridge/kinematics_pink.py`, `apps/bridge/src/bridge/skills/manipulation.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/test_kinematics_pink.py`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`
- **Change:** Add `[project.optional-dependencies] ik = ["pin==4.1.0", "pin-pink==4.4.0", "qpsolvers[daqp]>=4.13"]`. coal 3.0.3, eigenpy, cmeel-boost and scipy come transitively. PyPI has cp312 manylinux_2_28 aarch64 wheels, and JP5's glibc 2.31 is >= 2.28. kinematics_pink.solve has the same signature and IKResult as kinematics.solve: FrameTask on <side>_wrist_yaw_link plus the TCP offset, a PostureTask using xr_teleoperate's weights (50·pos + rot + 0.02‖q‖² + 0.1‖q−q_last‖²), and ConfigurationLimit; coal distance checks against the Menagerie collision geometry. Selected with C3PO_IK=pink only when the import succeeds; pure Python stays the default and the fallback. plan_pick runs via asyncio.to_thread on both paths.
- **Tests:** Differential test: pink against pure Python on 200 random reachable targets, TCP within 1 cm where both reach. A CI job with --extra ik fails on skip. Existing suites stay green without the extra.
- **Done when:** `uv sync --extra ik` works on the Mac and the Orin. Import time and RSS are measured on the Orin and recorded. The planner chooses pink only when asked.
- **Risk:** About 100 MB of compiled wheels (cmeel-boost 36 MB, scipy 36 MB, assimp 14 MB, pin 9 MB) in the process that owns the stop, which kinematics.py:4-6 argues against. numpy>=2 is required by cmeel-boost (the bridge has numpy 2.4.4, which is fine). Wheel install on the JP5 uv Python is unverified.

#### ⚪ I4: MuJoCo Menagerie G1 arm twin as a pytest harness (no DDS, no SIM_MODE change) `M`

- **Backlog:** 5.4 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/bridge/pyproject.toml`, `apps/bridge/uv.lock`, `apps/bridge/scripts/fetch_menagerie_g1.sh`, `apps/bridge/.gitignore`, `apps/bridge/tests/sim/conftest.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/sim/plant.py`, `apps/bridge/tests/sim/test_arm_twin.py`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`
- **Change:** Add dependency group `sim = ["mujoco==3.17.0"]` (cp312 wheels for macOS arm64 and linux aarch64/x86_64), never a runtime dependency. fetch_menagerie_g1.sh downloads google-deepmind/mujoco_menagerie unitree_g1 at the pinned commit 98a70c569c4c (g1.xml plus 51 asset files, about 36 MB) into apps/bridge/.cache/menagerie (gitignored). plant.py loads g1.xml, welds the pelvis to the world (no balance policy needed) and uses its existing position actuators; the joint names match the URDF's. A fake publisher replaces ArmSdkDriver._get_publisher. Its Write(cmd) reads the REAL _build_command output: motor_cmd[15..28].q, waist slots 12-14, weight slot 29. It applies the documented blend q = q_fw·(1−w) + q_cmd·w against a fixed firmware posture, sets ctrl, and steps 0.02 s. A fake sampler serves get_arm_state() (arm_q and waist_q from qpos, lowstate_age_s=0, fsm_id=500, mode_machine). SIM_MODE='real' and TELEOP_ARM_ENABLED=1 are monkeypatched in-process only, as tests/test_manipulation.py:89 already does. Leave mujoco_local alone. A locomotion twin is out of scope (see decisions).
- **Tests:** (1) Parity: the MuJoCo wrist_yaw_link pose against kinematics.forward for 100 random q, < 2 mm; if this fails, stop and document the model difference. (2) The audit §1.2 regression: request_release mid-slew moves the TCP < 2 cm beyond what the blend explains during the 2 s ramp. (3) execute(plan_pick(...)) through the real driver: max TCP deviation from the planned lines < 1.5 cm, no arm-torso contact (mjData.ncon filtered by body), no joint past its limits. Mark them @pytest.mark.sim. The CI job fetches the assets and fails on skip; the lesson from 9.1 is that tests which always skip are worthless.
- **Done when:** `uv run --group sim pytest -m sim` is green locally and in CI. Test (2) fails on a scratch revert of baf1894's request_release, which proves it would have caught the bug.
- **Risk:** Menagerie g1.xml may not match rev_1_0 URDF offsets (unverified, test 1 decides). Actuator gains differ from the real kp 60/kd 1.5, so the dynamics are qualitative. The firmware posture under blend is unknown, so it is assumed arms-down. 36 MB of assets fetched in CI.

#### ⚪ I5: Native toolApproval for T2 in the typed-chat and headset agent turn `M`

_Superseded by one-press Confirmar (76995ad, 2026-11-05): one press runs the refused call with its stored arguments, and the model reads the outcome instead of retrying. This design is kept for the record. Whether to keep a native approval UI as an optional extra is open._

- **Backlog:** 7.1 · **Depends on:** —
- **Files:** `apps/back/src/agent/runtime.ts`, `apps/back/src/agent/turns.ts`, `apps/back/src/routes/agent.ts`, `apps/back/src/routes/skills.ts`, `apps/back/src/bridge/client.ts`, `apps/back/src/agent/runtime.test.ts`
- **Change:** (a) Move the fetch inside POST /skills/:name/confirm (skills.ts:290-345) into confirmT2(name) in bridge/client.ts and call it from both places. (b) runAgentChat gets opts.approvals?: {userRole}. Only routes/agent.ts passes it (console chat and the VR headset). The legacy voice loop (routes/voice.ts:68) and realtime keep today's refuse path, because they have no approval UI. (c) When approvals are on, streamText gets experimental_toolApprovalSecret = 32 random bytes per process (fail-closed across restarts) and a generic toolApproval({toolCall}) that does nothing for non-T2 tools. For T2: if the toolCallId is unknown, record {at, chatId, stopEpoch} in a pendingApprovals Map in turns.ts and return {type:'user-approval', reason}. If it is pending, this is the replayed approval: approve only when age <= 60 s, same chatId, userRole === 'admin' and stopEpoch unchanged, then delete it (single use). Everything else is denied. The SDK's signature binds only approvalId, toolCallId, toolName and sha(input), with no expiry, user or single use (dist/index.js buildPayload), so back must add these. (d) abortAllTurns clears pendingApprovals and bumps stopEpoch. (e) The T2 execute calls confirmT2(name) right before callTool, so the bridge still checks the PARAR latch, the person rule and consumes a grant; the bridge stays the real limit. (f) Change the SYSTEM_PREAMBLE line at runtime.ts:144 to: the operator approves in the chat; on 'denied' do not retry.
- **Tests:** bun test with MockLanguageModelV4 from 'ai/test': (1) a T2 call emits tool-approval-request with a signature, and neither callTool nor confirm runs; (2) an admin-approved replay calls confirm then callTool exactly once; (3) replaying the same history is denied with no call; (4) a non-admin is denied; (5) after abortAllTurns it is denied; (6) a tampered input or signature raises InvalidToolApprovalSignatureError and nothing runs; (7) T0/T1 tools are not-applicable; (8) the legacy voice path (no approvals) keeps today's behaviour. skills.test.ts confirm-route tests stay green, and so do the bridge test_safety.py tests (no bridge change).
- **Done when:** In the console a T2 call shows an approval card. Approve runs the tool in the same chat with no 'Confirmado' user message and no model re-plan. The bridge log shows safety.t2_confirmed then the tool. Approving after PARAR, after 60 s, or twice is refused.
- **Risk:** The approval is still a second POST to /agent, so this is one conversation turn but not one HTTP request. Until 1.4 lands, any session could POST /agent, but (c) checks admin itself. A per-tool-name grant (11.1) is still a race window of milliseconds; do 11.1 too. convertToModelMessages must carry approval parts from the persisted chat (chat persistence path untested for approvals).

_Superseded with I5 by one-press Confirmar (76995ad, 2026-11-05)._

- **Backlog:** 7.1 · **Depends on:** I5
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/routes/(protected)/chat/+page.svelte`, `apps/web/src/lib/components/chat/tool-card.svelte`, `apps/web/src/lib/chat/tool-part.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/robot/voice-panel.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/robot/vr-voice.svelte.ts`, `apps/web/tests/chat-logic.test.ts`
- **Change:** Both Chat instances (chat/+page.svelte:122 and vr-voice.svelte.ts:55) get sendAutomaticallyWhen: lastAssistantMessageIsCompleteWithApprovalResponses, exported by ai@7.0.140. In tool-card.svelte, an 'approval-requested' part renders Aprobar/Rechazar (admin-only, like Confirmar today) that calls chat.addToolApprovalResponse({id: part.approval.id, approved}) and shows part.input, the exact args. voice-panel.pendingConfirmation returns {tool, approvalId}: approval-requested parts first, with needsConfirmation refusals as the fallback for the legacy engines. vr-voice #confirm(approvalId) calls addToolApprovalResponse instead of POST /skills/:name/confirm plus the 'Confirmado' message. voiceMenu keeps 'Volver' at index 0, so index 0 never grants.
