- **Risk:** Two UIs (a refusal button and an approval button) live side by side until the legacy voice engines get approvals or are retired. Quest grip/menu ergonomics need a headset check.

#### ⚪ I14: Prove edge-of-image grounding at the real pitch; switch to per-sample depth-to-point only if it fails `S`

- **Backlog:** 3.8 · **Depends on:** backlog:3.1
- **Files:** `apps/perception/tests/test_grounding.py`, `apps/perception/vision/c3po_vision/grounding.py`
- **Change:** First, a test. Add REAL_CAM = CameraExtrinsic(..., pitch_deg=47.6). Render a synthetic aligned depth image of a 0.3 m cube on the floor at positions across the FOV (centre, edges, corners, 1–3 m) through the pinhole at REAL_CAM. Call ground_box on the cube's box and compare with the true centre. If range error < 5 % and bearing error < 2° everywhere, close 3.8 as 'it was the pitch (3.1)'. ground_box already deprojects (grounding.py:652). Only if it fails: change ground_box to deproject every inner-region sample (u, v, z) through camera_to_base and take the median of x and y, instead of deprojecting the box centre at the median depth. That needs a sample_depths_m variant that also returns pixel coordinates (grounding.py:281). Use the mask centroid when a mask exists. Stdlib only, Python 3.8.
- **Tests:** The new edge and corner tests at REAL_CAM. All LEVEL_CAM tests stay green. Plane-fallback tests at the real pitch belong to 3.2.
- **Done when:** The test proves < 5 % / < 2° across the FOV at 47.6°, with or without the change.
- **Risk:** Synthetic depth has no stereo noise or edge bleed, so the robot check stays mandatory. The per-sample change costs more Python work per detection, bounded by max_samples=1024.

#### ⚪ I17: Kimodo-G1 clip to a vetted, arm-only gesture asset (offline) `M`

- **Backlog:** 5.6 · **Depends on:** I4
- **Files:** `apps/bridge/scripts/kimodo_to_gesture.py`, `apps/bridge/src/bridge/assets/gestures/`, `apps/bridge/tests/sim/test_gesture_assets.py`
- **Change:** On a GPU laptop, not the robot: generate with nv-tlabs/kimodo (Kimodo-G1-SEED-v1, trained on the open Bones-SEED data) and save its MuJoCo qpos CSV. The script maps columns to Menagerie g1.xml joint order (verify the layout), keeps only the 14 arm joints, and resamples to 50 Hz. It clamps to kinematics.limits(side) minus LIMIT_MARGIN_RAD, and time-stretches so no joint exceeds arm_sdk.MAX_JOINT_RATE_RAD_S (0.6 rad/s). It blends in and out from the rest pose, then writes {name, prompt, model, rate_hz, q:[[14]]}. Nothing generates at runtime, and no model-supplied trajectory is ever accepted.
- **Tests:** Every asset loads and is within limits and rate. It replays in the I4 twin with no self-contact and within URDF limits.
- **Done when:** Two or three gestures ('reverencia', 'saludo con las dos manos') pass the twin checks and are committed with their prompt and source.
- **Risk:** Kimodo licence, the checkpoint's terms and the CSV layout are unverified. Dropping legs and waist makes some motions look wrong. Outstretched arms make the G1 shuffle (BACKLOG §12).

That reuses arm_pose.slew, the per-tick abort (person_in_reach, base_moving), the stop path and release-freezes. There is no new motion code. An unknown name is refused.

_Notes:_ Corrections to the BACKLOG and to the task text, from reading the installed code:

- **7.1.** In the installed ai@7.0.140, tool-level `needsApproval` is marked @deprecated but streamText still honours it (dist/index.js resolveToolApproval), and the package has no WorkflowAgent. Call-level `toolApproval` is the supported path.
  - The HMAC signature covers only approvalId, toolCallId, toolName and sha(input). It has no expiry, no user and no single use, so a replayed history re-executes an approved call. I5 closes that in back with a TTL, single use, an admin check and a stop epoch.
  - Approval is still a second POST to /agent. It removes the 'Confirmado' message and the model's retry, not the round trip.
  - The legacy voice loop (routes/voice.ts:68) consumes `textStream` and has no approval UI, so it must keep the refusal path.
- **7.6.** Partly done in the bridge: object_memory plus recall_object, and 'llaves' already maps to keys. It is blocked on the robot only by the pose source (FAST-LIO). Postgres + pgvector is NOT in apps/back: the database is Neon over drizzle bun-sql, with no vector column.
- **3.8.** The code already deprojects with depth (grounding.py:652). The edge errors in the audit are a symptom of the 0° pitch default (3.1). I14 proves or disproves this with a test before any code change.
- **5.4.** SIM_MODE=mujoco_local is a dead profile: it publishes Isaac's rt/run_command, and arm_sdk refuses anything other than 'real'. So the harness runs in-process in pytest, not over DDS.
- **4.6.** Blocked on 4.1, because slam_operate needs the vendor lidar_driver. It also conflicts with the one-commander check in _common.sh.

Versions checked on PyPI and npm today:

- pin 4.1.0, pin-pink 4.4.0, coal 3.0.3: all have cp312 manylinux_2_28 aarch64 wheels.
- mujoco 3.17.0.
- @ricky0123/vad-web 0.0.31, onnxruntime-web 1.31.1.
- The bridge has numpy 2.4.4 transitively.
- Menagerie unitree_g1 at commit 98a70c569c4c (2026-11-02): g1.xml uses position actuators and URDF joint names; the assets total about 36 MB.

- [Kimodo paper (arXiv 2603.15546)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.15546)
- [Kimodo-G1-RP-v1 on Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/yang0520/Kimodo-G1-RP-v1)
- [pebblous blog on Kimodo](https://blog.pebblous.ai/blog/kimodo-text-to-motion/en/)
- [OpenAI realtime transcription guide (es-419)](https://developers.openai.com/es-419/api/docs/guides/realtime-transcription)
- [Azure realtime transcription session reference](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/reference/rest/realtime-create-realtime-transcription-session)
- [OpenAI Go SDK realtime reference](https://developers.openai.com/api/reference/go/resources/realtime/index.html)

What I skipped or did not check:

- I ran no test suites.
- Not verified: Menagerie-vs-URDF kinematic parity, the Kimodo licence and CSV column layout, the OpenAI transcription model names, Docker and glibc behaviour for Noble images on JP5, and pin wheel install on the Orin's uv Python.
- I wrote no files. graphify queries were run before the reads.
- Risk the user must know: I5/I6 and I10 touch safety-adjacent paths (the T2 grant mint, and the memory pose frame). Neither weakens PARAR or the cmd_vel gate, and T2 still needs a human approval that the bridge enforces. But I5 moves the moment a grant is minted into back's tool execute, so 11.1 (a grant bound to its arguments) should land with it or before it.

## 5. Cross-package findings

### Items no WP covers yet (robot-independent)

- 1.5 (M): teleop WebSocket Origin allowlist plus a token from apps/back. No WP covers it. teleop/server.py:919 calls serve(handle_client, TELEOP_HOST, TELEOP_PORT) with no origins and no token, and scripts/robot/run_teleop:59 defaults to 0.0.0.0. Put it in WP C after C6/C7, because those two edit the same server.py.
- 1.8, the code part only: pin DDS_INTERFACE=eth0. apps/bridge/.env.example:19 leaves it blank, and nothing in scripts/robot sets it (only lidar_service.py:47 reads it). The firmware read and update stay robot-only.
- 11.1, partly: A2 binds the grant to the arguments only. The backlog item asks for the arguments AND the chat id. The chat id never reaches the bridge today: back's callTool(name, args) in bridge/client.ts:182 carries no context.
- 6.1, partly: B8 wires the headset reply to speech. Piper to PlayStream already exists in skills/tts.py. But apps/bridge/scripts/install_piper.sh:21 still downloads rhasspy/piper 2023.11.14-2, not OHF-Voice/piper1-gpl v1.8.0. Nothing covers that switch.
- 6.5 (M): camera over WebRTC instead of MJPEG. The code needs no robot; checking it needs the Jetson.
- 6.6: the AEC configuration half of the stand audio kit. The mic purchase is hardware, not code.
- 6.7 (M, research): benchmark STT on OpenSLR 61, offline.
- 7.2, partly: G7 writes one structured log line per turn. The item asks for experimental_telemetry to Langfuse/OTel. This is a reasonable zero-dependency deviation, but the backlog should say so.
- 8.3: feat/autonomy has no upstream; `git branch -r` shows only origin/integrate/october. Pushing needs the user's go-ahead. Until then, F9/F10 (CI on every push) have no effect.
- 8.4: decide what happens to the uncommitted WIP in the main checkout (feat/real-g1-dispatch-and-hardening). This is a decision for the user, not code.
- 8.5: delete or rotate operador@c3po.local. B6's new apps/back/scripts/create-user.ts could do it, but B6 does not list it.
- §12 safety architecture: a geofence and a heartbeat watchdog. `grep geofence apps/bridge/src` finds nothing.
- §12 Gemini data terms: use a paid key or blur faces before upload. There is no blur anywhere in the bridge. Separately, the ER 2 pointing JSON format and the thinking_level setting are not checked by G4.
- §12 Livox: pin a livox_ros_driver2 commit and SDK2 >= 1.3.3, and raise FAST-LIO `blind`. These are Dockerfile and config edits. Only I15 touches apps/perception/nav/Dockerfile, and I15 is a different change.
- Audit §1.4, not in the backlog or any WP: camera_relay.upstream_status() runs synchronously inside the async camera routes (mcp_server.py ~4208 and ~4236). That is a blocking urlopen of up to 1.5 s (camera_relay.py:64) on the bridge event loop, and it can stall stop_everything over HTTP. Fix: asyncio.to_thread.
- Audit §1.4: the stop burst, velocity and FSM poller share one sport client and one timeout (sdk/g1_rpc.py:45-53, 83). Also, skills/gesture.py and skills/_g1_request.py catch only Exception, so a CancelledError can pin a task as running.
- Audit §1.4: the walk_to deadline uses time.time() (skills/walk_to.py:129,134), and the Jetson has no RTC. This matters more once 8.1 brings NTP. E3 rewrites walk_to.py; fold the time.monotonic() change into E3.
- Audit §1.4: the bridge cmd_vel gate ignores the freshness of its obstacle source (it ignores lidar_online in perception_link). E7/E8 add sources:=depth, but no subtask closes the gate when the depth cloud goes stale. Without it, Nav2 fails open.
- Stop paths that do not abort agent turns: only name === 'stop_everything' in routes/skills.ts:233 and the voice loop (loop.ts:193,224) call abortAllTurns. soft_stop through /invoke and the /tasks estop mode (routes/tasks.ts) do not.
- The person rule ignores capture age: safety.person_proximity (safety.py:141-166) checks the REPORT age against PERCEPTION_STALE_S=1.0. Meanwhile world_model_publisher re-emits objects for up to DETECTOR_OFFLINE_AFTER_S=1.5 with stamp_unix=now (world_model_publisher.py:72,250). D5/E1 add the age field, but no WP makes the person rule or the T2 reach check use it.
- Audit §2: admin-bootstrap.ts:56-66 promotes any account whose email matches ADMIN_EMAILS, without checking emailVerified. Also, auth.ts:35-45 enables GitHub/Google sign-in, and B6's emailAndPassword.disableSignUp does not stop OAuth from creating users implicitly.
- Audit §2: /agent accepts messages: t.Array(t.Any()) (routes/agent.ts:142), so a client can forge assistant and tool history. Also, javascript: links in the model's markdown are not stripped (response.svelte). Neither is in any WP. The bridge gate still holds against both, so they are lower priority.
- Audit §5: Realtime error events are fatal, an odd-length PCM chunk throws (voice/pcm.ts:13), and the abort signal never reaches a tool's execute, so a 300 s walk_to can outlast the 240 s turn budget (runtime.ts:310-314). None of these is in a WP.
- Already done or partly done, for the record. 6.8 is done: vr-voice.svelte.ts:55-61 posts to /agent with a Chat id, and agent.ts:74-96 ensureChat persists it (default channel 'text'), so it appears in the console Chat. 9.3 is partly done: ci.yml already runs back, web, bridge and perception, plus svelte-check through check-types, but only on push to main and on PRs.

### Inconsistencies to resolve while implementing

- D5 vs E1, the same change twice: both make world_model_publisher.py carry capture age instead of stamp_unix=round(now,3) (:250), for 3.6 and for 11.5's nav side. On separate branches they will pick different field names. Make D5 the owner. E1 becomes tests against D5's field.
- B3 vs G6, the same fix twice: runtime.ts:388 calls beginTurn before the Promise.all at :389-402, so a throw in buildSystemPrompt or buildTools never calls done, and done is wired only to onEnd/onAbort (:421-422). Merge G6 into B3. G7 then depends on B3.
- C2 vs A6 battery thresholds: C2 warns under 25 % and gives the strong alert under 15 %, but A6 refuses arm motion and long locomotion under 20 %, as BACKLOG 1.10 says. Between 15 and 20 % the console would show a mild warning while the bridge refuses tools. C2's strong tier should start at A6's threshold, ideally read from /safety/status. A6 also has to decide what battery_pct None means (state.py:456: no BmsState_ yet), and must never refuse stop, damp or T0.
- A2 vs A3/A5, argument canonicalization: the agent's execute passes the model's raw args (runtime.ts:314). /invoke passes Ajv default-filled args (skills.ts:175-186). The bridge receives raw `arguments` (mcp_server.py:121). Unless A2 hashes after applying each tool's schema defaults, the grant from A3's Confirmar (args from the tool part) and from A5's /invoke will not match the call they are meant for. A2 also drops 11.1's chat-id binding.
- A4 vs the existing confirm route: /safety/confirm answers not_a_t2_tool for anything that is not T2 (mcp_server.py:3806), and safety.tool_refusal returns early for tier != T2 (safety.py:308). A4 must change both, or A5 has nothing to grant. The agent-facing refusal text (safety.py:328-332) and the prompt line 'These are T2' (runtime.ts:144) must cover high-danger T1 too (G3).
- A5 vs B7, an invariant risk: after B7 every /agent caller is an admin. A5 ('an admin's /invoke is the confirmation') lists runtime.ts. If any part of the grant lands in runtime.ts tool execute, every agent turn grants itself. Keep the grant strictly inside the /invoke handler in skills.ts, and keep runtime.ts to prompt text.
- E4 assumes 'a person armed the gate', which is only true after A4. Today the typed-chat agent can call arm_navigation unconfirmed. Add A4 to E4's depends_on. Also, no console control arms the gate (gate.svelte.ts is read-only, and no web code calls arm_navigation), so after A4 the only route is chat, then refusal, then Confirmar (A3). E8 and c3po_preflight:103 must say so.
- I10 vs E6 vs E2, three uses of vendor odometry: _memory_pose (mcp_server.py:2776-2793) deliberately refuses vendor odometry as 'a different frame'. I10 adds it as a fallback. E6 makes the nav container's pose come from rt/odommodestate (state.py:11,60), so after E6 the report has a pose and I10 is redundant: it would label the same physical frame differently. E2's pose-at-capture history in state.py should be the one helper. Drop I10, or make it a frame-labelled fallback that E6 retires.
- A1 vs C6, per-process stop state: the in-process copy of the stop is per process (MCP server vs teleop server). It must be a time.time() stamp compared against the ack file's mtime, not a boolean and not monotonic, because estop.py compares mtimes. Otherwise a stop latched in the teleop process (C6) can never be cleared by /safety/ack, which runs in the MCP process. C6's socket stop also does not abort agent turns in apps/back, so the existing back path must keep firing alongside it.
- B5 vs the headset: quest_setup.sh:34-36 records that adb reverse connects over IPv4, and that a ::1-only bind gave a blank headset (the Vite fix). B5 must bind hostname 127.0.0.1, not 'localhost'.
- B6 vs auth.ts: emailAndPassword.disableSignUp does not stop GitHub/Google sign-in (auth.ts:35-45) from creating users implicitly. admin-bootstrap.ts:56-66 promotes ADMIN_EMAILS addresses without emailVerified. B7's admin gate is only as strong as these two.
- C7 vs 11.6: renew_teleop_lease() runs on every tick, whatever the headset is doing (server.py:553). C7 renews only while the headset commands motion, so head yaw alone (yaw_to_vyaw, :566) must count as commanding, or the agent and the headset will write cmd at the same time. C8's 'decision D4 = b' collides with WP D's subtask D4 (the wedged D435). Rename the decision.
- A7 vs H5/H8: both edit test-day/safety.md and BACKLOG.md. Fold A7's docs into H.
- F9/F10 vs 8.3: 'CI on every push' does nothing while feat/autonomy has no upstream, and pushing (8.3) is in no WP.
- E7/E8 vs the gate: the depth costmap and collision monitor arrive, but the bridge gate still ignores whether its obstacle source is alive (audit §1.4: Nav2 collision monitor fails open on stale input). Add a fail-closed check on depth-cloud freshness to E8, or the invariant 'never open the cmd_vel gate by default' holds only on paper.
- B closes 6.8: confirmed done (vr-voice.svelte.ts:55-61 Chat id plus POST /agent, agent.ts ensureChat). But the chat is stored as channel 'text', so continuing a headset conversation from the console runs in operator mode, which offers high-danger T1. That is acceptable, but document it in H7.

## 6. Left over from the wave-2 review (minor, 2026-11-04)

- [x] Behavioural tests for the capture-age wiring. Two mutations survived: the publisher using arrival time, and the detector stamping at publish time.
- [x] With no `SAFETY_CONFIRM_SECRET`, `/invoke` of a gated tool returns 503. Map `confirmation_not_configured` to a Spanish hint in `skill-outcome.ts`, and consider making preflight treat a missing secret as a blocker.
- [x] A pre-grant stays pending for 30 s when the dispatch is refused right after it (battery, PARAR). Drop that tool's grant in `tool_refusal` when it refuses a gated call.
- [x] The teleop token is replayable for 60 s over plain `ws://` on the LAN. Bind it to one use, or move to `wss`.
- [x] Test hygiene: `teleop.test.ts` leaves `TELEOP_SECRET` set; the headset socket-stop and token tests only grep the source.
- [x] C8 (wave 3): head yaw alone ends an agent walk that the headset's own voice started.

Adversarial review fixed before commit: the Meta menu / headset-off return no longer steers until the head is centred; Nav2 paths (go_to_object, navigate_to, navigate_to_landmark) refuse and cancel while the headset drives; a non-finite velocity axis is 0, not the cap; walk_to's deadline is monotonic.

Pending for the robot session:

If it is ≥ 0.09 m, widen the box and move the far-side test target (0.26, -0.30, -0.05). Watch the tuck pose on that side: the forearm passes ~0.088 m from the midline.
- verify now runs in back on the school vision model (Gemini only for find_object's pointing): after a simulated pick, check that `verify('¿la mano derecha sostiene la taza?')` answers no, with a reason.
- E2/E3 leftovers: chair moved mid-walk, covered chair → target_lost.

## 9. Robot-independent leftovers closed (2026-11-07)

From §5 "Items no WP covers yet":

- ✅ `camera_relay.upstream_status()` runs in a thread in the three camera routes (5e4c03c). A slow vision container can no longer delay `stop_everything` over HTTP.
- ✅ The cmd_vel path fails closed on a stale obstacle source (d6acd8b). `safety.govern` zeroes every autonomous setpoint unless a world summary ≤ 1 s old says `lidar_online`, whatever SAFETY_PERSON_RULE and SAFETY_STALE_POLICY say. `arm_navigation` reports `obstacles_fresh`. **nav2-depth (E5–E8) must add the depth cloud to `safety.obstacles_fresh()`**, or Nav2 on depth gets zero velocity.
- ✅ `soft_stop` on /skills and the /tasks estop abort every agent turn, like `stop_everything` (cf5d136).
- ✅ The turn's abort and its remaining budget reach the bridge call (4d77169): a 300 s `walk_to` is cancelled on PARAR, when the client goes away, or at the 240 s budget, and `walk_to` runs its stop sequence on cancel. Safety skills are never cancelled this way.

- ✅ A cancelled `gesture` still releases the arm, and a cancelled `gesture` or `_g1_request` task ends as `cancelled` instead of staying `running`. 4d77169 made this reachable: every ended turn now cancels its tool call. The release waits for the gesture's own RPC to finish, so the two never race on the arm client.
- ✅ A PARAR latched on the bridge directly (headset socket, stop phrase) ends back's agent turns. While a turn runs, `agent/stop-watch.ts` polls `/safety/status` every 500 ms and aborts every turn when `stopped_at` moves. It compares stamps rather than clocks, because `stopped_at` is the robot's wall clock.
- ✅ The back tests pass under `bun test --randomize` (40 seeds). `catalogue.test.ts` loads its own copy of the catalogue, since bun applied another file's mock of `skills/index` to the re-exported functions. `camera.test.ts` no longer relies on the order of its tests.

Robot check for the next session: arm Nav2 with the lidar faulted. `arm_navigation` must report `obstacles_fresh: false`, and the bridge log must show `safety.speed_factor reason=obstacle_source_stale` with zero velocity sent.

## 10. Second pass, 2026-11-07

Done off the robot, each with tests that fail without the fix:

- ✅ PARAR stops our speech: `stop_everything` sends `g1_rpc.stop_play` beside the burst, and `stop_play` ends a `play_pcm` still sending chunks (466f503).
- ✅ Chat markdown keeps only http/https/mailto/relative links and http/https/relative images (`safe-urls.ts`); raw HTML renders as escaped text (3672bcd, c981aa5).
- ✅ POST /agent takes only the last message from the client, as user text. A persisted chat's history comes from the database; without one, only user text. Forged assistant or tool turns are dropped (984bd5d).
- ✅ Realtime voice: an odd PCM byte carries over, and four error codes no longer end the session (d87836a).
- ✅ B8: a completed headset reply is spoken behind `HEADSET_SPEAK_REPLY=1` (default off), under its own turn so PARAR cuts it, with a 10 min backstop (104fd9a, adc1cf4). The B8 risk "stop_everything has no stop_play" is closed by 466f503.
- ✅ Geofence on Nav2 (0553c27), see OPERATIONS §4.
- ✅ Faces blurred before any cloud upload (03153df), see OPERATIONS §6.
- ✅ I4, partly: the MuJoCo arm twin runs in CI (82d9f0c). **It found that all six pick plans put an arm link 6–51 mm into the torso**: `TORSO_BOX` uses y = ±0.08 m, the torso is about ±0.10 m, and links have thickness. Its strict xfail is I1's acceptance test. Until I1, do not run `pick` (test-day/manipulation.md §5).
- ✅ F9: a `voice` CI job runs the spoken-stop decode tests on real vosk + piper (8af37d0). Running them showed the test spoke the banned words, and the stop detector never flushed at the end of the audio (fixed, dd18ce6). Tests now synthesise deterministically. Measured on random syntheses: «emergencia» 13/15, «pará, pará» 14/15, «¡detener!» 14/15, flat «detener» 7/15 (a non-strict xfail), and no false stops.
- ✅ F10, partly: concurrency cancels superseded PR runs; triggers unchanged.
- ✅ The back tests pass under `--randomize` (`client.test.ts` and `catalogue.test.ts` load private module instances).

Still open off the robot: I1 (arm-link clearance), the heartbeat watchdog, the external-stop watcher (11.3), the Livox SDK version, the shared sport client, the Piper switch to piper1-gpl, and the heavier I4 driver-in-the-loop tests.
